## 2026-11-08 — Cycle 15 (improve): Icon coverage for batches 34-36 + recovery of the main-chat sprite-name fix — COMMITTED

- **Recovery (step 2):** the tree arrived with the main chat's sprite-name fix uncommitted (8 modified files + `tests/spritename.test.js`, `tests/iconcoverage.test.js` untracked; `hydra/script.js` 534,065 bytes and `tests/browser-smoke.js` at HEAD, both already restored from cycle 10's zeroing — no restore needed). The first full run on that tree failed the smoke stage with 4 failures, all one root cause: the fix's `normalizeScene` uniquify block renumbered duplicate names on load (coin, coin 2, coin 3), which breaks the engine's supported same-name pattern — every goblin is 'goblin', every coin is 'coin', and objectCount, the tag picks, and the for-each loops address the whole group by that one name. The failures: the conditions seed counted 1 instead of 2 (only one object was still named 'coin') and objectVisible never fired (the first 'coin' was the hidden one); the Joints section's "asks for a name on an unnamed sprite" check failed because a fresh sprite is now born named; and the pick-by-tag live preview timed out waiting for `coinHp === 5` (the loop damaged the single remaining 'coin' three times, to -5, instead of each coin once). **The fix:** the uniquify block is deleted from `normalizeScene` — load never renames a non-blank name; a blank name still repairs to its kind default in `normalizeObject`, and two repaired blanks sharing a name are just another same-name group, while the editor's `addGameObject` still gives a fresh object a distinct name. `tests/spritename.test.js` now pins the corrected behavior (blank names repair without renumbering siblings; an authored same-name group survives load unchanged — 24 checks), and the Joints smoke section now checks that a fresh sprite is born named instead of asking for a name. The rest of the sprite-name fix is untouched (default names, blank-never-matches, no "(none)" on required targets, blank-target warning, toolbar grouping).
- **Item (highest priority; the single unchecked ROADMAP item, count corrected 13 → 16):** icon coverage for batches 34-36. Cycle 9's probe never tested read-tagged-count, tagged-count, or for-each-tagged, so the real scope is 16 blocks, all falling back to text labels that overflow the 56px cell and clip at the grid edge in column 0: add-tag, remove-tag, clear-tags, read-tagged-count, has-tag, tagged-count, pick-nearest-tagged, pick-farthest-tagged, pick-random-tagged, pick-tagged-object-into-variable, for-each-tagged, show-minimap, hide-minimap, set-minimap-size, set-minimap-zoom, minimap-visible. ## 2026-11-08 — Main chat (Ky'lin's live bug): nameless sprite + "(none)" + toolbar gaps — DONE in the main chat, COMMITTED in cycle 15 (recovery)

- **Report (Ky'lin, Nov 8, 13:23-13:41 EST, three screenshots):** a sprite he created did not appear in the Apply Force block's Object dropdown, yet the block still moved it; he found it himself ("the issue was because the sprite didnt have a name but that shouldn't be possible"); then "why is none here why is that a option?" about the "(none)" row in the same dropdown; then the Preview showed an empty scene after he tested the force; then the Logic toolbar buttons "have a big gap between others and are literally just placed anywhere in the ui like the dock left button and the search bar and the variable count thing."
- **Root cause (verified in code):** `createObject` in `hydra/hydra-universal.js` gave every kind a default name (Entity, Button, Panel, Tilemap, Camera3D, Light, Box3D...) except a plain sprite, which got `''`. So every sprite was born nameless. The picker hides nameless objects (`getAllObjectNames` only adds names `if (o.name)`), so the sprite never listed. But the runtime looked objects up by name equality, and a blank target matched every object whose name was blank (`getObjectsByName` filtered `o.name === ''`), so "(none)" secretly WAS the sprite and the block moved it. A fresh Apply Force block also defaulted to blank here (`legacyDefaults` sets `targetObject: names[0]`, which is `''` when the only object is nameless). It failed silently: 0 diagnostics.
- **Fix:** (1) One `defaultNameFor(kind)` mapping in `hydra-universal.js`, used by `createObject` (a plain sprite is now "Sprite", text is "Text", every kind non-empty) and by `normalizeObject` (a blank or whitespace-only name repairs to the kind default on load); `normalizeScene` uniquified names in a scene (Sprite, Sprite 2, ...) in the first version of this fix — **removed in cycle 15**, because it renumbered authored same-name groups on load and broke objectCount, the tag picks, and the for-each loops (see the cycle-15 entry above); `addGameObject` in `hydra/script.js` uniquifies the new name against the scene; the Inspector Name field rejects a blank on blur (restores the previous name, trims otherwise). (2) Blank never matches: `named()` in `hydra/hydra-execute.js` returns `[]` for a blank name (shared module, both runtimes), and the editor host `getObjectsByName` in `hydra/script.js` does the same. (3) No "(none)" where a target is required: `makeObjectSelect` in `hydra/hydra-propinputs.js` takes `allowBlank` (default true); the editor wrapper in `hydra/script.js` defaults it to false, so the hardcoded target panels (Apply Force, teleport, destroy, show/hide, animations, collision Object A/B, the Variables object-scope picker) offer no blank row and coerce a blank stored value to the first object; the catalog-field path passes true explicitly, so blocks that legitimately use blank (knockback "leave From object blank to push away from the X/Y point", emitter followObject) keep it. (4) A block that runs with a blank target now warns once per block type per session into the Diagnostics panel (`targets()` in `hydra-execute.js`, warn-once Set), so a targetless block is visible instead of silent. (5) Toolbar: the Logic command bar no longer spreads with `space-between`; the actions + search group tightly on the left and Dock left + Compact blocks sit in one `.script-command-right` group pinned right (`margin-left: auto`). The Preview's device controls + Live variables now sit in one `.preview-sidebar` pinned top-right, stacked, instead of floating as separate centered flex items of the canvas container (the trace panel stays bottom-left, intentional). (6) The speed cap was tried and REVERTED the same day: capping plain-object velocity in `integrateBody` broke the export test that pins Set Velocity as exact for plain objects (a runner set to 30 must keep 30 and bounce to -30). The correction: Apply Force already caps at maxSpeed (25) on application; Set Velocity is exact on purpose; the sprite left the screen because 25px per frame still crosses the scene in about half a second and nothing stops it at the edge, which is legitimate engine behaviour (clampToScreen / wrapScreen exist for that; Restart resets the scene). `hydra-physicsstep.js` is unchanged. The test file pins the no-clamp behaviour so it is never "fixed" away again.
- **Tests:** new `tests/spritename.test.js` (24 checks after the cycle-15 correction: every kind born named, load repair, same-name groups survive load unchanged, blank target moves nothing + warns once + named target moves, plain-object velocity kept exact / steady glide / non-finite repair, picker allowBlank false/true, Logic bar + Preview sidebar static pins). One stale pin updated in `tests/core.test.js` (the old check pinned the bug: "unnamed sprite keeps blank name" now expects "Sprite"). Full node suite: **122/122 files PASS, 0 FAIL** (`node tests/run.js`, node stage). The browser smoke test could NOT run: Chromium is down with SIGTRAP (exit 133, "Trace/breakpoint trap", confirmed on a direct headless launch at 14:06 EST), the same flickering environment failure as Nov 7-8, so the layout changes are pinned statically but not live-verified in a browser yet. **Done in cycle 15:** Chromium is back, the full suite ran live end to end, and the smoke run caught the uniquify regression above — see the cycle-15 entry.
- **Delivery:** done in the main chat because Ky'lin asked for the fixes immediately ("Hurry up and do these fixes"), but the commit itself could not happen there — the sandbox write block stopped it — so cycle 15 commits it as the recovery part of its commit, after repairing the uniquify regression the live smoke run caught. The GitHub snapshot needs a re-push of the changed files so Ky'lin's downloaded zip carries the fix (the binaries are still absent from that route).

The new smoke section and the visual QA screenshots are NOT yet run: Chromium is down (SIGTRAP on every launch, confirmed repeatedly this cycle), so the smoke loud-skips and no screenshots could be taken. ROADMAP.md / CHANGELOG.md updates and the commit are NOT yet done.
- **Environment block (why this cycle defers instead of committing):** a sandbox EPERM wave started ~12:42 UTC (first symptom: a 0-byte full-run log; then every exec-spawned file write fails with `PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted`, including the write probe, which failed on every 5-minute retry for 60 minutes, last check 13:42 UTC, still failing). Per the write-safety rule the cycle stopped editing and attempted `git checkout -- .` at the 60-minute mark — and the attempt itself did the damage the rule exists to prevent: git opens each target with O_TRUNC and then the write fails with EPERM, so **both edited files were truncated to 0 bytes** (`hydra/script.js` and `tests/browser-smoke.js`, both 0 bytes as of ~13:43 UTC; the checkout exited 128, "unable to write file hydra/script.js / tests/browser-smoke.js", `.git/index.lock` write error). The icon work is therefore LOST from the tree, not merely uncommitted. What survives: `tests/iconcoverage.test.js` (6137 bytes, untracked, intact — it fails against the zeroed/restored files until the icons are re-applied, so the next cycle must restore the two files from HEAD first, then judge it), this PROGRESS.md entry (the full icon spec is in the Fix bullet above), and the probe scripts in the workflow workdir (`icon_probe.js`, `icon_visual_qa.js`). No other files were zeroed (a `find hydra tests -type f -size 0` sweep found only those two). HEAD is intact (`git show HEAD:hydra/script.js` is 532768 bytes, `HEAD:tests/browser-smoke.js` 714781 bytes). No stale `.git/index.lock` was left behind. Chromium SIGTRAP is a separate, concurrent failure (every launch attempt this cycle died the same way). Lesson for the rule itself: during an EPERM wave, `git checkout -- .` is not a safe revert — it truncates first and writes second, so it zeroes the very files it is meant to restore. A revert attempted while writes are failing must be expected to destroy, not restore.
- **Recovery for the next cycle (step 2):** the tree arrives with `hydra/script.js` and `tests/browser-smoke.js` at 0 bytes. First, when writes recover, restore them from HEAD (`git checkout -- hydra/script.js tests/browser-smoke.js`, then verify the byte counts 532768 / 714781 and `node --check` clean). Only then judge the leftovers: `tests/iconcoverage.test.js` is the complete test for the lost icon work — keep it, re-apply the icon changes from the Fix bullet above (the 3 helpers, the `SM.tag` badge, the 16 map entries, the smoke section), and it should go green again; if the icons are not re-applied, the test file is broken against the restored tree and step 2(b) deletes it. The smoke section and the visual-QA screenshots still need Chromium back; ROADMAP.md (check off the icon-coverage item, correcting 13 → 16), CHANGELOG.md, and this PROGRESS.md entry still need their updates, none of which were made in this cycle.

## 2026-11-08 — Cycle 9 (improve): Advanced batch 37 — object parenting / attach — COMMITTED

- **Item (highest priority; every ROADMAP item was already checked off, so per step 3 the next professional-engine capability was researched and added):** batches 34-36 gave the game tags, picking, and seeing a group, but one object still could not ride on another — a sword in the hero's hand, a hat on a goblin, a name tag above an NPC all meant re-aiming the child every frame by hand. Now a child object follows its parent's position with a fixed offset, applied every frame as the last word on the child's position.
- **Fix:** new shared module `hydra/hydra-attach.js` (`HydraAttach`, factorySource pattern, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): run state `{ links: {} }` child name → { parent, dx, dy }, fresh per scene load (a scene load re-clones objects to authored positions, so a stale link would snap the child), never in save slots; `attach` refuses a blank name, a self-attach, and a cycle (walk the chain); attaching again replaces the old link (one parent per child); `applyAttachments` runs as a new `updateAttachments` frame phase after `updateCombat`. Four new blocks in a new **Attach** category (catalog 552 → 556): **Attach Object To Object** (Child + Parent + Offset X/Y, blank offset keeps the current spacing) / **Detach Object** (Object) / **Set Attachment Offset** (Object + Offset X/Y) actions and the invertible **Is Attached** condition (Object + optional Parent; `level` trigger default, evaluates while paused). Wiring: both runtimes' dispatch and contexts, the preview runtime's `attachState()` accessor (reads the run state's record, no module-level stash, so the launch-order issue that bit the minimap cannot bite here), the flow level default, the scene-reset hook, and the test-module load lists in export-boot/conditions/execute/flagship/fps-export/golden/playtest/draw/scenereset. Docs: `### Attach (4)` in `docs/block-reference.md`, an **Attach** paragraph in `docs/user-guide.md`. Visual QA found a real defect in-cycle and it was fixed before commit: the four new blocks had no grid icons, so they fell back to text labels that overflowed the 56px cell and clipped at the grid edge in column 0. Each now has a unique icon in `CONCEPT_ICONS_BY_TYPE` (`hydra/script.js`): Attach is a diagonal pair of interlocked rings (`picChain`), Detach is two separated rings with a slash (`picUnlink`), Set Attachment Offset is a ring with a double-headed shift arrow (`picOffsetLink`), Is Attached reuses the chain with the standard question badge (`mod(picChain(), SM.q)`); all symmetric about the block center (28, 28), pinned by static checks in `tests/attach.test.js` and a smoke check that no placed attach cell renders its label as text.
- **Honest limits:** position only, no rotation or scale following; one parent per child (attaching again replaces the link); a child follows the first live object with the parent's name; links clear on scene load (re-attach in a gameStart row); no `hs` script-API surface yet. Follow-up found in-cycle, not fixed here: the same text-label fallback still affects the 13 blocks from batches 34-36 (4 tags, 4 pick-by-tag, 5 minimap), verified with a browser probe that calls `conceptIconForBlock` for each — they need the same icon treatment, recorded as a new ROADMAP item.
- **Tests:** new `tests/attach.test.js` (39 checks: module surface + factorySource, state, attach/detach/offset, cycle detection, chained apply, quiet/missing/non-finite cases, routing, hostile input, static wiring pins, icon uniqueness + symmetry). Integration pins: `tests/conditions.test.js` 473 (Is Attached true/false/reversed/blank/raw-table/no-freeze), `tests/execute.test.js` 612 (blank-offset spacing, explicit offsets, setOffset, detach, all warn-and-hold cases incl. cycle, quiet without module/state), `tests/scenereset.test.js` 99 (`createAttachState` in `fullHooks`, mint/re-mint/missing/throwing), `tests/flow.test.js` 28 (level default), `tests/frame.test.js` 48 (updateAttachments after updateCombat), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 661 (Attach wiring section), `tests/catalog.test.js` 73 (556 blocks), `tests/docs.test.js` 9, export-boot Phase CZ 16/16 (inlining, missing-module guard, hero/hat/sword follow positions, Is Attached fires, detach page, cycle warns + holds, setOffset, scene switch clears links, zero diagnostics on happy paths), browser-smoke Attach section (module, palette, category, order, four placements + panels, icon-not-text check, live previews follow + detach with pixel probes, zero page errors). ## 2026-11-08 — Cycle 8 (improve): Advanced batch 36 — minimap — COMMITTED

- **Item (highest priority; every ROADMAP item was already checked off, so per step 3 the next professional-engine capability was researched and added):** batch 35 let the game pick and walk a tagged group, but the player still could not *see* where the goblins are. Now a small live map sits in a screen corner: the whole level shrunk down, a dot for every live object, a white rectangle showing what the camera sees, so \"where is the exit\" and \"where are the goblins left\" read at a glance.
- **Fix:** new shared module `hydra/hydra-minimap.js` (`HydraMinimap`, factorySource pattern, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): per-scene authored config `scene.minimap` (enabled off by default, corner top-right, size 160px, zoom 1 = fit the whole scene, show-viewport on) with a Scene Settings **Minimap** section (enabled checkbox, corner select, size and zoom fields, viewport checkbox); run-level state minted fresh from the scene config on every scene load (like camera feel), never in project JSON or save slots. Five new blocks in a new **Minimap** category (catalog 547 → 552): **Show Minimap** / **Hide Minimap** / **Set Minimap Size** / **Set Minimap Zoom** actions and the invertible **Minimap Visible** condition (`level` trigger default, evaluates while paused). Dots: the boss object red and large while a fight runs, an object tagged `player` white and large, any other tagged object in a deterministic color from its first tag (same tag always reads the same color), untagged objects gray; hidden/destroyed/HUD/light objects never draw. Zoom above 1 shows a window of the scene centered on the camera, clamped to the scene edges. The map paints in the HUD pass of the shared draw module, above the HUD and below the screen flash, in both runtimes. Wiring: both runtimes' dispatch and contexts, the preview runtime's per-scene mint + reset-on-fresh-run-only, the editor's draw hooks, the flow level default, the scene-reset hook, the `hydra-universal.js` normalizer, and the test-module load lists in export-boot/conditions/execute/flagship/fps-export/golden/playtest/draw/scenereset. Docs: `### Minimap (5)` in `docs/block-reference.md`, a **Minimap** paragraph in `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Honest limits:** one minimap per scene (corner/size/zoom are per-scene config; blocks change size/zoom/visibility only); dots are positions only (no health, no names); zoom centers on the camera, not on the player; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Tests:** new `tests/minimap.test.js` (50 checks: module surface + factorySource, all normalizers incl. in-place repair, state creation without aliasing, setVisible/setSize/setZoom clamps + false on bad input, geometry incl. zoomed window clamping, tagColor determinism, dotStyle ranking boss > player > tag > untagged, drawMinimap paint on a recording context, quiet cases, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring pins). ## 2026-11-08 — Cycle 7 (improve): Advanced batch 35 — pick objects by tag — COMMITTED

- **Item (highest priority; every ROADMAP item was already checked off, so per step 3 the next professional-engine capability was researched and added):** batch 34 gave the game tags and counting, but no way to pick one of them — "damage the nearest goblin" still meant naming each goblin. Now a tag pick chooses one live object carrying the tag, and a tag loop walks the whole group one at a time, so the classic rows are plain blocks: Pick Nearest Tagged goblin → Damage Object, and For Each Tagged Object goblin → Damage Object.
- **Fix:** five new blocks (catalog 542 → 547). The pick geometry stays in the shared `hydra-pick.js` module (new `evalPickTaggedCondition` / `pickTaggedObject` over a `liveObjectsByTag` pick hook both runtimes supply through HydraTags; matching is trimmed and case-insensitive, a blank tag never matches; fallback filters `allLiveObjects()` with the same matcher when neither hook exists). **Pick Nearest Tagged** / **Pick Farthest Tagged** / **Pick Random Tagged** conditions in the **Tags** category (`level` trigger default for nearest/farthest, `edge` for random, evaluate while paused) and **Pick Tagged Object Into Variable** (mode nearest/farthest/random/first + scope + variable) in the same category, plus **For Each Tagged Object** (Tag + Object variable + Index variable) in the **Control Flow** category. The loop lives in `hydra-execute.js` (`runForEachTagged`: snapshot at start, first 1,000 with a warning naming the real total, Break/Continue + per-frame budget like the other loops, context narrowing per round with restore, index counting 1, 2, 3; the Object variable holds the round's handle as a flat variable, readable with Read Object Property). A real bug found by the export tests and fixed in-cycle: `HydraPick.isPickCondition` only knew the name picks, so the three tag picks were "Unknown block type" in the exported runtime — it now covers the tag picks too, and `tests/picktagged.test.js` pins it. Wiring: both runtimes' pick hooks and contexts, the flow level defaults, the object-reference routing, the loop classification. Docs: `### Tags (10)` and `### Control Flow (10)` in `docs/block-reference.md`, the Object tags paragraph in `docs/user-guide.md` rewritten around the five new blocks (the "no pick-by-tag yet" limit is gone).
- **Honest limits:** a chained action only narrows onto the picked/walked object when it names that object's name — the same rule as the existing name picks. When the tagged objects have different names, hold the pick in a variable and read it with Read Object Property, or destroy it with Destroy Object In Variable. A tag never travels in save slots; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Tests:** new `tests/picktagged.test.js` (36 checks: module surface, matcher, pickers, condition dispatch incl. hook fallback and throwing-hook cases, `pickTaggedObject` modes, static wiring pins for conditions/both runtimes/flow/loops/execute, catalog 547 + category order + fields, docs pins, `isPickCondition` covering the tag picks). Integration pins: `tests/loops.test.js` 104/104 (11 new forEachTagged checks + a mixed-name dispatch test), `tests/conditions.test.js` 467/467 (real-behavior section: nearest/farthest/point/random, quiet cases, reversed, not frozen while paused), `tests/objectrefs.test.js` 46/46 (7 new Pick Tagged Object Into Variable execute checks), `tests/flow.test.js` 28/28, `tests/catalog.test.js` 73/73, `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 638/638 (new `liveObjectsByTag` pin), export-boot Phase CX 18/18 (inlining; nearest/farthest/random gated damage with both goblins sharing the name "goblin", so the narrowing is what keeps the damage off the other one; for-each over same-name coins and over mixed names through the object variable; pick-into-variable nearest + farthest; blank tag warns once on the actions and stays quiet on the conditions; zero diagnostics on every happy path), browser-smoke batch-35 section (Tags palette now 10 in order, Control Flow holds For Each Tagged Object after For Each Field, three placements + panels, live preview: nearest goblin held at x 500, damaged to 20 while the farthest still reads 30, loop runs 3 rounds and damages a coin to 5, zero page errors). ## 2026-11-08 — Cycle 6 (improve): Advanced batch 34 — object tags / groups — COMMITTED

- **Item (highest priority; every ROADMAP item was already checked off, so per step 3 the next professional-engine capability was researched and added):** a game had no way to ask about a whole group of objects at once — "how many goblins are left" meant naming each one. Now a tag is a plain label on an object, so Has Tag asks whether an object carries one and Tagged Count counts the live objects carrying one, and the classic win row is Tagged Count goblin equals 0.
- **Fix:** new shared module `hydra/hydra-tags.js` (`HydraTags`, factorySource pattern, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): no per-run state — tags live on the objects themselves (`obj.tags`, already normalized to a list by `hydra-universal.js`, now also repaired in place: trimmed, deduped case-insensitively with the first spelling winning, at most 16 per object, 64 characters per tag). Six new blocks in a new **Tags** category (catalog 536 → 542): **Add Tag** (Object + Tag) / **Remove Tag** (Object + Tag) / **Clear Tags** (Object) / **Read Tagged Count** (Tag + Into variable) actions and the invertible **Has Tag** (Object + Tag) / **Tagged Count** (Tag + compare, default `== 0`) conditions (`level` trigger default, evaluate while paused). Matching ignores case, so Goblin and goblin are the same tag. The Inspector **Tags** section lists an object's tags as removable chips with an add box — those are the permanent tags, restored on every scene load; runtime tags last until the scene loads again (a scene load re-clones its objects from the authored definitions). Wiring: both runtimes' dispatch and contexts, the flow level defaults, the defensive normalize in `hydra-universal.js`, and the test-module load lists in export-boot/conditions/execute/flagship/fps-export/golden/playtest. Docs: `### Tags (6)` in `docs/block-reference.md`, an **Object tags** paragraph in `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Honest limits:** no pick-by-tag yet — pick the objects by name, or walk the count; a tag never travels in save slots (persist a count through Read Tagged Count into a variable, the way quests and inventory do); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Tests:** new `tests/tags.test.js` (40 checks: normalizers, cap + dedupe + first-spelling-wins, has/add/remove/clear, objectsWithTag/countTagged, classifiers, hostile input, factorySource, static wiring pins for both runtimes + the Inspector editor + the catalog). Integration pins: `tests/conditions.test.js` (Has Tag / Tagged Count reads, operators, reversed, raw-array fallback, not frozen while paused), `tests/execute.test.js` (the four actions end to end: add → count → remove → clear, blank-name and blank-tag warnings, quiet without the module), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (both contexts, no per-run state), `tests/flow.test.js` (both level defaults), `tests/catalog.test.js` (542 blocks), `tests/docs.test.js`, export-boot Phase CW (12 checks: inlining, missing-module guard, authored tags at boot, add/remove/clear, blank tag warns-and-holds, scene round trip drops the runtime tag and keeps the authored ones), browser-smoke Tags section (module, palette, category, order, six placements + panels, Inspector chips add/remove round trip, live preview: 2 authored goblins counted, runtime tag on the hero). Three tests written wrong in-cycle and fixed by the tests, not the code: the Phase CW guard regex was over-escaped (`\\.` never matched) — replaced with a plain `indexOf` string check; the scene-2 trigger used the wrong type `everyFrame` — the correct runtimeType is `always`; the round-trip assertion pinned `taggedHero`, a variable set in frame 1 — it now pins the live hero's tags list being empty. Two more fixes found by the full runner: ten older test files still pinned the boss-cycle catalog count (536) — all updated to 542; and the smoke preview wait used `window.gameVariables`, which is always undefined because `gameVariables` is a top-level `const`, not a `window` property — it now uses bare `gameVariables` like every other smoke wait, and the live preview then passed (`gLeft` 2, `taggedHero` 1).
- **Visual QA:** eight screenshots were captured with probe scripts and opened and inspected: the Tags palette (all 6 blocks in order under TAGS, teal colors distinct from the Boss reds, no clipping), the Add Tag panel (Object dropdown, Tag field with "e.g. goblin" placeholder, Target matching disclosure), the Has Tag panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)" — the level default, helper text, Object, Tag, Invert), the Read Tagged Count panel (Tag, Into variable "e.g. goblinsLeft"), the Tagged Count panel (Trigger level, Tag, Compare ==, Value 0 with fx, Invert), the Inspector Tags section empty ("No tags yet.", Add a tag box, Add tag button) and with a goblin chip (teal chip with ×, input + button below it), and the live preview canvas (red hero bottom-left, two green goblins top, correct positions). No defects found, nothing to fix in-cycle. Screenshots: workflow workdir `tags-{palette,panel-add,panel-has,panel-read,panel-count,inspector-empty-tight,inspector-chip,preview}.png`. Zero page errors on every probe page.

All three of Ky'lin's queued items from Nov 7 are now done (device letterbox cycle 24, universal velocity cycle 25, force arrow cycle 26). The write probe failed once at the start of this cycle with EPERM writing 64KB to /tmp, then recovered after ~30s and a service restart (WRITES_OK on retry); git writes worked afterward.
- **Item (highest priority; every ROADMAP item was already checked off, so per step 3 the next professional-engine capability was researched and added):** the hero fights, levels, grows, and the enemies scale, but the run had no climax — no big fight at the end of it. Now it does: a named boss object with health phases and a wide health bar across the top of the screen, so a multi-stage fight is plain blocks.
- **Fix:** new shared module `hydra/hydra-boss.js` (`HydraBoss`, factorySource pattern, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): transient per-run boss state (one boss at a time; survives scene switches, resets on a fresh run, never in project JSON or save slots) holding the boss object's name, the phase count (2–5, default 3), and bar visibility. Phases are equal slices of the boss's health bar, derived live from the health fraction — no frame phase needed (phase 1 at full health, the last phase in the final slice). Seven new blocks in a new **Boss** category (catalog 529 → 536): **Start Boss** (Object + Phases + Show bar) / **End Boss** / **Show Boss Bar** / **Hide Boss Bar** / **Read Boss Phase** (Into variable) actions and the invertible **Boss Phase** (compare) / **Boss Active** conditions (`level` trigger default, evaluate while paused). The bar draws in the HUD pass of the shared draw module (wide centered bar + boss name, green/amber/red by fraction like the per-object health bars); it shows only while a boss is active, the bar is on, and a live object with the boss's name exists. Wiring: both runtimes' dispatch and contexts, the preview runtime's lazy mint + reset-on-fresh-run-only, the editor's draw hooks, the flow level defaults, and the test-module load lists in export-boot/conditions/execute/fps-export/flagship/golden/playtest. Docs: `### Boss (7)` in `docs/block-reference.md`, a **Boss encounters** paragraph in `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Honest limits:** one boss at a time (starting a second boss replaces the first); phases are equal health slices, not custom thresholds — gate phase behavior on Boss Phase and wire it with ordinary blocks (Set AI Behavior, Set Scaling Rates, Start Spawner); the bar shows the first live object with the boss's name; a boss that has not spawned yet reads phase 0 and shows no bar until it exists; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Tests:** new `tests/boss.test.js` (49 checks: module surface + factorySource, state + normalizers, reads, writes, bar geometry + color, bar paint on a recording context, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring pins for both runtimes + the draw module + scene-reset survival + catalog + docs). Integration pins: `tests/conditions.test.js` (Boss Phase / Boss Active reads, operators, reversed, raw-state fallback, phase 0, not frozen while paused), `tests/execute.test.js` (the five actions end to end: start → phase 1/2/3 reads at 300/150/50 → hide/show bar → end → phase 0, blank-name and bad-phase warnings, quiet without module/state), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (both contexts, lazy mint, reset drops the fight, null without the module), `tests/draw.test.js` (bar geometry + colors at full/half/low health, no bar with no fight / hidden bar / unspawned boss / missing module), `tests/flow.test.js` (both level defaults), `tests/catalog.test.js` (536 blocks), `tests/docs.test.js`, export-boot Phase CV (18 checks: inlining, missing-module guard, happy path, damage moves the phase to 2 then 3, bar toggle + end, blank name warns-and-holds with exactly one diagnostic, the fight survives a scene switch), browser-smoke Boss section (module, palette, category, order, seven placements + panels, live preview: green bar at full health with phase 1 / Boss Active firing / Boss Phase quiet, red bar + phase 3 + gated row at 50 of 300). Two tests written wrong in-cycle and fixed by the tests, not the code: the draw recorder's fillStyle sits at index 5 of a fillRect call, not 4 (the name text at index 4 is right — fillText has one fewer arg), and `phaseOf` coerced an array to 0 via `Number([])` (a dead boss instead of no reading), so the module now only accepts real numbers or numeric strings as a fraction; the smoke seed's damage row ran after Read Boss Phase, so the phase read 1 — damage now runs before the read.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 26 (improve): Apply Force block arrow rotates to the force direction — DONE, COMMITTED in cycle 27 (recovery)

- **Item (highest priority, the last of Ky'lin's three queued items, Nov 7):** the Apply Force block's icon was a static right-pointing arrow no matter what force it carried. Now the arrow on a PLACED block rotates by atan2(forceY, forceX) in screen coords (Y down): (-100, 0) left, (0, 100) down, (0, -100) up, (100, 100) down-right. An expression in X or Y ("5 or speed") cannot give a static angle, so the arrow stays pointing right; a zero force has no direction either. The palette icon stays generic (no block data behind it).
- **Fix:** new shared module `hydra/hydra-forcearrow.js` (`HydraForceArrow`, factorySource pattern): `toNumber` (numbers and numeric strings count, expressions/blanks/NaN/Infinity do not), `angleDeg` (atan2 in degrees, rounded to 2 decimals, -180 → 180, -0 → 0, null for non-numeric or a zero force), `isForceBlock` (data.type === 'applyForce'), `angleForBlock`, `rotateTransform` (pivots on the icon center 28, 28). `hydra/script.js`: `conceptBlockSVG` takes an optional 5th angle param and wraps ONLY the Apply Force icon in `<g transform="rotate(angle 28 28)">` (label-checked, so no other icon ever rotates); `applyBlockVisualToCell` computes the angle from the cell's own data (guarded when the module is missing); placing a block now sets its defaults BEFORE drawing, so a fresh place already shows its angle (the old order rendered before `initCellDefaults`, which would have left a fresh (5, 0) block unrotated until the first panel edit); the X/Y panel fields in `showActionProperties` re-render the cell on every keystroke via a new `refreshForceArrow` helper (the input lives in the panel, not the cell, so no focus is lost). `hydra/index.html` loads the module after the other editor modules. Docs: the Apply Force section of `docs/block-reference.md` now states the arrow rule and the expression fallback; CHANGELOG.md carries the user-facing note.
- **Tests:** new `tests/forcearrow.test.js` (58 checks: parse cases, all eight spec directions plus the 1,2 atan2 pin, expression/zero/missing fallbacks, block gating, rotateTransform, factorySource, the script.js wiring pins, the index.html script tag, the docs pin) and a browser-smoke section (10 checks: palette stays generic, place, fresh (5, 0) right, panel X/Y fields, left 180 / down 90 / diagonal 45, expression X keeps the default arrow, back to left, reload restores the stored direction past the ~900ms autosave). **Full verification:** the full runner `node tests/run.js` exits 0 (RUNNER_EXIT=0) — that run's smoke phase went live end to end, so the 10 new smoke checks passed in a real browser, with zero page errors.
- **Visual QA, closed in cycle 27:** the screenshots could not be taken in cycle 26 (Chromium SIGTRAP flicker), but Chromium was alive again in cycle 27, so the probe script ran clean: angles 0 / 180 / 45 / -90 in the live DOM, zero page errors. The four screenshots were opened and inspected — left arrow points left, diagonal points down-right, up points up, all clean white arrows on the red block with no clipping, and the grid shot shows the placed blocks' arrows (right, left, up, down, diagonal) with no overlap or layout defect. Screenshots: workflow workdir `force-arrow-{left,diagonal,up,grid}.png`.
- **Base note:** HEAD moved mid-cycle to `ff8e7c4` (the main session's temporary editor-sounds overlay, committed while this cycle held the lock); this commit sits on top of it and touches none of its files.
- **Commit status:** COMMITTED in cycle 27 (recovery) as `1a3bbe1` — the tree was re-verified first (full runner exit 0, 10,565 `ok -` lines, ALL TESTS PASSED, live smoke green) and the visual-QA gap closed with inspected screenshots, so nothing was trusted on cycle 26's word alone. All three of Ky'lin's queued items are now done (letterbox, universal velocity, force arrow). Next: the flagship run (UI polish, menu widgets, save slots, pause/resume, then a complete multi-level platformer built only with the engine's blocks and verified beatable in the editor and as an exported standalone game), then the pro-engine pillars resume.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 25 (improve): Apply Force now moves non-physics objects (universal velocity) — COMMITTED

- **Item (highest priority, Ky'lin's queued brief, Nov 7):** Apply Force on a non-physics object silently did nothing — `applyVelocity` wrote velX/velY onto any non-text object, but `integrateBody` returned early for `!physicsEnabled`, so the force built up a velocity that never moved the object, with no warning anywhere. Took the brief's recommended option (a): velocity is now universal, GDevelop-style, with gravity, friction, the max-speed clamp, and collision staying physics-gated. The remaining queued item is the force-arrow rotation (last of the three).
- **Fix:** `hydra/hydra-physicsstep.js` gains `isVelocityBody` (the same exclusions as `isPhysicsBody` except physics is not required) and `integrateBody` now takes two paths: a physics body integrates exactly as before (gravity, friction ^ dt, clamp, position advance); a plain object takes the pure velocity step (pos += vel * dt, velocity untouched — no gravity, no friction decay, no clamp), with a non-finite velocity repaired to 0 so a bad record can never NaN a position. Text, static objects, 3D kinds, and lights still never move. One shared module, so the editor preview and the exported runtime change together (the export inlines the module via factorySource). The stale "only moves physics objects" claims are fixed where they lived: the Knockback Object note in `block-catalog.js`, the knockback comments in `hydra-execute.js` and `hydra-damagefx.js` (header + honest limits), and the Apply Force / Set Velocity / Knockback Object sections of `docs/block-reference.md` plus the knockback line in `docs/user-guide.md` — all now say any non-static, non-text object moves, and a plain object glides at a steady speed with no gravity, friction, or collisions.
- **Honest limits:** a plain object glides through solids and trigger zones (collision and triggers stay physics-gated); Set Velocity on a plain object is not clamped to maxSpeed (Apply Force and Knockback still are, at the write, via the default maxSpeed 25); old games are unaffected unless they set velocity on a non-physics object, where it only ever did nothing before.
- **Tests:** `tests/physicsstep.test.js` +22 checks (isVelocityBody pins; glide with gravity/friction/clamp ignored; huge velocity unclamped; non-finite velocity repaired; text, static, and 3D objects still stand still; step-level glide, fall-through-the-floor, physics body still lands, no trigger contact; factorySource carries the predicate) and export-boot Phase CU +7 checks (a hand-built project in the exported runtime: glider moves right at 60 px/s with posY and velX unchanged, faller sinks through the floor, Apply Force accelerates a plain object to the default max speed 25, the static rock never moves, zero diagnostics). One test written wrong in-cycle and fixed by the test, not the code: the step-level physics box started already overlapping the floor, so the resolver pushed it below the floor (posY 220) instead of landing it — starting it above the floor lands it at 160 as intended.
- **Visual QA:** this cycle changed no rendering, layout, widget, template, or editor UI — the change is physics integration plus block notes and docs, verified by the node tests and the live smoke run (zero page errors). No new screenshots apply.
- **Full verification:** the per-file node loop is **111/111 files, 9419 `ok -` lines, 0 failures** (9390 at cycle 24 + 29 new: 22 physicsstep + 7 export-boot); the full runner `node tests/run.js` exits 0; the standalone smoke test ran live twice end to end and passed both times (**1078 ok, 0 failures**, "browser-smoke.js: all checks passed" — the first full-run attempt's smoke phase loud-skipped on the Chromium launch flicker, then both standalone launches ran fine). **Commit status:** COMMITTED as `c98e2a8` (hash recorded in the follow-up commit). Next: the Apply Force block arrow rotates to the force direction (last of Ky'lin's three queued items).

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 23 (improve): recover cycle 22 tree, confirm the smoke test, fix two real outliner defects — COMMITTED

- **Recovery (step 2):** the tree arrived with cycle 22's work still uncommitted (P5-9, the F2 load-order fix, the smoke repairs: 24 modified files + new `tests/readtext.test.js` + `tests/script-load-order.test.js`). The F2 fix and the P5-9 work were re-verified instead of trusted: all **111 node test files pass individually (9375 `ok -` lines, 0 failures)**, syntax clean, no 0-byte files.
- **The confirming smoke re-run (cycle 22's open item) — GREEN, then two more latent sections surfaced.** The first full smoke run this cycle reached 921 ok and failed 7 checks, all in the paneldock-reload and outliner sections — sections that had never run live before (cycle 22's run died at 912 checks on the 2h cap; the outliner section starts after that). (1) The paneldock reload check repeated cycle 22's already-fixed mistake in one more place: it asserted `dockState().stored.logic === 330`, but `stored` holds the dock SIDES (`{scene, logic}` strings) — the stored width lives in `panelWidths().stored`. The visible state was already right (logic 330px restored); only the assertion read the wrong store. Fix: read the width from `panelWidths()`, keep the side assertion as `'left'`. (2) The outliner section assumed a pristine 6-object collector on the main page, but earlier sections had added 15 objects to it (panels, 3D objects, tilemaps, sprites, joints) — 21 rows, a Layer 20 group, and a covering object that ate the locked-drag mousedown. Fix: the section now runs in its own browser context with a fresh collector project, the same pattern as hub2/hub3 and the inspector page. (3) The section's layer expectations were stale: the collector's text objects (scoreLabel, scoreText, hint) sit on Layer 20 (`text()` defaults to layer 20 in `starter-templates.js`), not Layer 0, so the groups are Layer 20 · 3 / L5 / L4 / L3 and the rows run scoreLabel, scoreText, hint, player, hazard, coin — highest layer first. The collapse-index assertions moved from [2] to [3] with the group order, and the keyboard checks now walk from the coin (last row) up to hazard and back down, instead of assuming the coin sat mid-list. (4) The density section's three bgSize assertions expected a single-layer `'56px 56px'`, but `.grid-container` carries two background layers (horizontal + vertical grid lines), so the computed size is `'56px 56px, 56px 56px'` (and `'44px 44px, 44px 44px'` compact). The values were already right; the expectation was wrong — the section had never run live (Chromium down through cycle 21, cycle 22 died before it, cycle 23's first run threw at the rename step before reaching it).
- **Two REAL engine defects, both caught by the outliner section and proven with a Playwright repro before fixing.** (1) **A drag that starts on a locked object deselects it.** The lock correctly blocks the drag (`isDragging` never sets), but the press-on-object / release-elsewhere click bubbles with the scene as its target, and the scene's click-to-deselect handler fires on it — clearing the selection and emptying the Inspector, even though the mousedown had just selected the object (repro: after the locked drag, `active ""`, `summary ""`, `coinX ""`, while the coin itself never moved, posX still 520). Fix: a capture-phase document mousedown listener records `sceneMouseDownTarget`, and the deselect handler now requires the press to have started on empty scene too (`sceneMouseDownTarget !== newScene` returns). A genuine click on empty scene still deselects. (2) **Double-click rename never opened.** Two breaks stacked: the row click re-rendered the whole list, replacing the name span between the dblclick's two clicks so the browser never fired dblclick — and even with the re-render removed, the first click switched the Inspector tab away from the list, hiding the span before the second click landed (repro: rename input count 0 after dblclick; diag showed the span going invisible mid-dblclick). Fix: row clicks take a new `keepListDom` option on `selectGameObjectById` (in-place active-class sync instead of a full re-render; center / layer-nudge callers keep the re-render because they change the data the list displays), and a click whose target is the name span passes `switchTab: false`, so it selects in place and stays in the list — a click anywhere else on the row still opens the Inspector, and the smoke select check (which clicks the row, not the name) still passes. Verified live with the repro/diag: row click still opens the Inspector, the span survives a single click (same node), the dblclick event fires on the name, and the rename input opens. New pins in `tests/outliner.test.js` (+3 checks: the mousedown-target guard, keepListDom, the name-click stays-in-list rule).
- **Visual QA:** this cycle changed no rendering, layout, widget, template, or editor UI — the engine changes are selection/deselect behavior and list DOM stability, both verified live by the smoke section's own drag/rename/keyboard steps plus the standalone repro. No new screenshots apply beyond that.
- **Full verification:** the standalone smoke test passes end to end (**1077 ok, 0 failures**, "browser-smoke.js: all checks passed"), the per-file node loop is **111/111 files, 9375 `ok -` lines, 0 failures**, and the full runner `node tests/run.js` exits 0 with **"ALL TESTS PASSED" (10,452 `ok -` lines)**. **Commit status:** COMMITTED as `91275c1` — the tree that sat uncommitted since cycle 21 (P5-8 slice c) through cycle 22 (P5-9 + F2 fix) landed in one commit with this cycle's smoke/outliner fixes. **P5 is COMPLETE and committed.** Next: the queued force-arrow / device-squeeze brief (Ky'lin, Nov 7): Apply Force arrow rotates to the force direction, device-mode preview letterbox fix, Apply Force on non-physics objects.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 22 (improve): F2 load-order defect fixed, P5-9 the build-the-editor test COMPLETED

- **F2 defect — diagnosed and fixed (the open defect at HEAD).** The browser-smoke F2 panel step failed because `let outlinerCollapsed = null;` was declared in `hydra/script.js` AFTER the top-level load-time call `initOutliner()`, so the call threw a temporal-dead-zone ReferenceError that aborted the rest of `script.js`: the inspector-tab wiring never ran, the Create panel stayed hidden, and every later `renderObjectList()` threw the same error. Proven with a Playwright repro (3 `PAGEERROR: Cannot access 'outlinerCollapsed' before initialization` events, stacks through `initOutliner` / `renderObjectList` / `renderSceneObjects`), not guessed. Fix: the declaration moved above the call site, with a comment stating the ordering rule. Repro re-run after the fix: zero page errors, the panel button visible and clickable, a panel created with HUD badge. New regression test `tests/script-load-order.test.js` (5 checks): a scanner that blanks strings/comments/regex literals, collects top-level `let`/`const` bindings and load-time call sites (brace depth 0 only), and flags any load-time assignment to a binding declared after the call site. Negative control: restoring the old ordering fails the test with exactly the right diagnosis.
- **P5-9 — the build-the-editor test COMPLETED (P5 is now COMPLETE).** The Variable Inspector template (`id: 'variable-inspector'`, "Variable Inspector") is a working variable inspector built only from HydraScript blocks: it lists global score, scene coins, the goblin's object hp, and a global text name; edits them with - / + buttons or by typing a number; and watches them change live from play (coin collect, goblin bump, kill chain) and from the panel. No block sets the starting values — they come from the project's scoped-variable definitions (score 0, coins 0, hp 30, name 'Ky'). The one primitive it needed did not exist, so P5-9 adds **Read Text Into Variable** (block 529; Variables category now 44): it reads a text object's displayed text (`_runtimeText` when present, else the authored text) into a flat variable, in Text mode as-is or Number mode parsed with `Number()` (blank reads as 0, non-number warns and holds). It routes through the existing scoped-variable classifier, so the export player and the preview need no new wiring. Tests: new `tests/readtext.test.js` (31 checks); export-boot Phase CT plays the real template in the exported runtime end to end (starting values, + / -, typing 42 + Set + Clear, coin collect, goblin bump, hp - / +, the 30-click kill chain: goblin destroyed, +50, hp re-armed to 30); browser-smoke P5-9 section drives the same flow in the live editor and preview. Docs: block-reference Variables (44) + full section, user-guide typed-input paragraph.
- **Two real defects caught while verifying P5-9, both fixed in-cycle.** (1) Template layout adjacency accident: the - / + button rows sat directly below the kill chain's action row, and a condition fires ALL adjacent actions, so clicking scoreMinus also fired the destroyObject above it — the goblin died and the score jumped +50 on every minus click. Fix: a blank gap row between the kill chain and the button rows. The templates audit now also checks the reverse direction — no plain condition on another row may sit directly above or below an action (the vertical-and pattern, where an and/or combinator sits above its actions, stays allowed) — with a negative control that reintroduces the bug and fails with exactly `601:destroyObject fired by 661:objectClicked on another row`. (2) Test-harness edge-trigger artifact: `objectClicked` and `keyJustPressed` are edge-triggered, so two presses of the SAME condition in back-to-back single-frame clicks look like one long press and the second is swallowed. Real input always has idle frames between presses; the export-boot `ctClick`/`ctPressKey` helpers now pump one idle frame after each press, and the kill-chain test counts thirty clicks (30 - 1 takes hp 30 to 0), not twenty. No engine semantics changed for either fix.
- **Visual QA defects found by actually opening the screenshots, fixed in-cycle.** (1) The - / + / Set / Clear controls were plain text objects with a `color` set — but `drawTextObject` only fills a background when `HydraUI.isButton` is true, so they rendered as bare glyphs and the empty input field was invisible. The buttons are now real Button components (text + `archetype: 'button'` + Button component + explicit `buttonStyle`; Set blue, the rest slate) and the input has a panel behind it (`scoreInputBox`, layer 19 under the text). (2) The one-line hint ran past the canvas edge ("...and press" cut off); it is now two lines that fit. Verified with fresh screenshots after the fix: the preview shows slate - / + buttons, a blue Set, a visible input box, and both hint lines complete; the exported standalone HTML boots in a real browser with zero errors and plays the same flow (score 42 after typing 42 + Set).
- **Home card for the inspector.** `home.html` had hardcoded template cards and the inspector had none, so the template was unreachable from the hub. Added the Variable Inspector card after Coin Collector.
- **Smoke-test repairs found by the first live Chromium run since the EPERM wave.** The smoke test had never actually executed past F2 (Chromium was SIGTRAP-broken through cycle 21), so latent failures surfaced once it ran for real. (1) Two Control Flow palette checks were stale from before P5-6 slice c: the palette now holds nine blocks including For Each Field, so the checks expect the nine-block order and "seven loop blocks" — fixed, verified passing in the standalone smoke run. (2) The spawn section's `selectOption('hazard')` threw because the main page was editing the FLAGSHIP, not the collector: hub2 creates the flagship on the shared browser context, writing its id as the current project in localStorage, and the panel-width section's reload then opened the flagship (proven by a debug dump: the Object select listed flagship objects across 8 scenes, no hazard). Fix: hub2, hub3, the inspector page, and the project-JSON page each get their own browser context (the pattern the seeded-project sections already use), so their localStorage can never steer the main page's reloads — the spawn section passes after this fix. (3) The paneldock section's ArrowLeft/Home checks read the wrong store: they asserted `dockState().stored.logic === 310/320`, but `dockState().stored` is the dock SIDES (`{scene, logic}` strings) while the widths live in `panelWidths().stored` — the visible widths were already right (310px/320px), only the persistence assertion was wrong. Fix: assert the stored width via `panelWidths()`. The same section's reload sequence also missed a tab switch: it clicked the scene dock button while on the Logic tab (button not visible, 30s timeout throw) — fix: switch to the Scene tab for the dock click, back to the Logic tab for the resizer. These three dock fixes are applied but the confirming smoke re-run did NOT finish inside this cycle (the cycle hit its 2h cap mid-run): all node test files pass individually (197 PASS in the full runner, 0 failures, 10231 `ok -` lines in the run that reached the smoke step), syntax clean, and the standalone smoke run reached 912 ok with only those 3 dock checks failing. **Next cycle: re-run the smoke test standalone; if green, run the full runner once and commit. P5-9's own work (template, block, export-boot, smoke P5-9 section, visual QA) is verified; the only unverified piece is the dock-section fix re-run.**
- **Queued work (Ky'lin, Nov 7, 2026):** the force-arrow / device-squeeze brief at `goals/hydrascript-continuous-engine-development/agent_notes/force-arrow-device-squeeze-brief.md` is recorded in ROADMAP.md (Apply Force arrow rotates to the force direction; device-mode preview letterbox fix; Apply Force on non-physics objects). Not part of this cycle's P5-9 work.

Each row carries an eye and a lock. The eye is a starting-visibility flag (Godot-style): a hidden object leaves the editor stage and the 3D view AND starts the game hidden — the shared scene reset seeds the run's hidden set from `obj.hidden === true` at scene load, so the draw module skips it and Object Visible reads it as gone until a Show Object block reveals it; the row stays in the list, dimmed, so the object is never lost. The lock is editor-only: a locked object still selects on canvas (so it can be inspected and unlocked) but never drags or tile-paints, and its stage element gets a dashed outline. The bug it fixes is real and silent — on any grid taller than 60 rows (Neon Arena's arena grid is ~244 rows) every down-link past row 60 vanished, so an `and` at row 60+ saw zero neighbours and never passed. **The change, file by file:** `hydra/hydra-flow.js` is now the ONE definition of flow adjacency — its `adjacentIndexes` drops the `row + 1 < GRID_W` guard (columns still cap at 60) with a comment naming the bug and the single-definition rule. `hydra/player-runtime.js` loses its local `adjacentIndexes` and its dead `getActionIndexes` (defined but never called — the dispatch runs through HydraDispatch + HydraFlow); its condition context now reads `HydraFlow.adjacentIndexes`, and the player gains the hard load guard every other shared module already has (missing `hydra-flow.js` fails loudly at install instead of as a bare-name ReferenceError mid-game). `hydra/hydra-previewruntime.js` loses its local `getAdjacentIndexes` (up/down/left/right order, rows capped at 60) — the export keeps the name as a thin resolver through `mod('Flow')`, degrading to no neighbours when the module is missing, like every other dropped module tag. `hydra/hydra-universal.js` drops the row cap in the diagnostics copy (it cannot import HydraFlow — it loads first and is required standalone in node tests — so the comment says to keep the two in sync). `hydra/hydra-gridmodel.js` keeps its bounded 60x60 copy on purpose (the editor renders exactly 3600 cells; the comment now says so explicitly), and `script.js` keeps its `getAdjacentCells` DOM helper (editor visuals only, correctly bounded). `hydra-flow.js` was already inlined into exports, so part 3 of the item needed no new wiring — the golden test pins that the built export contains exactly one `adjacentIndexes` definition.
- **A second divergence closed along the way:** the preview's old copy ran neighbours up/down/left/right while the dispatch runs reading order (right/down/left/up), so the direct path (gameStart fallback) and the dispatch disagreed on fan-out order. Both now run reading order. The observable consequence: two adjacent actions writing the same variable end with the below action's value in both runtimes (the golden test pins fanOrder = 2). The `and`/`or` context merge inherits the same order in both runtimes, since it reads the same canonical function through the hosts' `adjacentIndexes` hook.

- **Slice c — iteration over structures COMPLETED (P5-6 is now COMPLETE).** One new block in the Control Flow category (528 blocks total), after For Each Object. **For Each Field** runs the body once per field of a structure — the dictionary the structure blocks build — handing the body the field name as text (key variable), the field's value (value variable), and a 1-based index when named. The fields are snapshotted at the start (`normalizeForEachFields` in `hydra/hydra-loops.js`: first 1,000 key/value pairs in insertion order, warning naming the real total), so a body that adds or removes a field cannot change the run; a blank structure name or a variable holding no structure warns and holds (a list is not a structure — walk a list with For Each Item); an empty structure runs nothing and stays quiet. When a field's value is an object handle — the kind Store Object In Structure writes — the body round also points the execution context at that live object, the same narrowing For Each Item uses, so a squad stored as a structure is walked one member at a time with Damage Object hitting each member exactly once; a stale handle narrows nothing, and a host without the hook warns once and runs the body anyway. The dispatch lives in `hydra-execute.js` (`runForEachField`, next to the other for-each runners); the flow and dispatch route through `isLoopBlock` generically, so no runtime wiring changed. Docs: block reference Control Flow (8) to (9) with the new block detailed, user-guide Loops paragraph extended and the "iteration over structure fields is the next step" honest-limits line replaced with the finished capability.

The key is read from the loop variable itself, pinned that way in the unit and export tests. (2) The handle-damage grid built the squad with varFromJson, which overwrote the seeded handles with an empty dict (the seed now stands alone). A third finding, docs-side: the keys-list manual path (List Structure Keys → For Each Item → Get Structure Field per key) cannot read values, because Get Structure Field takes a fixed field name — the block reference now says plainly that the keys-list path walks names only, instead of claiming the manual path reads values.

- **Slice b — object references inside structures COMPLETED.** Two new blocks in the Variables category (527 blocks total), after Set Nested Value. **Store Object In Structure** writes the handle a scoped variable holds at a nested path (`squad[0]`, `party.leader`), building the containers it needs on the way down like Set Nested Value (a list step grows the list with 0s up to the 10,000 cap, a field step creates the dictionary, a plain root is replaced); a variable holding no object stores the empty handle, so a later Is alive read is 0. **Read Object From Structure** reads the handle at a path back into a scoped object variable, and points the execution context at the live object like the pick, so chained actions hit that one object; a missing step or a value that is not an object clears the variable to the empty handle, quiet. Both dispatch in the `isObjectRefAction` branch of `hydra/hydra-execute.js` (now naming eight types), reusing the collections path helpers through `ctx.collections` (present in both runtimes, no wiring changes) and the existing scoped-variable read/write; no new shared module. A blank variable name, blank path, blank object variable, or object scope with no object warns and holds; an index past the cap warns and changes nothing; no scoped-variable state or no collections module stays quiet. The manual paths still work and agree exactly, proven in export-boot Phase CQ: Read Scoped Variable into a flat variable + Set Nested Value with Copy of another variable stores the same clone, and Get Nested Value into a flat variable + Read Object Property (its flat-variable fallback) reads X 600 from it. The per-item test lives in the damage amount because a condition cannot sit inside a loop body (the body's rightward scan stops at a condition). **Shot rebuild** (five rows): gameStart destroys the fireball template first, then Spawn Object Into Variable hands the clone back in `shot` and Reset Timer starts its lifetime; an always row keeps the shot's alive flag in `shotAlive`; the flight row (gated on `shotAlive` == 1) runs Pick Nearest fireball near the hero, then Move Toward Position toward a far point at speed 5; the hit row reads the shot's X/Y, lists the overlap circle riding on the shot, and loops the list with an alive re-read inside the body (the canned one-hit rule); a Timer Elapsed row destroys the shot when its lifetime ends. Two real defects caught in-cycle by the export test before any docs shipped. **(1) The speed units:** the first version flew the shot at speed 300 — 300 px per run, not per second — so it leapt over the goblins and never hit; a frame-by-frame probe (`co-probe.js` in the workflow workdir) showed the clone jumping 300 px a run and the template starting to move once the clone outran it. Speed 5 is the correct 300 px/s (the speed field counts pixels per frame; `dt` is in 60fps frame units). **(2) The template drift:** after the shot dies, the template is the only live fireball left, so the level-triggered Pick Nearest keeps firing and flies the template — the probe showed exactly 5 px a frame for exactly the frames after the kill (64.9 px over 13 frames in the hit check, 45 px over 9 in the lifetime check). The gate row (getVariable on `shotAlive`) cannot stop it, because flow edges target actions only (`isUsable` in `hydra-flow.js`) and conditions are independent event sources — a condition never gates another condition. The fix is to destroy the template in the gameStart row before spawning: the spawn clones from the scene's definitions (`sceneObjectDefs` returns `scene.objects`, a separate array from the live `state.objects`), which destroying the live template does not touch, so later spawns still work and the clone is the only live fireball. All 9 Phase CO checks pass with zero diagnostics (melee arc/range + both-iterations proof, shot flight + one-hit + template-destroyed-unmoved, lifetime kill + still-flying-at-10-frames). Browser-smoke section pins the Combat palette order and both panels (no live preview — the chains are proven by Phase CO, and no UI changed, so no screenshots apply to this slice). Honest limits in the guide: Attack Ready reads the combat module's cooldowns (rebuilds pace themselves with timers); growth/scaling bonuses do not ride along (read and add them); one shot at a time (a second shot overwrites `shot` and strands the first — a list of handles is P5-6). **P5-5 is COMPLETE.** Next: P5-6 data structures.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 13 (improve): P5-5 slice c — Spawn/destroy as instantiation COMPLETED

- P5-5 continues with **slice c — spawn/destroy as instantiation**. Two new blocks in the Variables category (523 blocks total, was 521), placed after List Overlapping Objects. **Spawn Object Into Variable** clones the named object at X/Y (blank coordinates fall back to the source position, the same rule as Spawn Object) and stores the clone's handle in a scoped object variable, pointing the execution context at the clone (like Pick), so chained actions that target the object's name hit only the clone. A missing source clears the variable to the empty handle, so a later Is alive read is 0, and stays quiet. **Destroy Object In Variable** destroys exactly the object a variable holds, the clone a spawn handed back or the goblin a pick chose, not every object with that name; a missing, empty, or stale variable stays quiet. A blank object or variable name warns and holds; an object scope with no object warns and holds; no scoped-variable state stays quiet and spawns nothing (the family convention). No new shared module was needed: the dispatch lives in the `isObjectRefAction` branch of `hydra/hydra-execute.js` (now naming six types), reusing the existing `spawnClone` (which returns the last clone made) and `ctx.pendingDestroy`; both runtimes' `actionCtx` already provide `varScopes`, `setExecutionContext`, `sceneObjectDefs`, and `pendingDestroy`, so no runtime wiring changed. Destroyed objects stay in `state.objects` but are flagged in `state.destroyed`, so tests assert on that set, not on removal. ## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 12 (improve): P5-5 slice b — Physics queries that return data COMPLETED

- P5-5 continues with **slice b — physics queries that return data**. Two new blocks in the Variables category (521 blocks total, was 519), placed after Read Object Property. **Raycast Into Variables** casts a ray From (fromX, fromY) to (toX, toY) against live collision objects, layer-mask filtered (the same ray as the Raycast Hit condition). On a hit it stores the object handle in a scoped object variable, the distance in a flat variable, and points the execution context at the hit object (like Pick). On a miss it writes distance -1 and clears the object variable to the empty handle (so a later Is alive read is 0), and stays quiet. A blank object var warns and holds; a blank distance var only skips the distance store; bad coords warn and hold; no physics module stays fully quiet and writes nothing (a miss is only reported when the ray was actually cast). **List Overlapping Objects** runs an overlap circle at (x, y) with radius r, layer-mask filtered (the same query as the Objects Overlapping condition). It stores a fresh array of object handles in a flat list variable (replaced every run, never appended) and the count in a flat var. An empty overlap stores [] and 0, quiet. A blank list var warns and holds; a blank count var only skips the count; bad coords/radius warn and hold; a negative radius reads as 0; no physics module stays fully quiet and writes nothing. Two composition bridges make the overlap list usable: **Read Object Property** falls back to a flat variable holding a handle when no scoped object entry is found (a scoped object always wins over a flat one of the same name), which is how a For Each Item body reads the current item (the item variable is flat); and **For Each Item** narrows the execution context to the current item when it is an object handle (`setExecutionContext({obj1})` for that body run, restored after, mirroring For Each Object), so a loop over the overlap list can damage each goblin exactly once. No new shared module was needed: the dispatch lives in the `isObjectRefAction` branch of `hydra/hydra-execute.js` (now naming four types), reusing `ctx.physics2d.raycast` / `overlapCircle`, `ctx.allObjects` + `ctx.isCollisionObject` (the same filter as the conditions), and `ctx.named` / `ctx.setExecutionContext`; both runtimes' `actionCtx` already provide all of these, so no runtime wiring changed. Docs: block reference Variables (35) to (37) with both blocks detailed, user-guide Object references paragraph extended with both query blocks and the For Each Item composition.
- Tests: new `tests/physicsqueries.test.js` (37 checks: module surface, execute dispatch through the real `HydraPhysics2D` module, dispatch integration through `HydraDispatch` + `HydraFlow`, catalog/wiring/docs pins). Export-boot Phase CM, 11 checks (raycast to damage to health read, wall hit + layer-1 ray, miss writes -1 / alive 0, overlap to For Each Item damages each goblin exactly once, empty overlap, blank raycast warns once). Browser-smoke section: live preview with a 7-cell chain (raycast, read prop, copy to scoped, overlap, copy count), palette-order and panel-field pins. Visual QA: 5 Playwright screenshots inspected (palette, both panels, chain, preview), all clean; the preview readout shows hit X 80, distance 80, overlap count 1. Full suite all green (9750 checks, 0 failures). One test-grid lesson, not an engine bug: an earlier smoke section flips the player's collision shape to circle, so a horizontal ray at y=120 grazes the circle at distance 87.5 instead of the box edge at 80 — the smoke ray now runs straight down x=103 through the player's center, where box and circle agree exactly, and the distance assertion uses a 0.01 tolerance for the float math. ## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 11 (improve): P5-5 slice a — Object references as values COMPLETED

- P5-5 is now started with **slice a — object references as values**. A sixth scoped-variable type, 'object': a variable holds a handle ({ name, id }) to one live object, kept by name and id and never by live reference, so it survives save/load and JSON. Two blocks in the Variables category (519 blocks total), placed after Scoped Variable Check. **Pick Object Into Variable** picks one live object — nearest, farthest, random, or first — from a Near object or an X/Y point, and stores it in a scoped variable. **Read Object Property** reads one property of the held object back into an ordinary variable: X, Y, width, height, rotation, velocity X/Y, health, max health, name, or alive as 1/0. The pick also points the execution context at the picked object, so chained actions that target the object's name hit only that one object — the dispatch integration proves it (pick nearest goblin, Damage Object 10, only that goblin's health moves). An id-bearing handle tracks that exact object and never retargets another with the same name when it goes stale; a by-name handle (empty id) takes the first live object with the name. Stale or missing reads fall back to 0 (empty text for Name) and stay quiet; a blank pick or a blank object-variable name warns and holds. The Variables manager and the Preview Live variables readout show a held object by name (object (goblin) / object (none)). Damage as a plain numeric operation already exists (Damage Object, P4 batch 21), so no new block was needed for it. New shared module `hydra/hydra-pick.js` (`HydraPick`: PICK_MODES, normalizePickMode, pickObject); `hydra/hydra-variables.js` gains the object type plus `isObjectRef`, `emptyObjectRef`, `objectRefOf`, `resolveObjectRef`, `isObjectRefAction`; `hydra/hydra-execute.js` dispatches the two actions right after the scoped-variable branch; `player-runtime.js` and `hydra-previewruntime.js` wire the pick module and hooks into `actionCtx`; `script.js` formats object values and treats them read-only in the Variables manager like list/dict. Docs: block reference Variables (35) with both blocks detailed, user-guide Object references paragraph after Scoped variables. Honest limits, in the module headers and the guide: a handle to a spawned clone goes stale on destroy or scene switch; a For Each Object body still cannot read the current object's position into a variable directly (the body reaches the object only through action targeting, and Read Object Property reads from a variable, not from the execution context).
- Two real defects caught in-cycle by the visual QA, both in the property panels, both fixed before commit. **(1) An unset object dropdown borrowed the first object's name.** `makeObjectSelect` built its options from the scene's object names only, with no blank option, so a blank stored value displayed as 'player' while storing ''. Fixed in `hydra/hydra-propinputs.js`: the select now leads with a blank option — '(none)' when the scene has objects, '(No named objects yet)' when it has none, '-- Any --' unchanged for includeAll. Pins updated in `tests/propinputs.test.js`, including a new pin that a blank value shows (none) instead of the first object. **(2) A fresh Pick block still showed Scope object 'player' after fix (1) — a different root cause.** `HydraGridModel.buildBlockDefaults` fills any empty `targetObject` with the first object name at placement, which is the intended convenience for primary-target blocks but wrong for scoped-variable blocks, where `targetObject` is the SCOPE object: a Global-scope block was silently bound to 'player', and switching its scope to Object later would have bound it to 'player' without ever warning. Fixed in `hydra/hydra-gridmodel.js`: `targetObject` is only filled when the block has no `scope` field, or its scope is Object. That changes the same behavior for all eight scoped-variable blocks (the six originals plus the two new ones); primary-target blocks are untouched and the existing set-text fill pin still passes. Pins added in `tests/gridmodel.test.js` for both scope cases.
- Two test-side defects fixed in-cycle. **(1)** The QA script's first preview screenshot caught the Live variables panel collapsed (its toggle starts hidden), so the readout values could not be seen in the shot — the script now opens the toggle before the shot, and the final shot shows the open readout. **(2)** `tests/variables.test.js` pinned the block-reference heading as '### Variables (33)'; the cycle's docs edit moved it to (35), so the first full-suite run failed on that one pin alone. Pin updated to (35); the second full-suite run is all green.
- Visual QA (cycle 11, script `objectrefs-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir, 5 screenshots opened and inspected): the Variables palette shows Pick Object Into Variable and Read Object Property right after Scoped Variable Check with purple chips and full labels; the placed 9-cell chain reads gameStart, Pick Obj, Obj Prop, Set Var, Damage, Obj Prop, Set Var, Obj Prop, Set Var with labels intact; the Pick panel shows Object hazard, Pick Nearest, Near object player, X/Y 0, Into variable orTarget, Scope Global (whole game), Scope object (none); the Read panel shows Object variable orTarget, Scope Global, Scope object (none), Property X position, Into variable orX; the preview runs with 0 diagnostics and the open Live variables readout shows orAliveLive 1, orHpLive 75, orTarget OBJECT object (hazard), orXLive 610. No defects remain. Known pre-existing quirk noticed while inspecting the preview shot, not fixed this cycle: the Live variables header count counts definitions only, so it reads 0 while live-only rows are shown below it — a candidate for a later cycle.
