## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 10 (improve): P5-4 slice c — Custom events (Broadcast / Listen) COMPLETED

- P5-4 is now complete: this cycle delivers **slice c — custom events**. Two blocks in the Control Flow category (517 blocks total), placed after For Each Object. **Broadcast Event** is an action that takes a plain-text event name and announces it; **On Event** is a condition that is true when that named event was broadcast. The host-owned run state is `{ counts, seen }` in the new shared module `hydra/hydra-events.js` (`HydraEvents`, the factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-loops.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `counts` maps each name to how many times it was broadcast this run, `seen` maps each listener cell (`scopeKey:cell`) to the count it last observed. State is created fresh on a fresh run and survives scene switches inside a run, like the loop state. A broadcast bumps the count by one; a poll fires exactly once per broadcast and records the count it saw, so several broadcasts of one name in one frame still fire the listener once, and a broadcast every frame fires the listener every frame. Delivery timing follows the grid walk: a listener after the broadcaster in the grid fires in the same frame, a listener before it fires on the next frame — either way exactly once per broadcast. A blank Broadcast Event name warns and holds; a listener for a never-broadcast name stays quiet with no warning (a misspelled name looks exactly like an event that never happens); with no event state at all the blocks stay quiet. Honest limits, documented in the module header, the user guide, the block reference, and the CHANGELOG: an event carries no value with it — set a variable first when the listener needs a number or a name; the event name is plain text, not an expression, so it cannot be built from a variable at run time; no `hs` script-API surface yet (use the blocks).
- One real defect caught in-cycle, by the unit test before any code shipped: **On Event must be level-triggered, not edge.** The first version put it on the edge list in `hydra-flow.js` `defaultTriggerMode`, and the "broadcast every frame fires the listener every frame" dispatch check failed — edge collapses consecutive per-frame broadcasts (true on consecutive frames) into a single firing. The poll is true for exactly one evaluation per broadcast, so level fires once per broadcast and never repeats. On Event now sits on the level list with a comment saying why.
- Two test-side defects fixed in-cycle, both in the smoke test. **(1)** The new events section placed an `always` block, but the Always condition's blockType is `always-condition` — the click waited 30s for a palette option that does not exist, and the first full-suite run failed on it. **(2)** The final events check read the tick count with `Number(evLive.tick.trim().slice(-6))`, a string slice that is wrong for short numbers — it now parses the trailing number with a regex. The re-run also survived a VM reboot mid-run (the machine restarted under the background smoke job, killing it silently with no failure line in the log); the smoke test was re-run after the reboot and passed clean.
- Visual QA (cycle 10, script `events-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir, 5 screenshots opened and inspected): the Control Flow category shows Repeat, While, Break, Continue, For Each Item, For Each Object, Broadcast Event, On Event in order with their purple chips and full labels; the Broadcast Event panel shows the Event name field holding "qaGoal"; the On Event panel shows the Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level explainer and the Event name field holding "qaGoal"; the placed chains read gameStart, Broadcast / On Event, Set Var, Set Var with labels intact; the preview runs at 60 FPS with 0 diagnostics, the collector scene rendering clean behind it. No defects found. One apparent anomaly was chased down and cleared: in the full shots the placed chains sit one column left of the template's chains — a geometry probe showed the grid is uniform (column N at x = N*56, cell 600 at x=0, cell 601 at x=56) and the collector template simply starts its own chains at column 1, so the offset is the template's layout, not a rendering bug. The QA script's only failed check is environmental: four `ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED` console errors from the Font Awesome CDN failing to load in the sandbox (the smoke test's own header notes the CDN cannot be reliably reached there); zero page errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: Diagnostics dock overlaps the bottom of long tab panels; the "Desktop" device pill is clipped at the right edge; inspector tab labels truncate.
- Tests: new `tests/events.test.js` 49/49 (module API, condition + execute integration, dispatch integration incl. same-frame / next-frame / dedupe / every-frame / two-listener / blank-name / name-isolation cases, flow trigger-mode pin, catalog + wiring + docs pins); `tests/export-boot.test.js` 1670/1670 (was 1662, +8: Phase CK — same-frame delivery, next-frame delivery, every-frame broadcast, blank-name warning + name isolation); `tests/loops.test.js` 90/90, `tests/catalog.test.js` 73/73, `tests/functions.test.js` 89/89 (count pins moved to 517); browser-smoke 3 new checks (both panels, and the live preview: one-shot listener counts once and stays at 1, heartbeat ticks at least 3 times). Full suite green before commit (log `full-suite-36.log` in the workflow workdir; the first full run failed only on the `always` blockType mistake above, fixed test-side, and the second full run went all green end to end).

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 9 (improve): P5-4 slice b — For Each Item / For Each Object

- P5-4 continues with **slice b — for-each**: two blocks in the Control Flow category (515 blocks total), sharing the loop body rule, caps, budget, and Break / Continue with Repeat and While. **For Each Item** runs the rightward body once per item of a flat list variable — the list the array blocks build — with the item variable holding the item each round and an optional 1-based index variable; the list is snapshotted at the start (`HydraLoops.normalizeForEachItems`, cap 1000 with a warning naming the real length), so a body that pushes or pops cannot change the run. A blank list name or a variable that holds no list warns and holds (a dict is not a list); an empty list runs nothing and stays quiet. **For Each Object** runs the body once per live object with a name, in live order, snapshotted at the start (cap 1000 with a warning); each round hands the object to the body through the execution context (`{ obj1: the object }`, the same narrowing pick conditions use), so body actions that target the object's name hit that one object — Destroy Object in the body destroys a different coin each round — and the context is restored after every round, so the loop never leaks its last object into the chain after it. The index variable counts the objects the same way. No item variable: object references as values are P5-5, so the body reaches the object only through action targeting, and the body cannot read the object's position into a variable yet. Both hosts carry the new `setExecutionContext` hook on their action contexts (export sets `state.executionContext`, preview sets `run.executionContext`); a host without it warns once and runs the body anyway, without the narrowing. The stray Break / Continue warning now reads "only works inside a loop" instead of naming Repeat / While.
- One real defect caught in-cycle, in the test grid rather than the engine: the export test for the two warnings put For Each Item and For Each Object on the same row, so the second block was the first loop's body and a failed loop never ran it — only one warning fired. Each loop now sits on its own row with its own trigger. The same row rule also explains why the fix was test-side: the engine behaved exactly as documented.
- Two stale pins fixed in-cycle (test-side, found by the full-suite sweep): the cycle-8 smoke check for the Control Flow palette still expected exactly four blocks, and `tests/catalog.test.js` / `tests/functions.test.js` still pinned 513 blocks — all three now expect six blocks / 515.
- Visual QA (cycle 9, script `foreach-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir, 5 screenshots opened and inspected): the Control Flow category shows Repeat, While, Break, Continue, For Each Item, For Each Object in order with their purple chips and full labels; the For Each Item panel shows List variable feDeck, Item variable feCard, Index variable (optional) feN; the For Each Object panel shows Object coin and Index variable (optional) feObjN; the placed chains read gameStart, Push, Push, Push, For Each, Set Var, Set Var / gameStart, Repeat, Spawn / gameStart, Each Obj, Set Var, Destroy, Set Var with labels intact; the preview runs at 41 FPS with 0 diagnostics, the coin gone from the canvas (the object loop destroyed all three), Score 0 and the hint text clean. No defects found. The QA script's only failed check is environmental: three `ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED` console errors from the Font Awesome CDN failing to load in the sandbox (the smoke test's own header notes the CDN cannot be reliably reached there); zero page errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: Diagnostics dock overlaps the bottom of long tab panels; the "Desktop" device pill is clipped at the right edge; inspector tab labels truncate.

Full suite green before commit (log `full-suite-34b.log` in the workflow workdir; the first full run reported gltf.test.js as a failure, but it was the runner's 120s per-file timeout firing under load — a standalone re-run passes clean, and the second full run went all green end to end).

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 8 (improve): P5-4 slice a — Repeat / While / Break / Continue loops

- P5-4 starts with **slice a — loops**: four blocks in a new **Control Flow** palette category, placed right after Functions. **Repeat** runs the blocks to its right on the same row a set number of times (an expression, default 3) with an optional 1-based counter variable the body reads like any other variable; **While** runs the same rightward body while a two-value comparison holds (the six operators, same as the conditions module); **Break** stops the innermost loop at once and **Continue** skips to the next round, both warning and doing nothing outside a loop. The loop body is the rightward same-row run after the loop block — a condition, an empty cell, or the row end stops it, and actions below the block are not the body. Safety caps, all synchronous in one frame: Repeat clamps to 1000, While stops after 1000 with a warning, nesting caps at 8 deep, and a 10,000 body-action budget per frame. The counter keeps its last value after the loop. The shared module is `hydra/hydra-loops.js` (`HydraLoops`: caps, compare, loop state, budget); the flow module routes loop blocks through an `executeLoop` hook, and both the editor preview and exported games run the same shared `hydra-execute.js` loop code. The user guide and block reference document the placement rule, the counter, the While pattern, the caps, and the do-not-put-a-condition-right-after-the-body warning (legacy adjacency re-fires the body's last action once).
- Three real defects fixed in-cycle. **(1) The loop-ownership return.** `executeLoopAction` returns whether the loop took ownership of its body, and `propagate` pulls the body out of the outer queue only when it did — before the fix, a loop skipped by the 8-deep cap still pulled its body, so the body never ran at all. The return had to be threaded through every host layer and was missed in three places before the tests caught it: the export's `executeLoop` function and dispatch hook, the preview's `executeLoopAction`, and the node harness hook. **(2) The gameStart launch path never looped in the preview.** The preview fires gameStart through its own `processGameStartConditions`, which ran each adjacent action through plain `executeAction` — where a loop block is a quiet no-op and its body ran once — while exported games fire gameStart through the dispatch's `propagate`, where the loop branch works. The smoke probe caught it: the loop counter reached 3 but the sum never moved and the body warned about unknown names. The fix routes the launch through `Flow.propagate` with the dispatch-built API (`fireGameStart` in `hydra-previewruntime.js`), resetting the loop budget first since the launch runs outside the frame phase, and falling back to the direct path for a gameStart with an eventId (custom events, a later slice) or a missing Flow/Dispatch module. **(3) The raw hooks are not a propagate API.** The first version of that fix passed `dispatchHooks(...)` straight to `propagate`, but propagate calls `api.executeAction` / `api.evaluateCondition`, which only the dispatch-built API provides — the raw hooks carry `execute` / `evaluate` instead, so the body propagate threw inside the loop (swallowed by the budget wrapper) and nothing ran while the counter still reached 3. The fix extracts `HydraDispatch.buildPropagateApi(hooks, scopeKey, grid)`, used by both `processScope` and the launch path, so the two can never drift again; `tests/dispatch.test.js` pins it (a Repeat chain through the built API takes the loop branch and runs its body). The same run also fixed that test's stale player-runtime delegation regex, which the cycle-8 budget-reset lines in `processScope` had broken.
- Visual QA (cycle 8, scripts `loops-smoke-probe.js` and `loops-visual-qa.js` / `loops-visual-qa2.js` in the workflow workdir, all screenshots opened and inspected): the Control Flow category shows Repeat, While, Break, Continue in order with their purple chips and full labels; the placed chain reads gameStart, Repeat, Set Variable, Set Scoped Variable left to right with labels intact; the Repeat panel shows Times 3 with the fx badge and Counter variable loopProbeI; the preview's Live variables readout shows loopProbeLive NUMBER Global 6 with the live dot, the collector game running behind it at 38 FPS with 0 diagnostics. No defects found. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: Diagnostics dock overlaps the bottom of long tab panels; the "Desktop" device pill is clipped at the right edge; inspector tab labels truncate.
- Tests: `tests/loops.test.js` 62/62 (loop semantics, caps, nesting, budget arithmetic — the nested 1000x1000 budget totals 9992, nine nested Repeat-2 loops total 256); `tests/export-boot.test.js` 1655/1655 (Phase CI: Repeat sums 1+2+3+4+5 to 15, While counts to 5, Repeat 1500 clamps to 1000 with a warning, an unchanging While stops at 1000 with a warning, a stray Break warns and the chain continues); `tests/dispatch.test.js` green (buildPropagateApi surface + loop-branch contract + the fixed player-runtime delegation regex); browser-smoke 17 new checks (module loaded with caps, palette category and order, the four blocks place at exact cells, all four panels, and the live preview sum 1+2+3 to 6). Full suite green before commit.
- **Next capability (not yet implemented):** P5-4 continues with **slice b — for-each over a list and over objects**, then **slice c — custom events (broadcast / listen)**.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 7 (improve): P5-3 slice c — Functions manager/body-editing polish COMPLETED

- P5-3 is now complete: this cycle delivers **slice c — manager/body-editing polish**, the last piece of functions / custom blocks. Three small, visible improvements in the Functions manager, all in the Scene inspector's Functions tab. **(1) Duplicate function.** Every row has a Duplicate button (`HydraFunctions.duplicateFunction` in `hydra/hydra-functions.js`): same parameters, a deep copy of the body grid (JSON round-trip, the codebase's usual deep-copy), a fresh minted id, and a non-colliding name — "add copy", then "add copy 2", "add copy 3" when taken — so a variant starts from a working chain instead of an empty grid; the copy gets its own `ƒ <name>` scope tab through the existing `refreshScopeTabs`. **(2) Call count on every row.** The note under each function now reads "7 blocks in the body. Called once." / "Empty body — open it and add blocks. Not called yet.", reusing the stored-grid `countFunctionReferences` the delete confirm already uses, so a dead helper and a workhorse read differently at a glance. **(3) Dropped-parameter reporting.** `normalizeParamsReport` is `normalizeParams` with a list of what it dropped (duplicate names, names past the 8-parameter cap; blanks and non-strings still drop silently, they were never names), and the panel turns the report into one plain line via `fnParamsDropNote` — 'Kept x, y. Dropped duplicate "x".' / 'Kept a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h. Dropped "i", "j" past the 8-parameter cap.' — shown as `#fn-params-note` under the add disclosure after an add or a row parameter edit, instead of trimming the list silently; a clean edit clears the note, and rename/duplicate/delete clear it too. The row parameter edit re-renders the panel to show the note (safe: `change` fires on blur). A unit test pins `normalizeParamsReport` and `normalizeParams` to agree on the kept list for the same input, so the two cannot drift.

Dropped duplicate "x".' — clean; (4) row edit to ten parameters — keeps the first eight, note reads 'Kept a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h. Dropped "i", "j" past the 8-parameter cap.', input shows the kept eight — clean, the note wraps to two lines without overflow. Zero page errors; no console errors. ## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 6 (improve): P5-3 slice b — dynamic argument editor + 8-param cap + function-grid validation COMPLETED

- P5-3 slice b delivers **the dynamic argument editor, the raised parameter cap (4 → 8), and function-grid validation coverage**. Functions take up to eight parameters: `MAX_PARAMS` is 8 in `hydra/hydra-functions.js` (`normalizeParams` caps at eight and drops the ninth), the Call Function block declares arg1..arg8 in the catalog (defaults included, so the catalog stays the source of truth for expression validation), and `hydra-execute.js` reads all eight argument slots (blank still ends the list). The property panel for a Call Function block is built by a new `showCallFunctionProperties` in `script.js` (called from `showCatalogBlockProperties` for `blockType === 'call-function'`): the Function picker, then exactly one argument field per parameter of the picked function, labeled with the parameter name — a call to sum8(a..h) shows fields a, b, c, d, e, f, g, h with the seeded values 1–8, not Argument 1–8. Picking a different function rebuilds the panel (the picker's change listener re-renders after the stored name updates); values already typed into arg1..arg8 stay on the block and reappear when a function with that many parameters is picked; a parameter-less function shows a note instead of fields, and a blank or missing name says so plainly (bare `.prop-note` paragraphs, not `makeNote`, which hides behind the property-help flag). The Functions manager placeholders now read "up to 8", and the panel hint in `index.html` reads "up to eight parameters".
- **Function grids are covered by block validation now.** `collectProjectDiagnostics` in `hydra-universal.js` scans every function body grid like any other grid (scope label "Function <name>"; a body runs in every scene's context, so it sees the union of object names, like the global grid), and adds two function-specific checks: a Call Function naming a function that does not exist is a warning (a blank name is an info finding), and a Return From Function outside a function body is a warning. `refreshValidationDiagnostics` in `script.js` now passes the live function list (`getMutableFunctions()`) into `runBlockValidation` — without that, the editor's validation calls carried only scenes + global grid and the function checks never ran.
- **Two real defects fixed in-cycle, both caught by the visual QA.** (1) The Functions panel hint text still said "up to four parameters" (index.html line 431 — the one "four parameters" the grep sweep missed because it only covered .js/.md at first). (2) The sum8 manager row's name vanished: `.var-top` is a no-wrap flex row, the 8-param badge is `flex-shrink: 0`, and the name button is `flex: 1; min-width: 0`, so the badge squeezed the name to zero width. Function rows now carry an `fn-row` class with wrapping (`.var-row.fn-row .var-top { flex-wrap: wrap }`), a 48px name floor, a badge capped at 180px with ellipsis + full list on hover (title), and a narrower 72px params input so short rows like add(a, b) still fit on one line with Edit body and delete. The first draft used bare `.fn-row` selectors and lost to the later equal-specificity `.var-row` rules (the × still wrapped alone on the add row), so the selectors carry `.var-row` too — pinned by a test. The re-shot manager shows sum8 with its name, full badge, and Edit body/× on the second line, and add on one clean line.
- **QA-script lessons (script-side, not engine).** (1) A Return value expression reads flat variables, not locals — the first QA seed body returned `a + b + ...` directly and the runtime warned "value is not a number" every run; the seed now follows the Phase CH pattern (read each local into a flat var, sum into a flat var, Return the flat var). (2) The Live variables readout shows scoped variables only, so a flat `total` never appears there — the seed adds a Set Scoped Variable block copying flat total into scoped total, and the readout shows "total NUMBER Global 36". (3) Mutating the stored grid directly (`window.globalGridData[2]`) gets clobbered by the next commit from the live cell map (autosave/commitActiveGrid rewrites the store from cellData) — the validation probe now mutates `cellData` and calls `commitActiveGrid()`. (4) `playwright-core` does not resolve from the workflow workdir (the cycle-28 lesson): the QA script requires it by absolute path from the repo's node_modules.

## 2026-11-07 — Cycle 5 (improve): P5-3 slice a — Functions runtime core COMPLETED

- P5-3 slice a delivers **custom functions**: define a reusable block chain once in the Functions manager (name, up to four parameters, a body grid like any other scope) and call it from any row with Call Function. New shared module `hydra/hydra-functions.js` (`HydraFunctions`, the factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after hydra-variables.js): definitions (`project.functions`, `{id, name, params, grid}`, stable ids minted as `fn_` + 8 base36 chars, names unique first-wins), a transient call stack with a 32-deep recursion guard (the 33rd call is skipped with a warning and returns 0), scope keys `function:<id>`, and identity-preserving normalization (clean lists pass through untouched, so the autosave cannot orphan the panel's array — the same race fixed for definitions in cycle 26). Parameters arrive as **local variables**, the fourth scoped-variable scope: `hydra-variables.js` gains `local` (`createVariablesState` now carries `{global, scene, object, local}`, `bindLocalScope` / `restoreLocalScope` swap the map per call frame, definitions with a `local` scope coerce to `global` rather than vanishing), and the six scoped-variable blocks all offer `Local (this function only)` in their Scope select. Body grids live under scope key `function:<id>` in `hydra-gridmodel.js` (`normalizeScriptScope` passes them through, `getGridDataForScope` / `saveGridToScope` read and write `fn.grid`). Two blocks in the new Functions category (Call Function — four expression arguments, return value into an optional variable — and Return From Function, 509 blocks total), wired through `hydra-execute.js` (blank name warns and holds, bad argument warns and holds, missing function and depth-cap warnings) and both runtimes: the export's `callFunction` binds the frame's locals, saves and restores the execution context, runs the body grid through `processScope` with Return gating in `dispatchHooks`, and pops the frame; the preview runtime mirrors it (`callPreviewFunction` / `returnPreviewFunction`, call state reset on `resetGame`). A body never runs on its own — only via Call Function, never the per-frame script pass — and a gameStart block inside a body fires only on the first call of the run (edge memory and started scopes are keyed by function scope). The Functions manager panel (Scene inspector, Functions tab, white cards like the Variables panel, add form behind a disclosure) offers create / rename (rewrites every Call Function block naming the old name, stored grids and the live cell map) / editable parameters / Edit body (opens the `ƒ <name>` scope tab) / delete (confirms when calls still name it, falls back to the global grid when its own body was open). Return with no Return in the body returns 0; Return outside a function warns and does nothing.
- **Two real bugs fixed in-cycle.** (1) `saveGridToScope` treated the string `'global'` as a function scope (its branch tested `typeof normalizedScope === 'string'` without excluding `'global'`), so every global save silently returned false and wrote nothing — caught by gridmodel.test.js, fixed to mirror `getGridDataForScope`'s global-first check. (2) The test-fallout script that pins the new module into the export lists (`work/functions-fallout.py`) was not idempotent on its first version — it re-inserted its lines after every variables line on re-run — fixed by peeking at the next line before inserting, then the four test files were restored from git and the fixed script re-run (second run +0 everywhere). Known limit recorded for later: function grids are not covered by `runBlockValidation` yet (it only scans scenes + globalGridData); arguments are expressions, so parameters always arrive as numbers; four parameters at most.
- Visual QA (cycle 5, script `functions-visual-qa.js`, 10 checks all green, 6 screenshots opened and inspected): (1) Functions manager with the add form open (name + params + Add button, hint text, empty note — clean); (2) manager row — **one real defect caught: the parameter badge read "(A, B)" for lowercase params `a, b`, because the row reused the `.var-type` class whose `text-transform: uppercase` is meant for type names like NUMBER** — fixed with a dedicated `.fn-params` class (same look, no transform), pinned by a test, and the re-shot row reads "(a, b)"; (3) Logic tab palette with the FUNCTIONS category (Call Function, Return From Function, teal swatches, `ƒ add` scope tab visible — clean); (4) Call Function property panel (Function "add (a, b)", Arguments 1–4, Into variable "result" — clean; the first draft of this shot caught the panel after back-to-blocks, showing the block picker instead, so the shot was moved before the click); (5) function body grid (`ƒ add` active, scope note "Function body: add — only runs when Call Function names it", always → Read Var → Read Var → Return chained — clean); (6) preview run with the Live variables panel open showing "result NUMBER Global 7" at 60 FPS, 0 diagnostics (the first draft caught the panel collapsed, so the script now opens its toggle before the shot). Zero page errors; no console errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: inspector tab labels truncate; Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge.
- Tests: new `tests/functions.test.js` 74/74 (module API incl. the 32-deep cap and 4-param cap, call stack, local scope incl. bind/restore, gridmodel function scopes, execute dispatch incl. warn-and-hold paths, conditions reading locals through the module and the raw fallback, catalog + wiring + docs pins); `tests/variables.test.js` 77/77 (shape pin now carries `local`, local-scope + bind/restore + coercion pins); `tests/gridmodel.test.js` 106/106 (function-scope pins, incl. the global-save regression); `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 620/620 (Functions wiring: actionCtx hooks, call-state reset incl. on resetGame, projectFunctions from the host); `tests/catalog.test.js` (509 blocks, twelve controls incl. `function`); `tests/core.test.js` (blank project `functions: []`, definitions survive `createProject`); export-boot Phase CH 6 check groups, 17 checks (add(3,4) → 7, add(10,20) → 30, re-seeded third call 123, body gameStart firing once, Return stopping the body, locals not leaking, uncalled body never running, recursion guard warning once and unwinding with 1, missing-function and outside-Return warnings, inlining + missing-module guard); browser-smoke 17 new checks (module loaded, palette category + order, both blocks placing with working panels, manager add / duplicate refusal / rename rewriting the call / Edit body scope tab / delete with cleanup). Full suite green before commit: 182 PASS lines, 0 NOT OK, "ALL TESTS PASSED" (log `full-suite-31.log` in the workflow workdir; ~11 min wall).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 30 (improve): P5-2 slice d — resizable panels COMPLETED

- P5-2 pass 1 is now complete: this cycle delivers **slice d — resizable panels**, the last pass-1 slice. A 6px drag handle on the left edge of the Scene inspector (340px default, 240–560px) and the Logic panel (320px default, 240–520px). The layout reads CSS variables off the tab containers (`--scene-panel-width` on `.scene-editor`, `--logic-panel-width` on `.grid-editor`), so the stage, the scene toolbar, and the panel all move together from one value — the three hardcoded 340px rules and the 320px logic-panel rule are gone, replaced by the variables. Widths persist per browser in localStorage under the versioned key `hydra.panelWidths.v1` and restore on the next load; double-click a handle (or focus it and press Home) snaps back to the default. The handles are keyboard controls (role="separator", arrows step 10px, Shift steps 40px, `aria-valuenow` announces the width), so the panels resize without a mouse, matching the P3 accessibility pass. Below the 900px breakpoint the panels dock below their main area, so the handles hide there — resizing is a wide-screen feature. The widths are per browser, not per project, so they follow the machine, not the .hydra file (module header + user guide say so).
- New shared module `hydra/hydra-panelresize.js` (`HydraPanelResize`, the factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `script.js`): pure helpers `clampWidth` (non-finite → default, clamped to the panel's limits), `parseStoredWidths` / `serializeWidths` (one bad entry never takes the other panel down), `widthFromPointer` (container right edge minus pointer x — both panels hang off their container's right edge), `keyboardDelta`, `readStoredWidths` / `writeStoredWidths` (throwing storage degrades to defaults), plus `createResizer` (pointerdown/move/up with pointer capture, drag applies live and persists on release, double-click resets, keyboard resize persists at once, non-primary buttons never start a drag, `destroy` detaches). `script.js` gains `initPanelResizers` (reads the stored widths, applies the CSS vars, wires both handles, called at editor load next to `initStatusBar`, never throws). Nothing here is inlined into exports — exported games have no editor panels. Docs: a "Resizable panels" paragraph in the user guide's editor tour.
- **Defect fixed in-cycle — the logic handle was 3px off the seam (real layout bug, caught by the visual QA).** The first QA run dragged the logic handle 100px left and the panel widened by 103px, to 423px. Root cause: the scene handle is absolutely positioned and straddles the seam (its center IS the panel's left edge), while the logic handle is a flex item sitting fully left of the panel, so its center is 3px left of the edge and the pointer math carries a constant 3px offset. Fix: `#logic-panel-resizer` now has `margin-left: -3px; margin-right: -3px`, pulling it over the seam so its center is the panel edge exactly like the scene handle — the handle takes zero net layout space and both panels drag 1:1. Pinned by the QA script's exact-width checks (420px after a 100px drag, grid giving up exactly 100px).
- Visual QA (cycle 30, script `panelresize-visual-qa.js`, 21 checks all green, 4 screenshots opened and inspected): (1) Scene tab default — inspector 340px, handle a visible 6px strip straddling the seam, stage and toolbar ending at 1060px, ew-resize cursor, no horizontal overflow; (2) dragged wide — inspector 460px, stage and toolbar following to 940px, handle riding the seam to 937px, `aria-valuenow` 460, no overflow; (3) Logic tab dragged wide — panel 420px, grid giving up exactly 100px, palette labels clean, no overflow; (4) narrow viewport (820px) — both handles hidden, inspector still stacking below the stage, no overflow. Zero page errors; no console errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: inspector tab labels truncate in earlier cycles' shots (here they read full at both widths); the Diagnostics dock overlaps the bottom of long tab panels; the "Desktop" device pill is clipped at the right edge; at 820px the header wraps "HYDRA SCRIPT" onto two lines.
- Tests: new `tests/panelresize.test.js` 103/103 (pure helpers incl. hostile storage, fake-DOM drag / double-click / keyboard / destroy, markup + CSS-var + wiring + docs pins), browser-smoke 8 new checks (defaults 340/320, scene drag to 460 with the stage following + localStorage persist, double-click reset to 340, logic ArrowRight +10 to 330, Home reset to 320, reload restoring stored 460/330, panels back to defaults after). Full suite green before commit: 176 PASS lines, 0 NOT OK, "ALL TESTS PASSED" (log `full-suite-30.log` in the workflow workdir; ~10 min wall).
- **Next capability (not yet implemented):** P5-2 is done — the pro editor shell pass 1 is complete (status bar, denser panels + smaller type, muted palette, resizable panels). The natural next step is **P5-3 functions / custom blocks**, where local (function) scope lands.

The tree was clean after that commit.
- P5-2 is multi-cycle sized, so this cycle delivers **slice a — the status bar**, the first piece of the pro editor shell and the one variable Ky'lin judges by screenshot this cycle. A 28px muted bar (`#171820`, hairline top border, 12px type) pinned to the bottom of the editor: current scene name + object count on the left, live preview FPS and the diagnostics count on the right. FPS reads the profiler's rolling 60-frame average through a new `previewFps()` getter on the preview runtime and shows a dash while no preview runs; the diagnostics count mirrors the dock's count, turns amber when something is open, and clicking it opens the dock. `updateStatusBar()` in `script.js` refreshes on a 500ms tick (the Live variables precedent) with change-only DOM writes and a guard so the bar can never break the editor. The content area now ends at `bottom: 28px` and the diagnostics dock was lifted from `bottom: 14px` to `bottom: 42px` so it clears the bar. Docs: a Status bar paragraph in the user guide's Preview and diagnostics section.
- **Bugs fixed in-cycle — two real ones, both caught by the visual QA.**
  1. **The diagnostics button stretched across the whole bar (real layout bug).** The first QA screenshots showed the scene name as "Coin..." with an ellipsis. A probe measured the cause: `#status-diagnostics` was 1131px wide, squeezing the scene item until its name shrank to min-content. Root cause is the global `button { width: 100% }` rule (plus its purple background, margin-top, and hover lift), which my `.status-diagnostics` rule had not reset — it set background/padding but left width, margin, and flex alone. Fix: `.status-diagnostics` now resets `width: auto; margin: 0; flex: 0 0 auto; box-shadow: none` and the hover resets transform/shadow; `.status-item` also got `flex-shrink: 0` so the left items never shrink (the spacer absorbs the slack). Pinned by two new checks in `tests/statusbar.test.js` and two live probes in the QA script (button width < 200px, name shown width >= full width).
  2. **Stale FPS after the preview stopped (real logic bug).** The narrow-viewport screenshot showed "49 FPS" on the Scene tab after the preview had stopped. Root cause: the profiler keeps its last samples after a stop (the frame loop only feeds it while `loopRunning`), so `previewFps()` returned the stale value forever. Fix: `previewFps()` now returns 0 unless `loopRunning` is true. Pinned by a new unit check (start, then stop → 0) and a QA-script regression check (Scene tab after preview reads "—").
- **QA-script fixes (script-side, not engine).** The first QA run's scene-tab screenshot was the loading screen: the 1500ms wait was shorter than the loading screen's own schedule (five 350–700ms steps + 400ms delay + 0.4s fade), so the script now waits for the `.hidden` class plus 800ms. The same run failed two visibility checks because `offsetParent` is always null for a `position: fixed` element — the probes now read `getBoundingClientRect().height === 28` instead. And the script's Chromium path was stale (1181); it now uses the cached chromium-1248 binary, like the smoke test.
- Visual QA (cycle 27, script `work/statusbar-visual-qa.js`, 18 checks all green, 5 screenshots opened and inspected): (1) scene tab shows "Scene Coin Rush", "6 objects", "— FPS", "0 diagnostics", bar exactly 28px pinned to the viewport bottom, content area ending where the bar begins, dock clearing the bar, no horizontal overflow; (2) logic tab keeps the bar visible with the same values; (3) preview tab shows a live FPS number (55) while the game runs; (4) a diagnostic probe reads "1 diagnostics" in amber with the dock showing the probe row; (5) narrow viewport (820px) keeps the bar at 28px with no overflow and FPS back to "—" after the stop. Zero page errors; no console errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: inspector tab labels truncate ("Localiza…", "Achieve…"); the Diagnostics dock overlaps the bottom of long tab panels; the "Desktop" device pill is clipped at the right edge; at 820px the header wraps "HYDRA SCRIPT" onto two lines.

After pass 1, P5-3 functions / custom blocks, where local (function) scope lands.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 26 (improve): P5-1 slice b — Variables manager panel + Scoped Variables Demo COMPLETED

- P5-1 is now complete: this cycle delivers **slice b — the Variables manager panel** (editor listing of the project's scoped-variable definitions with create/rename/delete/edit of starting values), the **Preview tab Live variables readout**, the **Scoped Variables Demo** template as the acceptance game, and definition seeding in both runtimes plus exports. P5-1 is checked off in ROADMAP.md; local (function) scope stays deferred to P5-3, as recorded.
- Definitions API (`hydra/hydra-variables.js`): `normalizeDefinition` (blank name → null, object scope with no object → null, unknown scope → global, unknown type → number, value coerced via `normalizeValue`), `normalizeDefinitions` (dedupe first-wins, non-array → `[]`), `definitionKey` (`scope:objectName:name`), `seedRun` (global + object, skips scene, returns count), `seedScene` (only scene defs into that scene's map). Definitions live in project JSON (`project.scopedVariables`); a scene definition applies to every scene — there is no per-scene definition yet. `normalizeProject` (`hydra-universal.js`) deep-normalizes the list; old projects get none. `serializeProject` already preserves the field.
- Seeding: `player-runtime.js` normalizes once at run start (`varDefs`) and seeds run defs in `start()` and scene defs in `loadScene()`; `hydra-previewruntime.js` adds `seedPreviewRunVariables()` / `seedPreviewSceneVariables(sceneId)` (called from `resetGame`, `loadPreviewScene`, `launchPreview`) plus `isPreviewRunning()`; the editor hosts the list via `getProjectScopedVariables` in the preview-runtime `configure` host. A fresh run starts with the definitions (global score 0, object hp 30, scene-1 coins 0) and no block has to set them first; a scene switch re-seeds the new scene's definitions while global and object values survive.
- Manager panel (`hydra/index.html` Variables inspector tab, `hydra/script.js` `renderVariablesPanel` / `initVariablesPanel`, `hydra/style.css` `.var-row` family): groups Global — whole game / Scene — current scene / Object — per object name; each row is a name button (click → rename prompt with duplicate check), a type badge, a starting-value input (number/text input, bool checkbox; list/dict shown as a count), and a × delete. The add form sits behind an "Add a variable" `<details>` disclosure (the middle-ground rule) with name, scope, object (shown only for object scope), type, and starting value, plus validation (blank name, object required for object scope, duplicate key refused with an error line). No `confirm()` on delete, matching the High Scores Clear precedent. Rows use the shared white card (dark text `#1e293b`).
- **Design correction — live values live in the Preview tab, not the manager panel.** The first draft put live values, a live dot, disabled inputs, and a Live-only group (with Save-as-definition) in the manager panel. That mode is unreachable in the real UI: every tab click calls `stopPreviewLoop()`, so the preview never runs while the Scene tab (and the panel) is visible. The panel now edits starting values only, and the live view is a **Live variables** readout in the Preview tab (`#preview-variables`, collapsible header with a count badge, refreshed every 500 ms by `renderPreviewVariables`): every definition plus any variable the blocks created mid-run, with name, type, scope, and current value (`data-preview-var-key` holds the definition key). The user guide and the panel's hint text say so plainly. The runtime-level live machinery (`seedPreviewRunVariables`, `seedPreviewSceneVariables`, `isPreviewRunning`, `variablesLiveState`) stays and is tested; only the unreachable panel mode was removed.
- Scoped Variables Demo template (`hydra/starter-templates.js`, `variables-demo`, New Project): two scenes (Level 1 `#0f172a`, Level 2 `#1e1b4b`) with definitions score 0 / coins 0 / hp 30 (object goblin). Player (46×46, physics, arrow-key `applyForce`), coin, goblin, door, and a HUD that reads the three scopes into flat vars and shows them. Coin → coins +1, score +10, coin teleports; goblin → hp −10, goblin teleports; hp ≤ 0 → destroy goblin, score +50, hp re-armed to 30; door → switch scene. One event per row (cycle-15 rule).
- **Bugs fixed in-cycle — two real ones, both caught by the visual QA, plus a layout fix.**
  1. **Orphaned definition edits (real editor bug, flaky).** The Variables add worked in a probe but failed intermittently in the full QA: the panel closes over the definition array from its render, but `getMutableScopedVariables` handed back a FRESH normalized array on every call, and the autosave's `normalizeProject` reassigned `project.scopedVariables` again 900 ms after any edit — so an add pushed into the old array and the next read saw the new one without it. A stamp probe reproduced it 1/5 runs (the stamp vanished from the post-click array). Fix at the root: `normalizeDefinitions` is now identity-preserving — when the input is already clean it returns the SAME array with the SAME definition objects (a dirty entry is replaced once, then the list is stable) — and `getMutableScopedVariables` rebuilds in place instead of reassigning. Pinned by two new checks in `tests/variables.test.js` (same array/objects when clean; replace-once-then-stable when dirty) and by the browser-smoke CRUD section; the flake probe then passed 5/5 with the stamp preserved.
  2. **The demo's kill chain fired every frame (real logic bug).** The first QA playthrough showed the score growing ~50/frame from boot while hp stayed 30 and the goblin never died. Root cause is engine flow semantics, not the check: the horizontal `and` layout (check / `and` / Exists on one row, actions below) put the score action directly below the Exists condition, and a condition fires the actions adjacent to it — so Exists fired the score+50 chain on its own every frame, while the `and` itself was correctly gated. Fix: a vertical `and` layout (check above the `and`, Exists left of it, the four actions on the row below the `and`, door moved to row 12) so no action sits below any triggering condition but the `and`. The template audit still passes, and the playthrough now reads exactly 0/0/30 → coin 1/10 → goblin hp 20 → scene 2 coins 0, score 10, hp 20.
  3. **Demo scene cosmetics (template-only).** The hint text ran off the right edge of the canvas (106 chars at fontSize 16 in a 700-wide object, no wrapping) — both hints shortened to fit one line. The player spawned at (80, 80) overlapping the "30" HP value — start moved to (80, 140), clear of the HUD.
  4. **Object dropdown uses the shared builder (caught by the first full-suite run).** `tests/propinputs.test.js` failed: the panel's hand-built object dropdown reused the `'(No named objects yet)'` empty-state string, which that suite bans from `script.js` (the object-select builder lives in the HydraPropInputs module). The panel now uses the shared `makeObjectSelect` (every named object in every scene; the previous pick is passed back in when the select is rebuilt), the unneeded `variablesObjectNames` helper is gone, and the smoke test now covers the object-scope add through the form too.
- Visual QA (cycle 26, script `work/variables-panel-visual-qa.js`, 18 checks all green, 6 screenshots opened and inspected): (1) panel groups the three definitions by scope with starting values 0/0/30 and the Object: goblin sub-line; (2) add form behind its disclosure; (3–5) add `lives` = 3 through the form, rename to `energy` via prompt, delete via ×, back to 3 definitions; (6–8) preview seeds 0/0/30 (live runtime and HUD flat vars agree) and the Live variables readout shows the three seeded values with scope labels and count 3; (9–10) steering into the coin reads Coins 1, Score 10, HP 30 in the HUD and the readout; (11–12) steering into the goblin reads HP 20 with coins/score untouched; (13–15) walking through the door switches to Level 2 (indigo background, Level 2 hint) with Coins re-seeded to 0 and Score 10 / HP 20 carried. Zero page errors; no console errors. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: first placement lands in grid cell 0 left of gameStart, rendering half off the left edge; inspector tab labels truncate; Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge.
- Tests: `tests/variables.test.js` 74/74 (was 49, +25: definitions API, seeding pins, panel + preview-readout pins, identity pins, guide pins), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 612/612 (was 598, +14: run/scene/launch seeding, `isPreviewRunning`, degradation without the module or the host entry), `tests/core.test.js` 60/60 (blank project `scopedVariables: []`, definitions survive `createProject`), `tests/templates.test.js` 157/157 (demo audit incl. the vertical `and` layout), `tests/docs.test.js` 9/9, export-boot Phase CG 17/17 (fresh-run seeding, block overwrite + reads, scene-2 re-seed, switch-back re-seed, hostile definitions tolerated), browser-smoke 13 new checks (panel rendering + CRUD + duplicate refusal + rename/edit/delete + object-scope add and cleanup + Preview-tab live readout + toggle). ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 25 (improve): P5-1 slice a — scoped variables (global/scene/object) with typed values COMPLETED

- P5-1 is multi-cycle sized, so this cycle delivers **slice a — the scoped-variables runtime core**: the shared module, six blocks, both runtimes, tests, an export-boot acceptance demo, docs, and visual QA. The **Variables manager panel** (editor listing with live values, create/rename/delete) and the in-editor demo game are the next slice (P5-1b), recorded in ROADMAP.md as remaining work; P5-1 stays unchecked after this cycle.
- New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-variables.js` (`HydraVariables`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-scaling.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`). State is transient run state: `{ global: {}, scene: {}, object: {} }`, each entry `{ type, value }`, types `number | text | bool | list | dict`. Three scopes: **global** = whole run, survives scene switches; **scene** = one map per scene id, cleared on scene load (the host clears it in `loadScene`, both runtimes); **object** = one map per object NAME (keyed by name like the growth/scaling ledgers, survives scene switches, and does NOT need a live object — the name is the key). API: `createVariablesState`, `normalizeScope` (unknown → global), `normalizeVarName` (trim, blank → null), `normalizeType` (unknown → number), `typeOfValue`, `normalizeValue` (strict-number rule per the cycle-13 lesson: `Number([])` is 0, so arrays/objects/booleans read as fallback), `objectKeyOf` (name, fallback id), `sceneKeyOf`, `set` (type inferred, or explicit `opts.type` with coercion; deep-copies lists/dicts; null/undefined value with no inferable type → null), `get` (copy or null), `read` (raw value or fallback), `has`, `remove`, `list` (name-sorted; with no `objectName` lists every object with `objectName` attached), `clearScene`, `clearScope` (object scope with no name clears ALL objects), `resetRun`, `serialize`/`deserialize` (JSON round-trip; hostile snapshot → fresh state), `isScopedVariableAction`, `isScopedVariableCondition`. Nothing ever throws; hostile input degrades to safe defaults. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): scoped vars are not in save slots yet (persist via Read Scoped Variable into a flat var); no `hs` script-API surface yet; lists/dicts can be stored by copying a variable that already holds one, but no block builds one from scratch yet (that is P5-6); the check condition compares numbers numerically and text/bool as text, and a list/dict never matches `==`.
- Six new blocks in the **Variables** category (507 total, was 501), indigo family (distinct from flat-variable blue `#2563eb` and struct violet `#7c3aed`) — **Set Scoped Variable** (Scope select global/scene/object, Object, Variable name, Value is select number/text/true/false/variable, Value expr; cell label "Set Var", `#4f46e5`) / **Read Scoped Variable** (Scope, Object, Variable name, Into variable; "Read Var", `#6366f1`) / **Delete Scoped Variable** (Scope, Object, Variable name; "Del Var", `#4338ca`) / **Clear Scoped Variables** (Scope, Object with label "Object (blank = every object)"; "Clear Vars", `#3730a3`) / **Scoped Variable Exists** condition (Scope, Object, Variable name, Invert; "Var Exists?", `#818cf8`) / **Scoped Variable Check** condition (Scope, Object, Variable name, operator, Value expr, Invert; "Var =?", `#a5b4fc`). Set warns and holds on a blank name, object scope with no object, or a bad number expression; Read writes the value (deep-copied) into the flat var, missing reads as 0, warns and holds on a blank target name; Delete warns and holds on a blank name and stays quiet on a missing variable. Both conditions are state reads, evaluate while paused, and never warn.
- Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch after the scaling dispatch (collections-style value-kind resolution; deep copy on variable-kind reads); `hydra-conditions.js` cases for both conditions (module path plus a raw-state fallback, so they read even without the module; numbers compare numerically, text/bool as text for `==`/`!=` only, list/dict never match, missing reads as 0); `hydra-flow.js` (both conditions → `level` trigger); `player-runtime.js` (hard guard naming `hydra-variables.js`, `varScopes` minted at boot after scaling, `clearScene` in `loadScene`, module/state in both context builders, classification lists, export inlining list); `hydra-previewruntime.js` (lazy `variablesRuntime()` / `resetVariablesRuntime()` on the scaling pattern, `clearPreviewSceneVariables(sceneId)` in BOTH scene-load paths; deliberately NO `sceneResetHooks` entry — the host clears the scene scope, not the reset module). Docs: six rows plus detail sections in `docs/block-reference.md` (`### Variables (33)`), a "Scoped variables." paragraph plus honest-limits sentences in `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Export-test findings (Phase CF, `tests/export-boot.test.js`, 11 check groups):** inlining (`hydraVariablesFactory`), missing-module guard (regex `/hydra-variables\.js/` — ONE backslash in file bytes, per the cycle-21 guard-regex lesson), fresh empty run, gameStart sets + typed entries + both gates open, three-scope reads (100/5/30/'hero'/true), bad value → exactly 2 diagnostics + score held at 100, blank varName → exactly 1 diagnostic matching /blank/, delete → title reads 0, scene-2 switch (global score + object hp survive, scene-2 coins start absent, then 99/100/30), switch back (scene-1 coins reset to 0 on load, hp 30 + score 100 survive), no diagnostics on switch flows — all passed. Grid uses the cycle-15 guard rule (one event per row).
- **Load-order fallout:** every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-variables.js` first (the hard guard). `work/variables-fallout.py` (idempotent; second run +0 everywhere): `tests/export-boot.test.js` +70 list inserts +25 line inserts, `tests/flagship.test.js` +1 list, `tests/playtest.test.js` +1 list, `tests/fps-export.test.js` +1 line. It handles two forms (array-element list lines vs `vm.runInContext` load lines), skips `.filter(...)` guard lines (Phase CE's scaling guard) and lines already carrying hydra-variables.js (Phase CF's own list). All four files pass `node --check` after.
- **Bugs fixed in-cycle.** One real engine bug, caught by the first full-suite run (not by the standalone suites): `clearScene` returned true on a second clear of the same scene, because a cleared map is `{}` and `{}` is truthy, so the "something was there" guard passed again. Fixed in `hydra-variables.js`: `clearScene` and all `clearScope` branches now return false when the map is missing or already empty ("returns true when at least one variable was cleared"), with new pins in `tests/variables.test.js` (second clear of a scene, one object, all objects, and global each return false). Everything else was test-side or script-side: (1) the Phase CF `cfRead` helper took 4 args but was called with 5 — fixed to `(s, scope, name, fallback, opts)`; (2) an edit swallowed the `check('exported gameStart sets...'` line (syntax error) — restored, and a junk `cePressFix` stub was removed; (3) the catalog validator rejects `placeholder` on `object` controls (extras are only `['includeAll']`) — the six `placeholder: ''` entries were removed via a Python replace; (4) the visual-QA script failed its first run on two checks, both script-side: the editor defaults to the Scene tab where the block selector is hidden (switch to the script tab first), and the demo grid was wiped on the preview-tab click — `activeScriptScope` defaults to `'global'`, and the tab switch runs `commitActiveGrid`, which REPLACES the stored global grid with `window.cellData` wholesale, so a demo built by writing grid stores directly must ALSO populate the `cellData` Map (the cycle-21 lesson was the mirror image: clear cellData when the scope is a scene). The debug script `work/variables-debug.js` pinned this down (`globalGridData` keys `[]` in the preview while the scene objects rendered fine).
- Visual QA (cycle 25, script `work/variables-visual-qa.js`, 13 checks all green, 5 screenshots opened and inspected): (1) palette shows the Variables category with the six blocks in order, indigo swatches, clean labels (the scoped rows sit below the fold under the Diagnostics dock overlap — a pre-existing non-defect — and are DOM-verified by the smoke test instead); (2) Set Scoped Variable places with cell label "Set Var" and its panel shows Scope ("Global (whole game)"), Object ("(No named objects yet)"), Variable name ("coins"), Value is ("Number (expression)"), Value ("0") with fx; (3) Read Scoped Variable places with "Read Var" and its panel shows Scope, Object, Variable name, Into variable ("coinsHere"); (4) Scoped Variable Check places with "Var =?" and its panel shows Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level hint, Scope, Object, Variable name, Compare ("at least"), Value ("1") with fx, and the Invert checkbox; (5–6) live preview on a dark scene with pixel probes: before KeyA the labels read "SCORE ?" / "COINS ?" / "HP ?" with the red enemy square; after KeyA they read "SCORE 100" / "COINS 5" / "HP 30" (`gameVariables` shown 100, shownCoins 5, shownHp 30) and all three pixel regions move. Zero page errors; the only console errors are the sandbox's Font Awesome CDN tunnel failures, filtered the same way browser-smoke filters them. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: first placement lands in grid cell 0 left of gameStart, rendering half off the left edge; inspector tab labels truncate; Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge.
- Tests: new `tests/variables.test.js` 49/49 (module API, normalizers, scope semantics, deep-copy isolation, clearScene/clearScope/resetRun, serialize/deserialize + hostile input, routing sets, hostile-state degradation, static wiring pins, docs pins), `tests/execute.test.js` 553/553 (was 527, +26), `tests/conditions.test.js` 446/446 (was 437, +9), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 598/598 (was 591, +7), `tests/flow.test.js` scoped conditions → level pins, `tests/catalog.test.js` count pin 501 to 507, `tests/docs.test.js` green, export-boot Phase CF 11/11, browser-smoke Variables checks green. Full suite green before commit: 175 PASS lines, "ALL TESTS PASSED" (log `full-suite-25c.log` in the workflow workdir; ~11 min wall, second run after the clearScene fix — the first full run caught the one failure above).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 24 (redirect): Ky'lin redirects the loop — P5 core-engine track, P4 PAUSED; batch 33 CANCELLED and reverted

- **The redirect (Ky'lin's call, evening Nov 6, 2026; brief at `~/workspace/hydrascript/REDIRECT-2026-11-06.md`).** He corrected the loop's direction, and he is right. Real engines (Godot, GDevelop) do NOT ship a single "attack" or "shoot" block; they ship primitives — variables, functions, objects, physics queries, events. A one-block Melee Attack / Shoot Projectile is a scene-editor convenience, not engine power. It limits what a user can build, because the mechanism is hidden inside the block. Blocks should HELP, not replace and not be the only options; every preset must be decomposable into ordinary blocks the user could place by hand, and there must always be a manual path from primitives. (This was already the Nov 4 doctrine. The P4 batches drifted from it: batch 29 combat, 30–32 leveling/scaling, 33 elite presets are canned game systems, not primitives.) His proof case: he once prototyped HydraScript itself inside GDevelop and got stuck at the variable system — it was almost impossible to build a variable system inside GDevelop. His dream is that HydraScript is powerful enough that someone could build that same prototype INSIDE HydraScript. That is the acceptance test for the new track: using only HydraScript blocks, build a working variable inspector inside a game (list variables, edit values, watch them change live). He wants actual engine developments and changes now, not more game-logic systems — and visual developments too: the editor layout looks childish and weak, not professional and powerful like Unreal. The visual track targets the editor SHELL (layout, density, palette, panels), not the block shapes he already directed. The logic grid stays the signature view; the event sheet is never reintroduced.
- **What was reverted and why.** Batch 33 (elite / boss enemy presets) was cancelled mid-cycle, before commit. The cycle had built the full batch uncommitted: new shared pure module `hydra/hydra-elite.js` (`HydraElite`, Elite +2/20/4 and Boss +5/50/8 presets over the scaling ledger), four blocks in a new Elite category (Make Elite / Clear Elite / Read Elite Name / Is Elite), wiring in `index.html`, `player-runtime.js`, `hydra-previewruntime.js`, `hydra-execute.js`, `hydra-conditions.js`, `hydra-flow.js`, `block-catalog.js`, plus `tests/elite.test.js` (39 checks, all passing), execute/conditions/previewruntime/flow/catalog additions, export-boot Phase CF (12 check groups, all passing), browser-smoke Elite checks, docs, and the ROADMAP batch-33 entry. All of it was reverted with `git checkout -- .` plus `rm -f hydra/hydra-elite.js tests/elite.test.js`; `git status --porcelain` is clean and HEAD is back at cycle 23 (`300b440`). Nothing from batch 33 was committed, and the revert was done regardless of the work being complete — the cycle-prompt rule about committing complete uncommitted work does not apply to a cancelled batch. The module, tests, and docs are gone from the tree; the design (hero-relative presets over the scaling ledger) is recorded here and in the cycle-24 workdir fallout script only, not in the repo. Cycles 22 (level-up rewards) and 23 (enemy scaling) landed before the redirect and stay.
- **Hard rules from this point on (now in ROADMAP.md design principles 5–6).** NO new canned game-action blocks. Primitives first, always: variables with scopes, functions, control flow, object references, physics queries that return data, events. If a capability needs a friendly face, ship the primitives first and the preset second, and the preset must ship with its visible decomposition. P2 shared-engine extraction is COMPLETE (slices 1–9, cycles 61–69) — do not redo it; the remaining unification work is the P5-7 leftovers, not a second P2. Existing canned blocks (Melee Attack, Shoot Projectile, leveling, scaling) stay working for backward compatibility — they are presets now, not the model for new work.
- **ROADMAP.md changes (this cycle, docs only).** New P5 core-engine track written at the top, marked highest priority above P4, with P4 Advanced batches marked PAUSED (do not start batch 34 or any further Advanced batch until P5 is done): P5-1 Variables v2 (scoped variables, typed values, Variables manager panel — FIRST), P5-2 pro editor shell pass 1, P5-3 functions / custom blocks, P5-4 control flow + events, P5-5 object primitives + decompose attack/shoot, P5-6 data structures, P5-7 core unification remainder (ONE flow module, port the row-60 fix), P5-8 pro editor shell pass 2, P5-9 the build-the-editor acceptance test. The "Next cycle" line now points at P5-1. CHANGELOG.md updated.
- **Verification:** full suite run on the reverted tree (this cycle) — all green, "ALL TESTS PASSED" (log `full-suite-redirect.log` in the workflow workdir).
- **Next capability:** P5-1 Variables v2. No further Advanced batches.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 23 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 32 — enemy scaling with the hero's level COMPLETED

- Batch 32 (Unity/Godot difficulty scaling, beginner edition): the natural next step after level-up rewards & stat growth (batch 31) — the hero grows stronger every level, but enemies do not, so the game gets easier as the run goes on. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-scaling.js` (`HydraScaling`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-growth.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`). State is transient run state: `{ byId: {} }`, a per-object ledger keyed by object NAME (the same name blocks address objects by), minted fresh on a fresh run, SURVIVES scene switches inside a run (like XP, growth, and lives), resets only on a fresh run, never in project JSON or save slots (persist via the Read blocks into variables). Deliberately NO scene-reset hook. Each object carries a level and two rates: `level` (1 by default), `hpPerLevel` (10 by default), `dmgPerLevel` (2 by default). Bonuses are derived, never stored: `hpBonus = (level - 1) * hpPerLevel` and `dmgBonus = (level - 1) * dmgPerLevel`, each capped at 10000 — so a level-1 enemy is +0/+0 and old games play exactly as before. Normalizers follow the strict-number rule (cycle-13 lesson): `normalizeLevel` whole 1–999 default 1, `normalizeHpPerLevel` whole 0–10000 default 10, `normalizeDmgPerLevel` whole 0–10000 default 2. Writes return deltas so the live max health composes with growth through deltas: `setLevel` returns `{ level, hpBonus, dmgBonus, hpDelta }` and `setRates` returns `{ hpPerLevel, dmgPerLevel, hpBonus, dmgBonus, hpDelta }` (or null without state/key); the host applies `hpDelta` via `HydraHealth.setMaxHealth(curMax + hpDelta)` and heals by `hpDelta` when positive, so scaling an enemy up never heals it to full — it only gains the new ceiling. Reads return 1 for a missing object's level and 0 for missing bonuses, and never throw; hostile input degrades to safe defaults. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): nothing scales on its own — chain Level Up → Scale to Hero Level, or put Scale to Hero Level on a gameStart row (gameStart fires once per scene, per the cycle-15 rule) so each scene's enemies scale when the scene starts; the health bonus applies when a scaling block runs, not on scene load (same rule as growth), while the damage bonus is read from the ledger at use time, so it applies in full in every scene; scaling never touches speed; scaling is per enemy, not per level curve — the author picks the rates per enemy; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Design correction — Damage Object does NOT add the scaling bonus.** Same correction as cycle 22 for growth: Damage Object names the victim, not an attacker, so adding the victim's own bonus would make a scaled enemy take MORE damage. Melee Attack and Shoot Projectile add the attacker's bonus (the projectile carries the boosted damage). To scale a Damage Object hit by hand, the author reads the bonus with Read Scaled Damage Bonus and adds it in the amount expression (`10 + dmgBonus`). Documented in the hydra-scaling.js module header, the catalog notes, the block reference, and the user-guide honest-limits paragraph.
- Seven new blocks in a new **Scaling** category (501 total, was 494), fuchsia family (distinct from Growth emerald, XP lime, Combat orange) — **Set Enemy Level** (Object + Level expr default 1; cell label "Set Lvl", `#a21caf`) / **Set Scaling Rates** (Object + HP per level expr default 10 + Damage per level expr default 2; "Scale Rates", `#86198f`) / **Scale to Hero Level** (Object only; "Scale to Hero", `#c026d3`) / **Read Enemy Level** / **Read Scaled HP Bonus** / **Read Scaled Damage Bonus** (each Object + Into variable text; "Read Lvl"/"Read HP+"/"Read Dmg+", `#d946ef`/`#e879f9`/`#f0abfc`) / **Enemy Level** condition (Object + operator + value, `level` trigger default, invertible, evaluates while paused; "Enemy Lvl?", `#701a75`). Bad level/rate expressions warn and hold; blank variable names warn and hold; missing objects stay quiet on writes and read as level 1 / bonus 0. The alternative is **per-scene difficulty settings** (one difficulty number per scene that every enemy in the scene reads) — either keeps the one-block-per-enemy authoring from becoming the only way to make a hard fight.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 22 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 31 — level-up rewards & stat growth COMPLETED

- Batch 31 (Unity RPG stat growth / Godot level-up rewards, beginner edition): the natural next step after experience & leveling (batch 30) — the hero earns XP and levels up, but nothing in the game reacts to the level yet beyond the rows the author writes by hand. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-growth.js` (`HydraGrowth`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-xp.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`). State is transient run state: `{ byId: {} }`, a per-object ledger keyed by object NAME (the same name blocks address objects by), minted fresh on a fresh run, SURVIVES scene switches inside a run (like XP and lives), resets only on a fresh run, never in project JSON or save slots (persist via the Read Bonus blocks into variables). Deliberately NO scene-reset hook. Each object carries three bonuses: max-health, damage, speed. Normalizers follow the strict-number rule (cycle-13 lesson): `normalizeMaxHealthAmount` whole 0–10000 default 10, `normalizeDamageAmount` whole 0–10000 default 2, `normalizeSpeedAmount` whole 0–5000 default 50; totals cap at 10000 / 10000 / 2000 (the dash cap). Reads return 0 for missing objects and never throw; writes return the new total or null without state/key. `rewardBundle(name)` returns a fresh copy of one of four bundles (Balanced +10 max health / heal 10 / +2 damage; Tank +20 max health / heal 20; Striker +5 damage; Swift +50 speed) or null for an unknown name; `applyReward` applies one through the same writers. Nothing ever throws; hostile input degrades to safe defaults. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): growth is per object, not per level curve; the speed bonus quickens dashes only (Dash is the only movement block with a speed); Damage Object never adds a bonus (it names the victim, not an attacker — read the bonus and add it in the amount expression); the ledger survives scene switches but the live max health is per-scene authored state (a fresh scene's hero starts at its own authored max health, the next Grow Max Health builds from there; damage and speed bonuses apply in full in every scene because they are read from the ledger at use time); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Design correction — Damage Object does NOT add the damage bonus.** The roadmap sketch said the bonus applies to "Melee Attack / Shoot Projectile / Damage Object", but Damage Object names the victim, not an attacker, so adding the victim's own bonus would make a grown hero take MORE damage. Melee Attack and Shoot Projectile add the attacker's bonus (the projectile carries the boosted damage). To grow a Damage Object hit by hand, the author reads the bonus with Read Damage Bonus and adds it in the amount expression (`10 + dmgBonus`). Documented in the hydra-growth.js module header, the Grow Damage catalog note, the block reference, and the user-guide honest-limits paragraph. Precedent for deviating from a sketch with documented reasoning: cycle 19 (wall slide extended hydra-movement.js instead of a new module).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 21 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 30 — experience & leveling COMPLETED

- Batch 30 (Unity RPG progression / Godot XP systems, beginner edition): the natural next step after combat (batch 29) — the hero can fight, but a defeated enemy earns nothing and there is no sense of getting stronger across a run. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-xp.js` (`HydraXp`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-combat.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`). State is transient run state, minted fresh on a fresh run, SURVIVES scene switches inside a run (like lives), resets only on a fresh run, never in project JSON or save slots (persist via Read XP / Read Level into variables). Deliberately NO scene-reset hook. State: `{ xp: 0, xpPerLevel: 100, levelAnnounced: 1 }`. Level is derived, never stored: `level = floor(xp / xpPerLevel) + 1`, min 1. `levelUp(state)` is consume-on-read: returns true once per level increase, then marks that level announced; a state with no announced level treats its current level as already announced (no spurious fire); lowering XP below the announced level never re-fires until the level climbs past it again; a multi-level jump fires once, not once per level. Normalizers follow the strict-number rule (cycle-13 lesson: `Number([])` is 0): `normalizeXp` whole 0–999999999 default 0, `normalizeXpAmount` whole 0–1000000 default 10, `normalizeXpPerLevel` whole 1–100000 default 100 (below 1 reads as 1, so level math can never divide by zero). Nothing ever throws; hostile input degrades to safe defaults. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one XP total per run (no per-object XP, no XP curves — one flat XP-per-level number); Level Up is consumed on read, so place one Level Up row per reward chain; lowering XP below the announced level never re-fires until the level climbs past it again; gaining XP never heals/damages/unlocks anything on its own; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- Eight new blocks in a new **XP** category (487 total, was 479), lime family (unused colors: Add XP `#65a30d`, Set XP `#4d7c0f`, Set XP Per Level `#3f6212`, Read XP `#84cc16`, Read Level `#a3e635`, XP `#bef264`, Level `#d9f99d`, Level Up `#365314`) — **Add XP** (Amount expr, default 10) / **Set XP** (XP expr, default 0) / **Set XP Per Level** (Amount expr, default 100) / **Read XP** (Into variable text) / **Read Level** (Into variable text) actions and the invertible **XP** (operator + value, default `>= 10`, `level` trigger) / **Level** (operator + value, default `>= 2`, `level` trigger) / **Level Up** (Invert only, `edge` trigger default) conditions, all evaluating while paused. Bad values warn and hold; blank variable names warn and hold; missing state stays quiet on writes and reads as 0 XP at level 1. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (after combat dispatch), `hydra-conditions.js` (cases after `gameOver`, each with a raw-field fallback without the module; levelUp fallback also consumes via `ctx.xp.levelAnnounced`), `hydra-flow.js` (`xp`/`level` → level, `levelUp` stays edge), `hydra-previewruntime.js` (lazy `xpRuntime()` / `resetXpRuntime()` on the lives pattern — NO new `script.js` host entry needed; `mod('Xp')` resolves via the default `globalThis['Hydra' + name]` provider), `player-runtime.js` (hard guard, `xp` minted at boot after lives, `xpModule`/`xp` in both condition and action context builders, classification lists, export inlining list). Docs: new XP section in `docs/block-reference.md` and an Experience and leveling paragraph (plus honest-limits sentences) in `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Export-test findings (Phase CC, `tests/export-boot.test.js`, 37 checks):** inlining (`hydraXpFactory`), missing-module guard, fresh run 0 XP level 1 with gameStart setting 50 XP per level, Add XP 10 then 40 → level 2 with Level Up firing once, six more 10s → 100 XP level 3 with a second Level Up and XP ≥ 100 firing, Set XP 250 → level 6 with one Level Up fire (multi-level jump), bad per-level value → exactly 2 diagnostics and cost stays 50, blank-variable Read XP → exactly 1 diagnostic, scene switch keeping XP/level/announced level (no spurious re-fire), lowering XP to 0 then climbing back to level 2 staying quiet and climbing past announced level 3 firing again — all passed. Grid uses the cycle-15 guard rule (one event per row; level conditions only feed idempotent `setVariable` writes). Two test-side corrections in my own checks: 40 + 6×10 = 100 (not 5×10); a fresh session's first level-up makes the count 1, not 2.
- **Load-order fallout:** every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-xp.js` first (the hard guard). `work/xp-fallout.py` (idempotent; second run +0 everywhere): `tests/export-boot.test.js` +67 list inserts +25 line inserts, `tests/flagship.test.js` +1 list, `tests/playtest.test.js` +1 list, `tests/fps-export.test.js` +1 line. `node --check` passed on all four. Verified 0 unfollowed combat loads; the single flagged line is the Phase CB guard-test filter (`cbModules.filter(m => m !== 'hydra-combat.js')`), a filter expression, not a load — correct as-is. Guard-regex trap restated (cycle-20 lesson): the regex in the test file must be `/hydra-xp\.js/` (ONE backslash in file bytes); a doubled backslash fails with "NOT OK - player-runtime throws a clear error without hydra-xp.js" — fixed with sed, and the passing test is the ground truth (tool-output escaping layers hide the difference, verify with a byte count).
- **Bugs fixed in-cycle — both test-side, not engine:** `tests/xp.test.js` had two wrong expectations that the first full-suite run caught (they also fail standalone, deterministically). (1) "climbing past the announced level fires again" used 750 XP: after the multi-level jump the announced level is 8, and 750 XP is level 8 — merely equal to it, not past it — so `levelUp` correctly reads false. The check now asserts quiet at 750 and a fire at 800 (level 9). (2) "addXp clamps at 999999999" added 2,000,000,000 to 10 XP: one call can add at most 1,000,000 (the amount cap), so the total can only reach 1,000,010. The check now seeds at 999,500,000 first, then adds 1,000,000 and reads the clamped 999,999,999. The module semantics (strict `>` for the level-up, amount cap before total clamp) are the spec; the tests encoded wrong arithmetic.
- Visual QA (cycle 21, script `work/xp-visual-qa.js`): (1) palette shows the XP category with Add XP / Set XP / Set XP Per Level / Read XP / Read Level / XP / Level / Level Up in order, lime swatches, clean labels (the last two rows sit below the fold under the Diagnostics dock overlap — a pre-existing non-defect — and are DOM-verified by the smoke test instead); (2) Add XP places with cell label "Add XP" and its panel shows Amount ("10") with fx; (3) Set XP Per Level places with cell label "XP / Level" and its panel shows Amount ("100") with fx; (4) Read Level places with cell label "Read Level" and its panel shows "Into variable" with placeholder "e.g. playerLevel"; (5) Level Up places with cell label "Level Up" and its panel shows Trigger "On become true (once)" with the edge/level hint and the Invert checkbox; (6–7) live preview with pixel probes on a dark scene: before any gain the labels read "XP: 0" (311 bright px) / "LEVEL 1" (522) with the banner blank (0); after KeyA (+10) and KeyB (+40) at 50 XP per level they read "XP: 50" (402) / "LEVEL 2" (541) with "LEVEL UP!" shown (693). Zero page errors; no console errors. **QA-script finding, not an engine defect — the cellData commit trap:** the first QA run failed the level half with the labels stuck at "XP: 10" / "LEVEL 1" and no banner, and the live `_runtimeText` values were provably correct in a debug run without the placement phase. Root cause: the script tab defaults to the SCENE scope (Coin Rush), so the placement phase puts its blocks in the editor's live `cellData` map, not in `window.globalGridData` (which stays empty after placing); every tab click runs `commitActiveGrid`, and `saveGridToScope` REPLACES the scope's stored grid with `cellData` wholesale — so writing the demo grid into the stores alone is not enough, because clicking the preview tab saves the placed blocks back over the scene grid, and the strays run in the preview (a stray Add XP adds 10 at launch, a stray Set XP Per Level at its default 100 overwrites the demo's 50, a stray Level Up condition eats the level-up). The demo build must clear `window.cellData` too, so the commit saves an empty scene grid. Lesson for the next harness author: any in-page demo build that writes grid stores directly must also clear `window.cellData`, and must check which scope the script tab is actually showing before assuming where placed blocks land. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: first placement lands in grid cell 0 left of gameStart, rendering half off the left edge; inspector tab labels truncate; Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge; preview canvas is CSS-scaled (1077×808 displayed for 800×600 buffer) so canvas clicks must scale by displayed bounding box; Diagnostics dock warnings in the earlier preview shots were pre-existing collector-project cross-scene references (blocks 543/541/483) — the fixed QA run's demo build clears the scene grid, so its preview shots show "No runtime diagnostics."
