The hero earns XP and levels up, but nothing in the game reacts to the level yet beyond the rows the author writes. The natural next step is a **level-up reward / stat growth** batch (Unity RPG stat growth / Godot level-up rewards, beginner edition): leveling up raises the hero's numbers (max health, damage, speed) through blocks, so the level is not just a label.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 20 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 29 — combat: melee attacks & projectiles COMPLETED

- Batch 29 (Unity combat / Godot hitboxes, beginner edition): the natural next step after movement abilities (batch 26), moving platforms (batch 27), and wall slide & wall jump (batch 28) — the hero can move, ride, and climb, but hurting an enemy still meant hand-wiring a collision row plus Damage Object, and a fireball still meant hand-wiring Spawn Object plus Move Toward plus another collision row. Now the hero fights back: a melee swing that hits everything solid in the arc in front of the attacker, and a projectile that flies straight until it hits the first solid thing.
- **Design decision — a new shared pure module:** `hydra/hydra-combat.js` (factorySource pattern, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-platform.js`). Per-object attack cooldowns on the frozen game clock (melee and shot tracked separately), a transient projectile list, and a transient slash visual, refreshed by a new `updateCombat` frame phase after `updateMovement`. State is transient run state, fresh per scene load via the scene-reset hook, never in project JSON or save slots. Melee hits every solid object whose center sits within range and within ±45° of the picked direction (90° arc, center-to-center), damage through the same path as Damage Object (invincible targets skip the hit, Object Died fires on the killing blow), and always draws a slash arc even with nothing in range. Shoot spawns a clone of the projectile template at the shooter's edge, recentered by half the clone size; it flies straight at the picked speed until it hits the first solid object (AABB overlap) or its lifetime ends — one hit, no piercing, never the shooter or another projectile, capped at 200 in flight. Normalizers follow the strict-number rule (damage 0–10000 default 10; melee range 0–2000 default 60; cooldown 0–60 default 0.5; speed 0–5000 default 300; lifetime 0–60 default 2; slash TTL 0.15s). Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one direction per attack (no diagonal, no facing-relative attacks — pick the direction on the block); melee hits anything solid in the arc, walls included; a projectile stops at the first solid thing it touches, wall included, and never pierces; projectiles move by direct position steps, so one can sit inside a thin wall for a frame before the hit check lands; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- Three new blocks in a new **Combat** category (479 total, was 476), orange family (#ea580c / #c2410c / #9a3412) to distinguish from Health reds — **Melee Attack** (Attacker + Damage 10 + Range (px) 60 + Direction Left/Right/Up/Down default Right + Cooldown (s) 0.5) / **Shoot Projectile** (Shooter + Projectile object + Direction + Speed (px/s) 300 + Damage 10 + Lifetime (s) 2 + Cooldown (s) 0.5) actions and the invertible **Attack Ready** condition (Object + Attack Melee/Shot + Cooldown (s) 0.5, `level` trigger default, evaluates while paused). Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (bad numbers warn and hold; missing object/template stays quiet and never spends the cooldown), `hydra-conditions.js` (module path plus a raw-field fallback reading `ctx.combat.cooldowns`), `hydra-flow.js` (`attackReady` → level), `hydra-scenereset.js` (`createCombatState` hook), `hydra-draw.js` (slash pass after the damage numbers), `hydra-frame.js` (`updateCombat` phase), `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `hydra/script.js` (preview host entry `ensurePreviewCombatState`, preview draw hooks), `player-runtime.js` (hard guard, both ctx builders, phase hook, inlining list). Docs: new Combat section in `docs/block-reference.md` and `docs/user-guide.md`.
- **Bug fixed in-cycle (real engine bug, found by the export tests):** the Attack Ready block's attack-kind field was named `kind`, which collides with the grid row's own `kind` marker (`'condition'`/`'action'`) — the row marker would clobber the picked Melee/Shot value, so Shot could never be selected from a real grid. Renamed the field to `attackKind` in the catalog and the condition (with a `data.kind` fallback so direct calls that pass `kind` still work), and pinned it in `tests/combat.test.js` (catalog pin) and `tests/conditions.test.js` (attackKind checks plus one legacy-kind check). Docs reference the field only by its label ("Attack"), so no doc change was needed.
- **Export-test findings (Phase CB, `tests/export-boot.test.js`, 33 checks):** the inlining, guard, melee, cooldown, projectile flight/hit/no-pierce, invincible-skip, bad-value, and scene-switch checks all passed on first run except 6 test-side failures, all fixed in the tests, none engine-side — (1) the guard regex had a doubled backslash (`/hydra-combat\\.js/` matches a literal backslash, never the message; the BU phase's identical-looking line has one backslash — the escaping layers in tool output hide the difference, verify with a byte count); (2) `updateCombat` runs before execute in the frame, so a projectile sits at its spawn spot (134, 110) on the press frame and only starts moving the frame after (assertions now read spawn, then +5px after one pump, then the hit); (3) destroyed objects stay in the raw `state.objects` array flagged in `state.destroyed` — the live count must filter that set (the engine's `applyPendingDestroy` phase is correct); (4) the export normalizer writes health 100 onto every object at start, so the hero's health reads 100, not undefined — the never-hits-the-shooter check now asserts it stays 100; (5) conditions are independent event sources — a `keyJustPressed` row does NOT gate an `attackReady` row, the level condition fires its action every frame while true (rewrote the Attack Ready grid as two level rows plus a KeyC clear row, reading both the melee and shot flags before/after the cooldown); (6) the scene-switch check expected two cooldown records, but melee and shot share one record per object id (assert the record's two fields instead).
- **Load-order fallout:** every vm sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-combat.js` first (the hard guard throws otherwise). Applied idempotently by `work/combat-fallout.py` (91 insertions in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, 1 each in `tests/flagship.test.js`, `tests/playtest.test.js`, `tests/fps-export.test.js` — the fps-export form was a bare-`sandbox` runInContext statement, which the first version of the script missed and hand-fix notes in cycles 15–16 warned about; second run reports +0 everywhere). The Phase BZ platform-guard test deliberately loads without `hydra-platform.js` and was left untouched. The same new host entry broke five more exact-substring host-list pins the first full-suite run caught (`tests/damagefx.test.js`, `tests/movement.test.js`, `tests/platform.test.js`, `tests/respawn.test.js`, `tests/spawn.test.js` all pin the script.js host list ending `ensurePreviewPlatformState, getPreviewBuses`) — each now pins `ensurePreviewPlatformState, ensurePreviewCombatState, getPreviewBuses`. Lesson restated: any new preview host entry must grep every `ensurePreview…` pin across tests, not just the preview-runtime suite.
- Tests: `tests/combat.test.js` 46/46 (normalizers incl. hostile input, directionVector, cooldown separation, melee geometry incl. 45° edge inclusion, damage + slash + cooldown refusal + empty swing, projectile exclusion from melee, spawn recenter math, blank/missing template + no-cooldown-spent, 200 cap, flight + first-hit + no pierce, lifetime expiry, vanished-clone drop, slash fade/prune, draw arc angles/alpha, routing, static wiring pins), `tests/execute.test.js` 461/461, `tests/conditions.test.js` 419/419, `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 571/571, `tests/draw.test.js` 99/99, `tests/scenereset.test.js` 85/85, `tests/catalog.test.js` 67/67, `tests/flow.test.js` / `tests/frame.test.js` green, export-boot Phase CB 33/33, flagship / playtest / fps-export green, browser-smoke combat checks green. Full suite green before commit (see cycle-20 visual QA below).
- Visual QA (cycle 20, script `work/combat-visual-qa.js`): (1) palette shows the COMBAT category with Melee Attack, Shoot Projectile, Attack Ready in order, orange icons, clean labels; (2) Melee Attack places with cell label "Melee" and its panel shows Attacker (player), Damage ("10"), Range (px) ("60"), Direction ("Right"), Cooldown (s) ("0.5"); (3) Shoot Projectile places with cell label "Shoot" and its panel shows Shooter (player), Projectile (blank — no template picked yet, correct), Direction ("Right"), Speed (px/s) ("300"), Damage ("10"), Lifetime (s) ("2"), Cooldown (s) ("0.5"); (4) Attack Ready places with cell label "Ready?" and its panel shows Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level hint, Object (player), Attack (blank — Melee default), Cooldown (s) ("0.5"), and the Invert checkbox; (5) live preview with pixel probes on a dark scene: the melee slash arc reads (299 white stroke pixels in the sector — the fill is 0.25 alpha so only the 6px white stroke counts, threshold 180 not 230), the enemy health bar shrinks 38 → 35 green px after the swing (40px bar minus the 1px border blend at each end) while the second enemy's bar stays full, the fireball is visible in flight right (400 yellow px, maxX 153) and the bar shrinks again 35 → 31 after the hit, and an upward shot climbs visibly (280 yellow px above the hero). Zero page errors; no console errors. QA-script findings, neither an engine defect: Playwright round-trips are too slow to catch a 150ms slash or a 50ms fireball flight, so the slash and right-shot probes sample from an in-page rAF loop that scans the canvas every frame the page renders; full-canvas getImageData costs ~50ms per poll, so the probes read small regions instead. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: first placement lands in grid cell 0 left of gameStart, rendering half off the left edge; Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge; preview canvas is CSS-scaled (1077×808 displayed for 800×600 buffer) so canvas clicks must scale by displayed bounding box; Diagnostics dock warnings in preview shots are pre-existing collector-project cross-scene references (blocks 543/541/483), not combat blocks.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 19 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 28 — wall slide & wall jump COMPLETED

- Batch 28 (Unity character controllers / Godot `is_on_wall()` / Celeste game feel, beginner edition): the natural next step after movement abilities (batch 26) and moving platforms (batch 27) — the hero can double-jump, coyote-jump, buffer, dash, and ride platforms, now vertical walls stop being dead ends. A hero pressing toward a solid wall while airborne slides down it slowly instead of falling, and jumping while sliding pushes off the wall — up and away — so wall shafts become climbable.
- **Design decision — extension, not a new module:** the roadmap sketch said "a new shared pure module," but wall state shares the movement record, the `updateMovement` frame phase, and the Inspector Movement section, so batch 28 extends `hydra/hydra-movement.js` (same factorySource pattern, same hard load guard already in place, no new module file, no new load-order fallout for the export-boot / flagship / playtest / fps-export suites). Per-object wall config on `obj.movement`: `wallSlideMaxFall` 0–2000 px/s (default 0, no slide — the old behavior exactly), `wallJumpSpeed` 0–100 (default 0, no wall jump), `wallJumpPush` 0–100 (default 8). `normalizeMovementConfig` / `movementConfigOf` extended (now 6 fields). Wall contact is a live geometric read: `wallSides(obj, hooks)` returns the touched sides (side edge within `max(5, |velX|+3)` of a solid object's side, vertical overlap, collision layers respected; a floor underfoot never counts as a wall, a one-way floor never blocks from the side, tilemap walls do not count yet). `isWallSliding(obj, hooks)` requires slide configured, falling, airborne, touching a wall, and pressing toward it (arrow keys / A-D via `hooks.keys` + `hooks.hasKey`). The slide cap applies in `updateMovement` as `velY = min(velY, wallSlideMaxFall/60)` — px/s converted to the engine's velocity units (px per 60fps frame). The wall jump is performed by the existing **Jump** block (no separate Wall Jump action block): `tryJump` order is ground → coyote → wall → air → buffered; `tryWallJump` costs one air jump (`jumpsUsed < maxJumps` required) and one wall jump per wall — record field `lastWallSide` (`'left'`/`'right'`/null), set on wall jump, cleared on landing in `updateMovement`; touching another wall allows another jump. When the wall branch refuses (same wall), the air jump still fires if one is left. Push: `velX = ±wallJumpPush` away from the wall, `velY = -wallJumpSpeed` (the configured wall speed, not the Jump block's speed).
- **Slide placement fix:** the slide cap was first placed inside the record loop, which meant a hero that never jumped/landed (no record yet) would never slide. It now lives in a standalone loop after the record loop in `updateMovement` — a live read for every object, record or not. Pinned by a unit check (no-record slide).
- Three new blocks in the **Movement** category (476 total, was 473) — **Set Wall Slide** (Object + Max fall speed, placeholder 60) / **Set Wall Jump** (Object + Jump speed 12 + Push away 8) actions and the invertible **Wall Sliding** condition (`level` trigger default, evaluates while paused), placed after Is Dashing. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (bad numbers warn and hold; missing object stays quiet), `hydra-conditions.js` (new `movementHooks(ctx)` + `wallSliding` case with a raw-field fallback mirroring the module, incl. the tile leg), `hydra-flow.js` (`wallSliding` → level), Inspector Movement section gains Wall slide max fall (px/s) 0–2000 step 10, Wall jump speed 0–100 step 1, Wall jump push 0–100 step 1. Frame-phase hooks in `hydra-previewruntime.js` (module-level `inputState()` + `keySetHas`, passes `keys: heldKeys, hasKey: keySetHas`) and `player-runtime.js` (`keys: state.keys, hasKey: (set,key) => HydraInput.keySetHas(set,key)`). `hydra-universal.js` needed no change (calls the extended `normalizeMovementConfig`). Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): walls are solid objects only; slide reads arrow keys/A-D directly, not remapped action bindings; wall-jump push is a velocity set once, so if the game sets horizontal velocity from held keys every frame the push lasts until the next frame's movement row — steer away after jumping; pressing jump against the same wall again spends a normal air jump if one is left; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bug fixed in-cycle:** my `hydra-conditions.js` edit accidentally renamed `platformHooks` to `movementHooks`, breaking 6 standingOnPlatform/standingOnOneWay checks. Fixed by restoring `platformHooks` unchanged and adding `movementHooks` as a separate function.
- **Export-test findings (Phase CA, `tests/export-boot.test.js`):** the slide, wall jump, same-wall refusal, and bad-value warnings all passed on first run; the 6 failures were test-side, not engine-side — (1) ArrowRight was still held after the wall jump, so the hero kept sliding at 1 px/frame and never reached the floor in 60 frames (fixed by releasing the key before the landing pump); (2) the bad-value grid had only the bad set, so the hero had no `movement` field at all (fixed by setting 60 first, then the bad value, and asserting the good write held); (3) the scene-switch record check expected an empty movement state, but the cycle-17 mint loop gives the scene-2 wall (standing on the floor) a record — harmless pre-existing behavior, so the check now asserts the new-scene hero (id 5) has no record and the wall's `lastWallSide` is null; (4) the new-scene hero reads Max Jumps 2, not the default 1, because the cycle-15 gameStart-fires-once-per-scene rule re-applies the global grid's Max Jumps 2 to the scene-2 hero — correct, assertion updated with a comment.
- Tests: `tests/movement.test.js` 74/74 (was 58; wall normalizers, config, wallSides/touchingWall/inputDirX/isWallSliding/tryWallJump, tryJump wall branch, landing-clears-memory, slide-cap frame checks incl. no-record slide, setWallSlide/setWallJump writers, routing 7 actions/3 conditions, static pins incl. catalog 10 blocks, flow level pin, docs, inspector wall fields), `tests/execute.test.js` 442/442, `tests/conditions.test.js` 414/414, `tests/previewruntime.test.js` 559/559, `tests/catalog.test.js` / `tests/docs.test.js` / `tests/flow.test.js` green, export-boot Phase CA 10/10, browser-smoke movement checks green. Full suite green before commit (see cycle-19 visual QA below).
- Visual QA (cycle 19, script `work/movement-visual-qa.js`): (1) palette shows all ten Movement blocks in order (Jump, Dash, Set Max Jumps, Set Coyote Time, Set Jump Buffer, Jumps Left, Is Dashing, Set Wall Slide, Set Wall Jump, Wall Sliding); (2) Set Wall Slide places with cell label "Wall Slide" and its panel shows Object and Max fall speed ("60"); (3) Set Wall Jump places with cell label "Wall Jump" and its panel shows Object, Jump speed ("12"), and Push away ("8"); (4) Wall Sliding places with cell label "Sliding?" and its panel shows Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level hint, Object, and the Invert checkbox; (5) Inspector Movement section on a sprite shows the three wall fields — Wall slide max fall (px/s) "0", Wall jump speed "0", Wall jump push "8" (defaults, with Max jumps / Coyote time / Jump buffer above them in the same section, verified in the panel text); (6) live preview with pixel probes: the hero slides down the wall at the capped 1 px/frame fall with the Wall Sliding condition firing (sliding variable reads 1), then wall-jumps off it (velX -8, hero visibly airborne left of the wall). Zero page errors; the only console errors are the sandbox's Font Awesome CDN tunnel failures, filtered the same way browser-smoke filters them. QA-script findings, neither an engine defect: the hero falls from spawn to the floor in ~0.4s, so the slide must be shown by jumping from the floor and sliding on the way down; and the sim runs ~2x realtime headless, so the wall jump's velY<0 rise lasts ~65ms of wall time — the check asserts the persistent velX -8 push instead. Known pre-existing non-defects, unchanged: first placement lands in grid cell 0 left of gameStart, rendering half off the left edge; inspector tab labels truncate ("Localiza…", "Achieve…"); Diagnostics dock overlaps bottom of long tab panels; "Desktop" device pill clipped at right edge; preview canvas is CSS-scaled (1077×808 displayed for 800×600 buffer) so canvas clicks must scale by displayed bounding box; Diagnostics dock warnings in preview shots are pre-existing collector-project cross-scene references (blocks 543/541/483), not movement blocks.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 18 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 27 — moving platforms & one-way platforms COMPLETED (recovered)

- **Recovery:** the first cycle-18 run (run `ed153676`) implemented batch 27 in full and then hit the 2-hour agent timeout mid-batch and died, leaving the work uncommitted in the tree (34 files, 1585 insertions, no PROGRESS.md entry, no commit). This run (`ee9caaec`) picked the tree up per the step-2 rule: inspected the diff, re-ran the FULL suite (first run failed only on the known gltf ETIMEDOUT load flake documented in the `tests/run.js` header — gltf passes standalone with all 40 checks — and the re-run went all green including the smoke phase), re-inspected all 9 visual-QA screenshots the interrupted cycle had captured, and committed the batch. No code in the batch was changed by the recovery; the only new edits are this PROGRESS entry and the ROADMAP DONE line.
- Batch 27 (Unity moving platforms / Godot platforms, beginner edition): the natural next step after movement abilities — the hero can double-jump, coyote-jump, buffer, and dash, now the ground itself moves. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-platform.js` (`HydraPlatform`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-movement.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): per-object platform config (path points, speed, ping-pong/loop mode, one-way flag, crumble delay, authored on the object with an Inspector **Platform** section) plus transient runtime refreshed by a new `updatePlatforms` frame phase (after `updateSpawners`, before `updateMovement`, so a newborn on a platform rides it from its first frame and the grounded check sees the rider on the platform's new position). Five new blocks in a new **Platform** category (473 total, was 468) — **Set Platform Path** (Object + Point 1 X/Y + Point 2 X/Y + Speed + Mode Ping-pong/Loop; turning it on with no path yet seeds one from where the object stands, 200 pixels to the right and back) / **Set One-Way Platform** (Object + One-way On/Off; flips the same flag the Inspector Collision section sets, works on any object, platform or plain floor) / **Set Crumble Time** (Object + Seconds; 0 means it never crumbles; once the timer starts it runs to the end even if the rider steps off) actions and the invertible **Standing On Platform** / **Standing On One-Way** conditions (`level` trigger default, evaluate while paused). The carry is positional: only objects standing directly on the platform ride it. A parked floor with a crumble time falls away underfoot too. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (a bad path value warns and holds; a missing object stays quiet), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallbacks without the module), `hydra-flow.js` (level pins), `hydra-frame.js` phase, `hydra-scenereset.js` hook (fresh state per scene load — every platform back at its first waypoint, no crumble timer running), `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `player-runtime.js` contexts/hooks, `hydra-universal.js` platform repair in `normalizeObject`. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): platforms move on straight legs between their points, no curves; the carry is positional, so only objects standing directly on the platform ride it; a platform moves by direct position steps, so it can sit inside a thin wall for a frame before collision pushes it out; one-way platforms block from above only, never from the side or below; a crumbled platform stays gone until the scene reloads; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bug fixed in-cycle:** jumping up through a one-way platform used to grant a free ground jump — the grounded check in `hydra-movement.js` counted the platform while the hero rose through it. The grounded check now skips a one-way floor while the object rises (`velY < 0`), so the jump through is an air jump and landing on top refills the jumps like any other floor. Caught by the new export tests, pinned by a unit check and an export sub-test.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-platform.js` first. `work/platform-fallout.py` inserted the load after each `hydra-movement.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, `tests/flagship.test.js`, `tests/playtest.test.js`, `tests/fps-export.test.js` (verified 0 unfollowed loads, 0 duplicates, `node --check` on all four files; idempotent).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 17 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 26 — movement abilities (double jump, coyote time, jump buffer & dash) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **movement abilities** (Unity character controllers / Godot platformer movement / Celeste game feel, beginner edition): the natural next step in the action-game chain — health (batch 21) → damage feedback (batch 22) → checkpoints & respawn (batch 23) → enemy spawner (batch 24) → lives & game over (batch 25) → the player needs the moves to survive. Until now jumping meant an Apply Force preset or Set Velocity plus a hand-wired On Floor gate, with no second jump, no grace after walking off a ledge, and no dash at all. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-movement.js` (`HydraMovement`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-lives.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeMovementConfig` / `movementConfigOf` (per-object `maxJumps` 1–10 whole, default 1; `coyoteSeconds` 0–1, default 0; `bufferSeconds` 0–1, default 0 — old objects default to a single jump with no coyote or buffer, exactly the old behavior) / `createMovementState` (per-object records, fresh per scene load via the scene-reset hook, never in project JSON or save slots) / `recordOf` / `isGrounded` (standing on a solid object floor under the shared grounded tolerance, or on a solid tilemap tile, so the moves work on tile levels too) / `tryJump` (returns `'ground' | 'coyote' | 'air' | 'buffered' | null`; a press with no jump left is buffered and fires on landing inside the window) / `startDash` (moves the object directly at an exact speed for the duration, velocity zeroed during the burst, refused while already dashing or cooling down) / `isDashing` / `canDash` / `updateMovement` (new frame phase, LAST in the world sim after `updateSpawners`; landing refills jumps and fires a buffered press) / `jumpsLeft` / `setMaxJumps` / `setCoyoteTime` / `setJumpBuffer` / `isMovementAction` / `isMovementCondition`. The strict `saneNum` is shared with the lives/spawn modules: only real numbers or numeric strings count, so a hostile array reads as the fallback instead of 0 (the `Number([])` is 0 trap from cycle 13). Time base is the frozen game clock in seconds (hosts pass `frozenNowMs()/1000`; coyote/buffer/dash windows hold while paused; the frame phase runs only while unpaused). Seven new blocks in a new **Movement** category (468 total, was 461) — **Jump** (Object + Jump speed 12) / **Dash** (Object + Direction Left/Right/Up/Down + Speed 400 + Duration 0.15 + Cooldown 0.5) / **Set Max Jumps** (Jumps 2) / **Set Coyote Time** (Seconds 0.1) / **Set Jump Buffer** (Seconds 0.1) actions and the invertible **Jumps Left** (Compare + Value 1, `level` trigger) / **Is Dashing** (`level` trigger) conditions, both evaluating while paused. Inspector **Movement** section in `script.js` (Max jumps / Coyote time (s) / Jump buffer (s), same gating as Health: skips 3D kinds, lights, tilemaps). Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (a bad value warns and holds; a missing object stays quiet; Jump/Dash stay quiet with no live state; the Set blocks need no state so they still write; `now` from `ctx.frozenNowMs()/1000`), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallbacks without the module), `hydra-flow.js` (`jumpsLeft` / `isDashing` → level), `hydra-frame.js` phase, `hydra-scenereset.js` hook, `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `player-runtime.js` contexts/hooks (new `ensurePreviewMovementState` host entry in `script.js`, next to `ensurePreviewSpawnerState` — the cycle-13 lesson), `hydra-universal.js` movement repair in `normalizeObject`. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one dash direction per Dash block (no diagonal or facing-relative dash); the dash moves the object directly, so it can sit inside a thin wall for a frame before collision pushes it out; jumps never refill on an object that cannot be grounded (collision off), so use Set Velocity for flying objects; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-movement.js` first. `work/movement-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 16's `lives-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-lives.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+25 one-line + 63 list inserts), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1 one-line — the bare-`sandbox` form the old regex missed; this script's regex handles both `foo.sandbox` and bare `sandbox`). Verified: 0 unfollowed loads, 0 duplicates, `node --check` on all four files. The script is idempotent (second run 0 inserts).
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** three REAL engine bugs, all caught by the new export tests, not by the unit tests: (1) **refill refund** — right after a jump the hero is still inside the grounded tolerance (the tolerance grows with |velY|: velY −12 means a 15px tolerance), so the landing-refill fired on the next frame and refunded the jump — a double jump never actually consumed, and a buffered press fired an air jump instead of buffering. Fixed: the grounded branch (refill + `lastGroundedAt` + buffered fire) now runs only when `grounded && velY >= 0` — a hero standing or falling onto the floor counts as landed; a rising hero does not. (2) **coyote triple jump** — the coyote branch fired even after a jump was already spent, so ground jump + coyote refresh + air jump = three boosts on two jumps. Fixed: the coyote branch requires `jumpsUsed === 0`. Coyote is the grace for leaving the ground WITHOUT jumping (walked off a ledge). (3) **no record for walk-offs** — the frame phase only iterated existing records, so a hero that never jumped had no record and `lastGroundedAt` was never tracked, so coyote could never fire for the walk-off case it was designed for. Fixed: before the record loop, mint a record for each object standing on the floor. Only grounded objects mint, so a fresh scene still reads as an empty movement state. The unit tests were updated for the fixes: the old coyote test encoded the buggy behavior (ground jump, then "coyote" at 0.05s) and is rewritten to the true walk-off scenario, plus new checks for no triple jump and no refill while rising. (4) Test-side: adding `ensurePreviewMovementState` to the script.js host list broke the exact-substring pins in `tests/damagefx.test.js`, `tests/respawn.test.js`, and `tests/spawn.test.js` — all three updated to the new list (the same stale-pin shape cycles 14–16 hit; grep for the host-list string after editing it). (5) Test-side: after a `bxPress` (press frame + released frame) the released frame's physics adds gravity (+1.5), so velY reads −10.5, not −12 — the export sub-tests now use a press-frame-only `byTap` helper for exact velY −12 reads. (6) QA-script lessons, neither an engine defect: the jump-trace rise counter missed the first rise because sampling started after the press (the trace has no pre-press baseline) — the probe now samples a baseline, presses the second Space at ~150ms while still airborne, and asserts the air jump carries the hero higher than the first peak plus continuous air past a single jump's ~270ms; the console-error check now filters CDN resource failures the same way the smoke test does (`Failed to load resource` / `net::ERR_` are environmental, the sandbox has no external network); the findHero canvas scan steps by 2 in both axes (4x faster) so the 50ms sampling loop does not miss both jump peaks; and the dash screenshot kept missing the burst because a Playwright element screenshot captures its frame at the END of the call (~150–250ms later), so the demo burst is 0.6s (the 0.15s default is covered by the export tests), the pixel probes run before the screenshot, and the distance check is 200–280px for the 240px nominal.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 16 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 25 — lives & game over COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **lives & game over** (Unity lives systems / Godot lives counters, beginner edition): the natural next step in the action-game chain — health (batch 21) → damage feedback (batch 22) → checkpoints & respawn (batch 23) → enemy spawner (batch 24) → the comebacks run out. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-lives.js` (`HydraLives`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-spawn.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `createLivesState` / `normalizeLives` (0–99 whole, default 3) / `normalizeLivesAmount` (0–99 whole, default 1) / `livesOf` (missing state reads 3, negative reads 0) / `isGameOver` (derived, never stored — lives ≤ 0) / `setLives` / `addLives` (clamps at 99) / `loseLives` (clamps at 0, null on a missing state) / `isLivesAction` / `isLivesCondition`. The strict `saneNum` is shared with the spawn module: only real numbers or numeric strings count, so a hostile array reads as the fallback instead of 0 (the `Number([])` is 0 trap from cycle 13). Six new blocks in a new **Lives** category (461 total, was 455) — **Set Lives** (Lives expression, default 3) / **Add Lives** (Amount, default 1) / **Lose Life** (Amount, default 1) / **Read Lives** (Into variable) actions and the invertible **Lives** (operator + value, `>= 1` default, `level` trigger) / **Game Over** (`edge` trigger — fires once when the count hits 0, holds while it stays 0, and fires again after a continue tops the count back up and it hits 0 again) conditions, both evaluating while paused. Lives are per-run state: they survive scene switches inside a run (like the quest log and the backpack) and reset only on a fresh run — deliberately NO scene-reset hook, pinned by a scenereset test that the reset leaves them untouched; they never travel in save slots (persist them through Read Lives into a variable, the way quests and inventory do). Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (a bad value or a blank variable warns and holds; writes stay quiet without a live state; Read Lives without a state reads the default 3), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallbacks without the module), `hydra-flow.js` (`lives` → level, `gameOver` → edge), `hydra-previewruntime.js` (lazy `livesRt` minted at 3 on first use, dropped by `resetLivesRuntime()` in `resetGame` — the quest pattern, no `script.js` host entry needed), `player-runtime.js` contexts. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one lives count per run, not per player or per object; no built-in lives HUD (show the count by reading it into a text object); Game Over fires once per fall to 0 and does not itself stop the game — the game decides what game over means (switch to a game-over scene, show a panel); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-lives.js` first. `work/lives-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 15's `spawn-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-spawn.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+87), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1); `tests/fps-export.test.js` needed the same hand fix as cycle 15 (its load uses the bare `sandbox` form the regex misses — fixed manually). Verified: 0 unfollowed loads, 0 duplicates, run counts match (export-boot 25/25, fps-export 1/1), the script is idempotent, `node --check` passed on all four files.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** both test-side, not engine: (1) same-key repeat presses in the export harness did not fire the second time — the `keyJustPressed` edge memory (`HydraFlow.shouldTrigger`) only resets when the condition evaluates false, and the `bxPress` helper pumped only the pressed frame. Fixed by pumping one extra released frame after each press (in a real game the 60fps loop provides those frames); the lesson is recorded here for the next harness author. (2) My own smoke-test filter bug: `'lose-life'` does not contain `'live'`, so the block-order check now uses an explicit block-type list. (3) QA-script lessons, neither an engine defect: catalog condition panels prepend a Trigger select (edge/level) before the fields, so "first select" is the Trigger select — the QA script now picks a select by the option value it must contain; and clicking a placed block by type picks the template's same-type block first, so the script now places by cell index (diffing the live `cellData` keys) and clicks the exact cell. (4) The first QA probe run failed the game-over half because the click used the canvas buffer scale (1) while the preview CSS-scales the canvas (1077x808 displayed); the click now scales by the displayed bounding box.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 15 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 24 — enemy spawner / wave spawning COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **enemy spawner / wave spawning** (Unity spawn systems / Godot spawners, beginner edition): the natural next step in the action-game chain — health (batch 21) → damage feedback (batch 22) → checkpoints & respawn (batch 23) → enemies that keep coming. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-spawn.js` (`HydraSpawn`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-respawn.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `createSpawnerState` / `startSpawner` (blank name or blank template stays quiet; restarting a running spawner replaces it with a fresh timer and count) / `stopSpawner` (blank name stops all; unknown name is a no-op) / `stopAllSpawners` / `spawnerActive` (false once the total is reached, even before the next tick notices) / `spawnedCount` (missing reads 0, negative reads 0) / `spawnerOf` / `updateSpawners` (dt <= 0 ages nothing, so the first frame spawns nothing; the first spawn lands after one full interval, never at once; at most 25 spawns per spawner per frame, so a long hitch banks leftover time instead of bursting; the max-alive cap counts every live object with the template's name however it got there, and a capped spawner waits and retries next interval; reaching the total deactivates the spawner on the same tick the last one lands; a missing named anchor holds the spawn with no X/Y fallback and retries next interval; a missing template (spawn hook returns null) counts nothing and retries quietly; missing or hostile hooks degrade quietly; nothing ever throws). The clone's **top-left corner** lands at the spawn point (the anchor object's top-left corner, or the X/Y point when no object is named) — consistent with Set Checkpoint. Normalizers: interval 0.1–3600 seconds (default 2), max alive 0–1000 whole (default 10, 0 = unlimited), total 0–10000 whole (default 0 = endless). The module's `saneNum` is strict: only real numbers or numeric strings count, so a hostile array reads as the fallback instead of 0 (the `Number([])` is 0 trap from cycle 13; the respawn module's loose `saneNum` was left alone). Five new blocks in a new **Spawner** category (455 total, was 450) — **Start Spawner** (Name + Spawn object + At object + X + Y + Every (seconds) + Max alive + Total; `templateObject`/`atObject` are NOT in `HydraGridModel.buildBlockDefaults` auto-fill list, so a fresh block stores blank and the X/Y path stays reachable — the cycle-14 checkpointObject lesson) / **Stop Spawner** (Name, blank stops all) / **Read Spawned Count** (Name + Into variable; a never-started spawner reads 0; a blank variable name warns and holds) actions and the invertible **Spawner Active** / **Spawned Count** (operator + value, `>=` default) conditions (`level` triggers, evaluate while paused). New frame phase `updateSpawners` in `hydra-frame.js`, LAST in the world sim after `updatePaths` (a newborn never gets a half-stepped physics/AI update on its birth frame); runs while unpaused only, follows the time scale, freezes while paused. Spawning runs through the existing Spawn Object action (`HydraExecute.spawnObjectAt`, new export; `spawnClone` now returns the last clone made, or null). A spawner never sets an AI behavior or a path on what it spawns — chain Set AI Behavior after the spawn if the newborn should chase. Spawner state is transient run state: a fresh state per scene load (via the `createSpawnerState` scene-reset hook), never in project JSON or save slots; a wave from level 1 never keeps spawning into level 2, and gameStart fires once per scene, so a global-grid spawner restarts fresh (count 0) in the new scene. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (a bad interval/maxAlive/total/X/Y warns and holds, a blank name/template stays quiet), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallbacks without the module), `hydra-flow.js` level pins, `hydra-scenereset.js` hook, `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `player-runtime.js` contexts/hooks (spawn via `spawnObjectAt`, anchor via `named(state, name, true)[0]`, alive count via `named(state, template, true).length` — hidden included deliberately), `script.js` (`ensurePreviewSpawnerState` in the preview host list — the cycle-13 lesson: the host entry must be present or the preview silently does nothing). Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one spawn per interval tick per spawner; the clone's top-left corner lands at the spawn point, not the center; max alive counts every live object with the template's name however it got there; a named anchor missing or destroyed holds the spawn until it exists again; no AI or path is set on spawns; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-spawn.js` first. `work/spawn-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 14's `respawn-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-respawn.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+86), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1); `tests/fps-export.test.js` needed a hand fix (+1 runInContext) because its load uses the bare `sandbox` form the script's pattern does not match (the same form cycle 14 hand-fixed). Verified with `work/spawn-fallout-verify.py`: 0 unfollowed loads, 0 duplicates, run counts match, `node --check` on all four files.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) the module's first `spawn.test.js` run failed one check — `normalizeInterval([])` read 0 instead of the default 2, the `Number([])` is 0 trap — fixed by making this module's `saneNum` strict (only real numbers or numeric strings count). (2) Phase BW first failed 7 assertions, and the cause was the test grid, not the engine: Spawner Active and Spawned Count are level conditions, true every frame, and a level condition re-triggers the action in the cell directly above it — so with Spawner Active sitting directly under Start Spawner, the spawner restarted (timer and count reset) every frame and never spawned. The debug script proved it (startSpawner called once per frame, timer stuck at 0; the module itself spawned fine when ticked by hand). Fixed by putting a blank guard row between Start Spawner and the level conditions in the BW grid, and the guard-row rule is now written into the test comment — any future level condition must never sit directly under a non-idempotent action. (3) The same debug run settled the scene-switch semantics: gameStart fires once per scene, so the global grid's Start Spawner restarts the spawner fresh (spawned 0) in the new scene instead of the record vanishing — the BW sub-test now asserts the fresh count, not absence. (4) The wave sub-test failed on `active` because the total was only noticed on the next tick; the module now deactivates the record on the same tick the last spawn lands, so Spawner Active reads false the moment the wave ends — pinned by a new unit check. (5) QA-script lesson, not an engine defect: the first pixel probe scanned the whole canvas for coin yellow, so the template coin at (520,320) and the spawned pile at (150,500) merged into one bounding box and the center check failed; the probe now scans only a window around the spawn point. (6) The first full-suite run failed only `tests/damagefx.test.js` and `tests/respawn.test.js` on the cycle-13 host-list pin, which still expected the exact string `ensurePreviewParticleState, ensurePreviewDamageNumberState, ensurePreviewRespawnState, getPreviewBuses` — the spawner host entry now sits between the respawn entry and `getPreviewBuses`, so both pins were updated to the new list (the same stale-pin shape cycle 14 hit with the respawn entry).

new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BW (10 sub-tests, 29 checks: inlining, missing-module guard, gameStart starts the spawner with Spawner Active firing, 2 goblins spawn on the interval at the pad's top-left corner (400,300) with Spawned Count firing, stop holds the count and Read Spawned Count copies it into a variable, cap holds at 3 bats with the template counting as one alive, wave spawns exactly its total then stops, bad interval warns-and-holds with exactly 2 diagnostics, missing template/anchor stay quiet with a never-started spawner reading 0, scene switch restarts the spawner fresh with a zero count); `tests/execute.test.js` (+14: start/restart, bad interval/X/Y warn-and-hold with exactly 1 warning, X/Y skipped when an anchor is named, blank name/template quiet, stop one / stop all / unknown quiet, read incl. never-started reads 0 and blank variable warns, quiet without the module or a live state); `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 450 to 455); `tests/ai.test.js` + `tests/pathfind.test.js` (phase-order pins extended to the new order); `tests/docs.test.js` (block reference + user guide); browser-smoke checks (+11: module, palette presence + Spawner category + Start/Stop/Read/Active/Count DOM order, Start Spawner placement + Spawner name / Spawn object / At object / Every / Max alive / Total panel, Spawned Count placement + Spawner name / Compare / Value / Invert panel, Stop Spawner / Read Spawned Count / Spawner Active placement). ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 14 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 23 — checkpoints & respawn COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **checkpoints & respawn** (Unity checkpoint/respawn systems / Godot respawn points, beginner edition): the natural next step in the action-game chain — health (batch 21) → damage feedback (batch 22) → coming back after dying. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-respawn.js` (`HydraRespawn`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-damagefx.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `createRespawnState` / `setCheckpoint` (a new checkpoint replaces the old — one checkpoint per scene; non-finite position reads null) / `clearCheckpoint` / `hasCheckpoint` / `checkpointOf` / `recordInitialPositions` (fills `initial[name] = {x, y}` from top-left corners, first object per name wins) / `initialPositionOf` / `respawnPosition` (checkpoint first, else the object's start position, else null — returns a copy so callers cannot mutate the checkpoint) / `noteRespawn` / `respawnCount` (negative reads 0) / `isRespawnAction` / `isRespawnCondition`. The respawning object's **top-left corner** lands at the checkpoint. Five new blocks in a new **Respawn** category (450 total, was 445) — **Set Checkpoint** (Checkpoint object + X + Y; the field is named `checkpointObject`, NOT in `HydraGridModel.buildBlockDefaults` auto-fill list, so a fresh block stores blank and the X/Y path stays reachable — the cycle-8 lesson) / **Respawn Object** (Object + Restore health checkbox, default true; teleports to the checkpoint or start position, zeroes `velX`/`velY` and `vel3x`/`vel3y`/`vel3z` when present, stops a walking path via `pathfindModule.stopPath`, refills health to full via `healthModule` when Restore health is ticked) / **Clear Checkpoint** (settings-free block; its note renders via the cycle-11 fallback-note fix) actions and the invertible **Checkpoint Set** / **Respawn Count** (operator + value, `>=` default) conditions (`level` triggers, evaluate while paused). Behavior rules: no checkpoint and no recorded start stays quiet; AI deliberately keeps running after a respawn (the brain re-plans from the new spot); the Make Invincible shield is deliberately left alone (chain Make Invincible after Respawn Object for a spawn shield). Respawn state is transient run state: a fresh state per scene load (via the `createRespawnState` scene-reset hook), never in project JSON or save slots. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch (a bad X/Y warns and holds, a missing object stays quiet), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallbacks without the module), `hydra-flow.js` level pins, `hydra-scenereset.js` hook, `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `player-runtime.js` contexts/hooks (start positions recorded after `initSceneObjects` in `loadScene`, in both `loadPreviewScene` and `launchPreview` before `processGameStartConditions`), `script.js` (`ensurePreviewRespawnState` in the preview host list next to the particle and damage-number entries — the cycle-13 lesson: the host entry must be present or the preview silently does nothing). Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one checkpoint per scene; the respawn point is the top-left corner, not the center; AI keeps running after a respawn; the shield is left alone; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-respawn.js` first. `work/respawn-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 13's `damagefx-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-damagefx.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+60 array, +25 runInContext), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1 runInContext — hand-added after the script missed that form), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array); verified 0 unfollowed loads, 0 duplicates, and `node --check` on all four files. The script's first run failed on a malformed verification regex (unbalanced paren) AFTER the inserts had been written; the verification was simplified to a count comparison and the script re-run — it is idempotent, and the final state was verified rather than assumed.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** one, test-side only, not engine: the first full-suite run failed only `tests/damagefx.test.js` on the cycle-13 host-list pin, which still expected the exact string `ensurePreviewParticleState, ensurePreviewDamageNumberState, getPreviewBuses` — the respawn host entry now sits between the damage-number entry and `getPreviewBuses`, so the pin was updated to the new list (the same stale-pin shape cycle 13 hit with the frame phase-order pins). No engine bug was found this cycle. Notable finding while starting the smoke work: `tests/browser-smoke.js` contains NO damage-feedback checks despite cycle 13's PROGRESS.md claiming "+11" smoke checks — those +11 were the Health (batch 21) checks, so cycle 13 apparently added no smoke checks for damagefx; this cycle adds respawn smoke checks following the health pattern, and the damagefx gap stays open for a later cycle.
- **Tests:** new `tests/respawn.test.js` (41 checks, all green: state minting, checkpoint set/clear/replace, start-position recording incl. first-wins and bad entries, respawn position incl. copy semantics, count incl. negative-reads-0, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring pins incl. index.html order, guard, inlining, dispatch/contexts/scene-reset in both runtimes, catalog, editor wiring incl. the host-entry pin, and docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BV (9 sub-tests: inlining, missing-module guard, gameStart checkpoint at the flag (500,100) + Checkpoint Set firing + start positions recorded, kill → respawn at the checkpoint with full health + zero velocity + Respawn Count firing, clear → respawn home (100,100) + count 2, bad X/Y warns-and-holds with exactly 2 diagnostics, missing object quiet, scene switch mints fresh state + records the new scene's start positions, respawn with no checkpoint ever set goes home); `tests/execute.test.js` (+10: respawn actions incl. velocity zeroing, path stop, health restore, missing object quiet, quiet without the module); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+8: checkpointSet / respawnCount incl. operators, reversed, not frozen while paused, raw fallback); `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+9: contexts carry the module, state minting through the host entry, null without the module/host); `tests/flow.test.js` (level pins); `tests/scenereset.test.js` (fresh state + no-aliasing + missing/throwing-hook checks); `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 445 to 450); `tests/docs.test.js` (block reference + user guide); browser-smoke checks (+11: module, palette presence + Respawn category + Set Checkpoint / Respawn Object / Clear Checkpoint / Checkpoint Set / Respawn Count DOM order, Set Checkpoint placement + Checkpoint object / X / Y panel, Respawn Object placement + Object / Restore health panel, Respawn Count placement + Compare / Value / Invert panel). ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 13 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 22 — damage feedback (floating numbers / invincibility frames / knockback) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **damage feedback** (Unity damage numbers / invincibility frames / knockback, beginner edition): the hit has to *feel* like a hit. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-damagefx.js` (`HydraDamageFx`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-health.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `createDamageNumberState` / `spawnDamageNumber` (cap 100, oldest dropped first; null on missing state/spec/position/empty text) / `updateDamageNumbers` (rise 48px/s, TTL 0.9s, fade over the last 0.3s; dt <= 0 holds still) / `drawDamageNumbers` (bold 16px sans, centered, dark 3px outline, world-space at x-camX/y-camY, culled outside the view + 40px margin, returns the count drawn) / `makeInvincible` / `isInvincible` / `invincibleRemainingSeconds` (on the frozen game clock; 0 seconds clears) / `normalizeInvincibleSeconds` (0–60, default 1) / `normalizeKnockbackForce` (0–5000, default 300) / `knockbackDirection` (unit vector away from the source; same-point or non-finite input pushes straight up) / `isDamageFxAction` / `isDamageFxCondition`. The strict `strictNum` coercion helper is shared by the normalizers: only real numbers or numeric strings count, so a hostile array reads as the default instead of 0 (the `Number([])` is 0 trap). Four new blocks in the **Health** category (445 total, was 441) — **Show Damage Number** (Object + Amount + Color, default `#ffffff`; display only, never changes health) / **Make Invincible** (Object + Seconds, default 1) / **Knockback Object** (Object + From object + X/Y + Force, default 300; pushes away from the source object, or from the X/Y point when blank; same spot pushes straight up) actions and the invertible **Is Invincible** condition (`level` trigger default, evaluates while paused). Behavior rules: Damage Object skips invincible targets quietly (Heal / Set Health still apply to shielded objects); the gate reads the frozen clock and degrades safely (missing clock reads now as 0, so a shielded object stays shielded); knockback moves physics-enabled, non-static objects only, through the shared `applyVelocity` add path. Numbers are transient run state: a fresh empty list per scene load (via the `createDamageNumbers` scene-reset hook, null without it), never in project JSON or save slots. New frame phase `updateDamageNumbers` in `hydra-frame.js` (after `updateParticles`, before `updateAI`; runs while unpaused only, slows with the time scale); drawn in `hydra-draw.js` after the particles pass, before the darkness overlay (world-space, zoom-scaled, below the HUD). Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` dispatch + invincibility gate in the damageObject branch, `hydra-conditions.js` (`isInvincible` with a raw-field fallback without the module), `hydra-flow.js` level pin, `hydra-frame.js` phase, `hydra-scenereset.js` hook, `hydra-draw.js` pass, `hydra-previewruntime.js` + `player-runtime.js` contexts/hooks/draw, `script.js` (`ensurePreviewDamageNumberState`, draw view/hooks). Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): numbers are plain text popups (no crit styling, no per-number size); invincibility blocks Damage Object only (not Heal / Set Health, not physics or timers); knockback needs a physics-enabled, non-static object and the object's max speed caps the push; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-damagefx.js` first. `work/damagefx-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 12's `health-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-health.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+84), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1); verified 0 unfollowed loads, 0 array duplicates, every runInContext damagefx line preceded by its health line, and `node --check` on all four files.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) a REAL preview bug caught by visual QA, not by the unit tests: `ensurePreviewDamageNumberState()` existed in `script.js` but was never handed to the preview runtime host (the host list had `ensurePreviewParticleState` only), so `hostCall('ensurePreviewDamageNumberState')` returned null and Show Damage Number silently did nothing in the editor preview while working in exports (Phase BU proves the export path). Fixed by adding it to the host list next to the particle entry; pinned by a new static check in `tests/damagefx.test.js` ("editor hands the number-state maker to the preview runtime host"). The unit tests missed it because `previewruntime.test.js` stubs the host entry itself — a reminder that a stubbed host entry is not the real wiring. (2) The first full-suite run failed only `tests/ai.test.js` on a stale phase-order pin (`'updateTweens', 'updateParticles', 'updateAI', 'updatePaths'` no longer appears once `updateDamageNumbers` sits between them); updated the pin (and the matching one in `tests/pathfind.test.js`) to the new order and the re-run went fully green. (3) Test-side: my first `execute.test.js` draft expected the damage-feedback section's `warnings.length === 1` per bad value, which is right for the unit ctx (no expression-evaluator companion warning there), unlike the export tests where bad values produce exactly 2 diagnostics — kept the unit expectation and pinned the export one in Phase BU. (4) QA-script lesson, not an engine defect: the properties panel writes the live `cellData` at once, but the scene `gridData` only syncs on commit — reading `gridData` right after a panel edit still shows the default (the Amount read 10 until the next commit). The QA script now reads `window.cellData` for the Amount check; a focused probe confirmed the input writes through (input '40', cell 40) in every mode (fill, select+fill, typing+Tab).
- **Tests:** new `tests/damagefx.test.js` (57 checks, all green: number spawn/update/draw incl. cap, rise, TTL, fade, culling, and the recording-ctx draw; invincibility on the frozen clock incl. 0-clears and strict-number coercion; knockback direction incl. same-spot straight-up; routing sets; static wiring pins incl. index.html order, guard, inlining, dispatch/contexts/draw/frame/scene-reset in both runtimes, catalog, editor wiring, and the new host-entry pin; docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BU (13 sub-tests: inline, missing-module guard, shield blocks the hit while the number still spawns and rises, shield expiry after 2s with the next hit landing, knockback velX −300 with the enemy walking left, same-spot knockback straight up, bad seconds/force/amount each warn-and-hold with exactly 2 diagnostics, missing object quiet, number gone after 0.9s, scene switch mints a fresh empty list, recording-proxy draw check); - **Visual QA:** `work/damagefx-visual-qa.js` captured 6 screenshots, all opened and inspected: (1) Health palette shows all ten blocks in Damage / Heal / Set Health / Set Max Health / Health / Died / Show Damage Number / Make Invincible / Knockback Object / Is Invincible order under the HEALTH heading; (2) Show Damage Number places with cell label "Dmg #" and its panel shows Object "player", Amount "10", and the Color swatch, aligned; (3) Knockback Object places with cell label "Knockback" and its panel shows Object "player", From object blank, X/Y "0", Force "300"; (4) Is Invincible places with cell label "Shield?" and its panel shows Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level hint, Object "player", and the Invert checkbox; (5–6) live preview on a dark scene with pixel probes: the white "25" floats above the player at gameStart (65 white pixels, bbox x 95–110 y 45–56, centered on the player), and the bar stays full green (176 green pixels, exactly 44x4, amber 0) because the shield blocks the 25 damage — then the number fades out by 1.6s while the bar stays full. The first QA run FAILED the number probe (0 white pixels) and caught the real preview-host bug above; after the fix the re-run passed all three probes (number, shield bar, Amount "25" in live cellData) with zero page errors. - **Open observation for a later cycle (not a blocker):** knockback travel vs the max-speed clamp. `applyVelocity` clamps each velocity component to the object's maxSpeed (the template player caps at 11), yet the QA run's gameStart knockback (from (0,0), force 300) carried the player from (80,80) to about (496,487) — far more than a clamped (11,11) velocity with per-frame friction 0.78 should travel. The push direction is right and the feature works; the magnitude question is in the pre-existing physics step (friction exponent / dt units), not in the batch-22 knockback code, so it stays an observation, not a fix in this cycle.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 12 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 21 — health & damage (hit points) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **health & damage** (Unity health systems / Godot health components, beginner edition): every action game needs something that can take a hit. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-health.js` (`HydraHealth`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-joystick.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeMaxHealth` (1–10000, default 100) / `normalizeHealthValue` (0..max) / `normalizeHealth` (in place) / `healthOf` / `healthFraction` / `isDead` / `damage` (clamps at 0, `died` flag true exactly on the killing hit) / `heal` (clamps at max) / `setHealth` (clamps 0..max) / `setMaxHealth` (re-clamps current health to the new ceiling) / `healthBarRect` (min width 24, 6px tall, 10px above the top edge) / `healthBarColor` (green over half, amber over a quarter, red below) / `drawHealthBar` (fill inset 1px inside the dark backing rect) / `isHealthAction` / `isHealthCondition`. Death is derived (health <= 0), never stored; Object Died uses the flow edge trigger so it fires once on the killing hit. Six new blocks in a new **Health** category (441 total, was 435) — **Damage Object** / **Heal Object** / **Set Health** / **Set Max Health** actions and the invertible **Health** (operator compare, `>=` default, `level` trigger) / **Object Died** (`edge` trigger) conditions, both evaluating while paused. Wiring: `hydra-execute.js` action dispatch (a bad amount/value warns and holds, a missing object stays quiet), `hydra-conditions.js` (raw-field fallback without the module), `hydra-flow.js` trigger pins, `hydra-draw.js` (bar drawn after the per-kind object draw, through the parallax effective camera), `hydra-universal.js` + export `normalizeObject` health repair, `hydra-previewruntime.js` contexts, and an Inspector **Health** section in `script.js` (Max health 1–10000, Health 0–max, Show health bar checkbox; skipped for 3D kinds, lights, and tilemaps). Health lives on the live object: a fresh run starts at the authored values, damage during a run never rewrites the project, and health never travels in save slots — persist it through a variable the way quests and inventory do. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): no floating damage numbers, no invincibility frames (a second hit lands the next frame — gate it with a timer), no knockback on damage, no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-health.js` first. `work/health-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 11's `joystick-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-joystick.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+25 runInContext, +58 array), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array); verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` on all four files.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** all were test-side or a small editor fix, not engine logic: (1) my first catalog edit accidentally REPLACED the Joystick Tilt block instead of appending after it — restored it verbatim; the catalog diff is now purely additive (92 insertions, 0 deletions) and `validateCatalog()` is clean. (2) my `execute.test.js` health section first expected both heroes damaged, but `targets()` resolves to the FIRST live match by default — fixed the expectation and pinned first-match semantics. (3) my Phase BT export test first asserted `healthy` stays unset after the kill, but the level-triggered Health condition latches `healthy=1` on frames before the kill — restructured into sub-test 6 (kill + edge no-refire) and 6b (starts-dead session).
- **Tests:** new `tests/health.test.js` (42 checks, all green: normalization, reads, damage/heal/set/setMax, bar geometry/color/draw via a recording ctx, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring pins, block reference + user guide docs); `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BT (9 sub-tests: inline, missing-module guard, gameStart Set Max Health 100→50 with health re-clamped, KeyD damage 50→30 with the Health condition firing and Object Died quiet, KeyH heal 30→45, KeyK killing hit → health 0, `died=1`, no re-fire on later frames, starts-dead enemy, bad amount `'oops'` → exactly 2 diagnostics with health unchanged, missing object quiet, health bar drawn via a recording proxy ctx); `tests/execute.test.js` (+~12: first-match damage, warn-and-hold on a bad amount, missing object quiet, quiet without the module); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+13: Health compare operators, Object Died, reversed, not frozen while paused, raw fallback); `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (contexts carry `healthModule`, null without it); `tests/draw.test.js` (+6: amber half-width bar, green full, red sliver, dead/bar-off draw nothing, missing module draws nothing, bar follows the parallax camera); `tests/flow.test.js` (healthCheck → level, objectDied → edge); `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 435 to 441); browser-smoke checks (+11: module, palette presence + Health category + Damage/Heal/Set Health/Set Max Health/Health/Died DOM order, Damage placement + Object/Amount panel, Object Died placement + Object/Invert panel, Inspector Health section with both values at 100 and the bar off). Full suite green (see `suite-cycle12.log` in the workflow workdir).
- **Visual QA:** `work/health-visual-qa.js` captured 6 screenshots, all opened and inspected: (1) Health palette shows all six blocks in Damage / Heal / Set Health / Set Max Health / Health / Died order under the HEALTH heading; (2) Damage Object places with cell label "Damage" and its panel shows Object "player" and Amount "10", aligned; (3) Object Died places with cell label "Died?" and its panel shows Trigger "On become true (once)" with the edge/level hint, Object "player", and the Invert checkbox; (4) Inspector Health section shows Max health 100, Health 100, and the unchecked Show health bar box, below Parallax and above Joints; (5–6) live preview on a dark scene with pixel probes: a full green bar at 50/50 (176 green pixels, exactly 44x4) and an amber half bar after the placed Damage Object block fires 25 at gameStart (88 amber pixels, exactly 22x4, green 0). Two QA-script findings, neither an engine defect: the bar fill is inset 1px inside its backing rect, so the first probe rect miscounted until it scanned the whole canvas; and placing a block auto-targets the first named object (`HydraGridModel.buildBlockDefaults`), so the placed Damage Object fired 10 damage at gameStart in the first run — the script now sets amount 0 for the full-bar shot (committed on blur before the preview runs) and 25 for the damage shot. Only page errors were the known Font Awesome CDN tunnel failures. ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 11 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 20 — virtual joystick (on-screen touch joystick) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented the **virtual joystick** (Unity on-screen joystick / Godot TouchScreenButton + virtual joystick, beginner edition): phone games need on-screen controls now that the mobile wrapper exports have shipped. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-joystick.js` (`HydraJoystick`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-ai.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeJoystickConfig` (in place; radius 20–200 default 60, knob 8–100 default 24, deadzone 0–0.9 default 0.15, anchors bottom-left / bottom-right / custom with 16px edge margin) / `createJoystickRuntime` (live record: enabled, pointerId, baseX/baseY, dx/dy, x/y in -1..1) / `joystickBase` (bottom-left (76,524) and bottom-right (724,524) on 800x600) / `joystickStart` / `joystickMove` (deadzone rescaled so full tilt still reads 1; knob keeps the raw clamped position; pointer ids `'mouse'` and `'touch:<identifier>'`) / `joystickEnd` / `joystickTilt` (unknown direction reads as any) / `joystickHeldActions` (fixed 0.35 threshold, diagonals hold two, blank skipped, deduped) / `applyJoystickOutput` (writes the two variables every frame while on, 0 on release, nothing while off; same name for both axes writes once, horizontal wins) / `drawJoystick` (translucent white base + knob, never throws) / `isJoystickAction` / `isJoystickCondition`. A press only grabs inside the base plus 12px slack, and a grabbed pointer never also clicks a button or fires a touch zone. Four new **Input** blocks in the catalog (435 total, was 431) — **Enable Joystick** / **Disable Joystick** (Disable also releases a held stick) actions and the invertible **Joystick Active** / **Joystick Tilt** conditions (Direction select Any/Up/Down/Left/Right + Min tilt, `level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, evaluate while paused). The stick is project config (`project.virtualJoystick`, defaulted in `hydra-universal.js` when missing or the wrong type; off by default) with a **Joystick** inspector tab (On by default checkbox, Base position select, Custom X/Y, Base/Knob radius, Dead zone, the two variable names, four action selects via the existing `makeActionSelect`) plus `getProjectJoystickConfig` / `getMutableJoystickConfig` in `script.js`. New frame phase `updateJoystick` in `hydra-frame.js` (after `pollInput`, before `runScripts`; runs while paused like the input poll) with matching hooks in both runtimes (`player-runtime.js` mouse/touch bindInput grab + steer + release, `pollInput` passes `sources.joystick`; `hydra-previewruntime.js` lazy runtime, `joystickRuntimeForDraw` gated on a live run, reset in `resetGame`); driven actions feed `HydraActions.update` through optional `sources.joystick`, so held/just-pressed edges ride the existing mechanism. Drawn host-side after `drawFrame`, before the scene-transition overlay, in both runtimes. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): fixed base only; one stick only; fixed 0.35 action threshold; a grabbed pointer cannot click a button under the stick; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Load-order fallout:** the new hard guard means every vm sandbox loading `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-joystick.js` first. `work/joystick-fallout.py` (adapted from cycle 10's `ai-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each `hydra-ai.js` load in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+25 runInContext, +57 array), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array); verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` on all four files.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** all were test-side or a small editor fix, not engine logic: (1) my `joystick.test.js` min-tilt-2 check expected full tilt to fail, but the min clamps to 1 so full tilt meets it — fixed the expectation. (2) `normalizeKnobRadius([])` reads 8, not the default 24, because `Number([])` is 0 — fixed the expectation. (3) my `joystickTilt` unknown-direction check ran right after a half-tilt move (0.41 < the 0.5 minimum) — the check now uses a 0.4 minimum. (4) `tests/previewruntime.test.js` loaded `hydra-frame.js` with plain `require`, but the module assigns `window.HydraFrame` at load — it now loads through a vm sandbox like `frame.test.js`. (5) Visual QA caught the Enable Joystick properties panel rendering blank: a settings-free block's fallback note goes through `makeNote`, which hides help text behind `SHOW_PROPERTY_HELP = false` in `hydra-propinputs.js` — `showCatalogBlockProperties` in `hydra/script.js` now renders the fallback note visibly for blocks with no fields (the same fix covers Disable Joystick and every other settings-free block). A browser-smoke check pins the note text.
- **Tests:** new `tests/joystick.test.js` (81 checks, all green); `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BS (25 checks: inline, missing-module guard, gameStart enable, mouse grab/steer/release with the Move Right action held and the Joystick Active / Joystick Tilt (right) conditions firing, touch grab/steer-up/release with Jump held and Joystick Tilt (up) firing, KeyE disable + no re-grab, outside press as an ordinary click, zero diagnostics throughout); `tests/execute.test.js` (+6: enable/disable, disable releases a held stick, quiet without a live stick); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+15: active/tilt firing, reversed, min-tilt default 0.5, not frozen while paused, raw-record fallback without the module); `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+17: contexts carry module + stick + config, canvas mouse/touch grab + steer + release, the real frame loop writes the tilt into the variables and 0 after release); `tests/flow.test.js` (level pins) and `tests/frame.test.js` (`updateJoystick` after `pollInput`, before `runScripts`); `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 431 to 435); `tests/docs.test.js` (block reference + user guide); browser-smoke checks (+11: module, palette presence + Input category + Enable/Disable/Active/Tilt DOM order, Enable placement + note, Tilt placement + panel fields, Joystick tab + all fields). Full suite green (see `suite-cycle11-final.log` in the workflow workdir).
- **Visual QA:** `work/joystick-visual-qa.js` captured 7 screenshots, all opened and inspected: (1) Input palette shows all four joystick blocks in Enable / Disable / Active / Tilt order at the end of the Input category; (2) Enable Joystick places with cell label "Joy On" and its panel now shows the explanatory note (the blank-panel defect above, fixed and re-shot); (3) Joystick Tilt panel shows Trigger "While true (every frame)" with the edge/level hint, Direction "Any direction", Min tilt (0-1) "0.5", Invert checkbox, all aligned; (4) Joystick tab shows the hint, On by default, Base position, Custom X/Y, radii, Dead zone, both variable names, and the four action drives; (5–7) live preview on a dark scene: base circle bottom-left with the knob centered at rest, dragged full right to the base edge, back to center on release.
