## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 10 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 19 — simple AI behaviors (patrol / chase / flee / wander) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **simple AI behaviors** (Unity NavMeshAgent + simple AI states / Godot NavigationAgent2D + steering, beginner edition): enemies and NPCs decide where to walk on their own instead of needing hand-built chase logic. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-ai.js` (`HydraAi`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-pathfind.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeSpeed` (1–2000 px/s, default 100, same range as Follow Path) / `normalizeRange` (0–5000 px, default 300) / `normalizeBehavior` (unknown/blank reads as 'none'), `objectCenter`, `behaviorOf` / `setBehavior` / `stopBehavior` (the record lives on the live object as `obj._ai`: behavior, target, speed, range, patrolA, patrolB, waypointIndex, home captured at set time, repath/idle timers, waiting flag; 'none' clears it), `targetInRange` (center-to-center distance), `fleePoint` (Range px away from the target, fanning over twelve angles when straight away is a wall or out of bounds), `wanderPoint` (a random walkable point within Range of home, uniform over the disc, up to 8 tries), `updateAI` (the brain: patrol walks A→B→A… looking the points up live, chase re-plans to the target every 0.5s while it stays in range and stops past it, flee re-plans to a flee point while the target stays in range, wander walks to a picked spot then idles 0.5–1.5s before picking again; the walk grid is built once per call, lazily, and shared; a missing target or patrol point holds the object quietly), `isAiAction` / `isAiCondition`. Four new **AI** blocks in the catalog (431 total, was 427) — **Set AI Behavior** (Object + Behavior select + Target object + Speed + Range + two patrol points; a bad speed or range warns and holds, a missing mover/target stays quiet, and a new behavior stops the walking path so the brain always plans fresh) / **Stop AI** actions and the invertible **AI Behavior** / **Target In Range** conditions (`level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, evaluate while paused). The brain is a new frame phase `updateAI` in `hydra-frame.js` (after `updateParticles`, before `updatePaths`, so a fresh plan walks the same frame) with matching hooks in both runtimes, so behaviors follow the time scale and freeze while paused. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): walls come from tilemap solid tiles only; one behavior per object; chase/flee re-plan every half second, not every frame; a cornered enemy cannot flee (every walkable direction heads toward the threat); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** all were test-side, not engine: (1) my first `targetInRange` bad-range check expected a 375px-apart pair to read true under the 300 default — the default reads false past 300, so the check now uses a close pair for the true leg. (2) My patrol/chase arrival checks expected the marker's own center, but the follower arrives centered on the goal *cell* (304,48 / 304,304), the pathfinder's documented contract — fixed the expectations. (3) My chase catch checks expected pixel-exact arrival, but the half-second re-plan ends the walk the moment the enemy enters the player's cell (a one-point path reads done at once) — the checks now assert within one cell (32px). (4) My wander second-cycle check demanded `waiting === false` after 120 frames, but a fast second walk can arrive and re-idle inside that window — the check now asserts the enemy moved off the first spot, which only a fresh plan does. (5) My `conditions.test.js` aiBehavior check expected 'none' to stay quiet for the hero, but the stub world has two heroes and the second holds no record, so 'none' fires for it — the check now uses the recordless wall for the 'none' legs. (6) My `targetInRange` failed-range check expected hero-vs-hero to read false, but the test ctx's expr reads a bad name as 0 and same-object distance is 0, so it reads true — fixed the expectation. (7) The export-boot flee sub-test first failed with the enemy cornered (fled 368.9 vs start 371.9): the chase starts the enemy in the top-left corner, where every walkable direction heads toward the player, so no flee plan can increase the distance — the test now repositions both mid-map first, like the unit test, and the flee walks away cleanly. That cornered case is a real product limit, documented as such. (8) The first full-suite run failed only `tests/pathfind.test.js` on its stale phase-order pin (`'updateTweens', 'updateParticles', 'updatePaths'` no longer appears once `updateAI` sits between them); updated the pin to the new order and the re-run went fully green.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-ai.js` first. A one-off script (`ai-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 9's `pathfind-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each pathfind load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+26 runInContext, +58 array — the Phase BR list was written with the load already in place and the negative lookahead skipped it), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1 runInContext), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array), idempotent; verified 0 unfollowed loads, 0 double-inserts, and `node --check` passes on all four files.
- **Visual QA (AI UI):** `ai-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots (`ai-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir): the AI palette with all 4 blocks, the placed Set AI Behavior block with its properties panel (Object player, Behavior Chase, Target object blank, Speed 100, Range 300, Patrol points blank), the placed AI Behavior block with its properties panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)", Object player, Behavior Chase, Invert), and a live preview proving the chase twice — mid-chase and caught. The preview proof is a gameStart chain into Set AI Behavior (enemy → goal at speed 200, range 1000) across a tilemap wall (column 4, rows 0–7): a magenta-pixel scan of the preview canvas found the enemy mid-chase at center (141, 269), just past the wall's bottom end, and caught centered on the goal cell (296, 302) with the enemy peeking out as a magenta border around the green goal. All 5 shots were opened and inspected; no visual defects. Non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles; the "Desktop" device pill in the preview is clipped at the right edge, pre-existing; a long properties panel's bottom button sits partly behind the Diagnostics dock (the panel scrolls), same as prior cycles.
- Tests: new `tests/ai.test.js` (75 checks: surface + factorySource, speed/range/behavior normalization, object center, behavior record incl. clamping/defaults/home capture/'none' clearing, targetInRange, fleePoint incl. the fan-out and sealed-grid null, wanderPoint incl. range-0 home and sealed-grid null, the full brain incl. patrol A→B around the wall, missing-point hold, chase catch + re-plan + out-of-range stop, missing-target hold, flee away + out-of-range stop, wander pick/walk/idle/re-pick, dt-0/negative/missing-module/hostile no-ops, routing sets, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BR (10 sub-tests: export inlining, missing-module guard, gameStart chase around the wall with AI Behavior / Target In Range firing, chase catch within one cell, patrol A then on toward B, Stop AI clearing behavior + path, flee away from the player, wander near home + idle, bad-speed and bad-range warn-and-hold leaving the chase running); `tests/execute.test.js` (+12), `tests/conditions.test.js` (+13), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+4), `tests/flow.test.js` (aiBehavior/targetInRange level pins), `tests/frame.test.js` (updateAI phase-order pins), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 427 to 431), `tests/pathfind.test.js` (phase-order pin updated for the new updateAI phase), `tests/docs.test.js`, and browser-smoke checks (+7: module loaded, 4 palette blocks + AI-category DOM-order grouping, Set AI Behavior + AI Behavior placement + panels).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 9 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 18 — pathfinding (A* grid navigation) COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **pathfinding** (Unity NavMeshAgent / Godot NavigationAgent2D, beginner edition): enemies and NPCs walk around walls instead of getting stuck on them. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-pathfind.js` (`HydraPathfind`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-spatialaudio.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeSpeed` (1–2000 px/s, default 100), `objectCenter`, `buildWalkGrid` (cell size from the first tilemap's tileSize else 32, grid covers the whole scene, a cell is blocked when any solid tile overlaps it, capped at 200x200 cells by growing the cell size), `cellOf` / `cellCenter` / `isBlocked` (out of bounds counts as a wall), `findPath` (A* with a binary-heap open list, 8-direction movement, octile heuristic, no corner cutting — a diagonal needs both orthogonal neighbours open — start cell always walkable, blocked goal has no path, 20,000-expansion cap), `hasLineOfSight` / `simplifyPath` (string pulling, first and last points always survive), `startPath` / `stopPath` / `hasPath` / `pathFinished` (the record lives on the live object as `obj._path`: points, index, speed, done; Stop Path clears it so both reads go false), `updatePaths` (walks each following object toward its next waypoint at speed * dtSeconds, snaps on arrival, zeroes velocity while active so physics never fights the walk), `isPathfindAction` / `isPathfindCondition`. Four new **Pathfinding** blocks in the catalog (427 total, was 423) — **Follow Path** (Object + Destination object [blank = X/Y point] + X + Y + Speed expression; bad speed/position warns and holds, unreachable destination warns and holds without clobbering a walking path, missing mover/destination stays quiet) / **Stop Path** actions and the invertible **Path Finished** / **Has Path** conditions (`level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, evaluate while paused). The follower is a new frame phase `updatePaths` in `hydra-frame.js` (after `updateParticles`, last in the world sim, so the walk is the frame's final word on a following object's position) with matching hooks in both runtimes, so paths follow the time scale and freeze while paused. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): walls come from tilemap solid tiles only (solid objects are not obstacles yet); the path is computed once when the block runs (a moving target needs a fresh Follow Path); one path per object; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** all three were test-side, not engine: (1) my first execute-test expectations used the wrong cell centers — hero center (30, 40) sits in cell (0, 1), whose center is (16, 48), not (16, 40); fixed the expectations. (2) The same test's maze check required the simplified path to have more than 3 points and a waypoint inside the gap column, but string pulling collapses the detour to exactly 3 points whose middle point (112, 272) sits in column 3, not 4 — the *segments* cross the gap, not the waypoints; the check now pins the exact 3 points and asserts every segment has line of sight. (3) `browser-smoke.js` failed `node --check` on duplicate `followBefore` / `followCell` / `followPropText` declarations (the camera-follow section already declares them); my smoke variables are now `pf`-prefixed.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-pathfind.js` first. A one-off script (`pathfind-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 8's `spatialaudio-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each spatialaudio load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+25 runInContext, +55 array), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1 runInContext), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array), idempotent; verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` passes on all four files. (The export-boot array count is 55, not cycle 8's 54, because the new Phase BQ module list adds one more spatialaudio mention; no double-inserts — the negative lookahead skips the already-paired BQ list.)
- **Visual QA (pathfinding UI):** `pathfind-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots (`pathfind-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir): the Pathfinding palette with all 4 blocks, the placed Follow Path block with its properties panel (Object player, Destination object blank, X/Y 0, Speed 100), the placed Path Finished block with its properties panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)", Object player, Invert), and a live preview proving the walk twice — mid-walk and arrived. ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 8 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 17 — positional / spatial audio COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **positional / spatial audio** (Unity AudioSource spatial blend / Godot AudioStreamPlayer2D, beginner edition): sounds play from a place in the world. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-spatialaudio.js` (`HydraSpatialAudio`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-parallax.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `DEFAULT_MAX_DISTANCE = 600` (clamped 50-5000), `normalizeMaxDistance` / `normalizePan` (non-finite/missing read as the defaults), `listenerOf` (the screen center in world coords, camera and zoom aware), `objectCenter`, `spatialMix` (linear falloff, volume `1 - dist/maxDistance` clamped 0-1, pan `dx/maxDistance` clamped -1-1), `isSpatialAudioAction` / `isSpatialAudioCondition` (no conditions in this batch). One new **Audio** block in the catalog (423 total, was 422), **Play Sound at Position** (cell label `Sound at pos`): Sound (new `audio` field control, a select of the uploaded audio assets, blank is the built-in tone) + Frequency / Duration / Waveform + At object (a named object, or the X/Y point when left blank) + Hear within (the max distance, default 600) + Volume (default 0.3) + Bus + Pitch. The listener is always the center of the screen: a sound on top of it plays at full volume, it fades to silent at the hear-within distance, and it pans left or right with the source. Past that distance nothing plays and no voice is taken. The execute guard (after the parallax guard, before the dispatch switch) reads the named object's center (hidden objects included, so a hidden coin can jingle) or the X/Y point; a bad hear-within distance or a bad X/Y warns and holds, and a missing object stays quiet (the diagnostics validator flags a dangling object name at edit time). The block passes a copy of its data to `ctx.playSound`, never mutating the block record, with volume `base * mix.volume` and pan `mix.pan`. Pan is wired through the WebAudio graph: `exportPanner` / `previewPanner` (pan 0, or a browser without `createStereoPanner`, plays centered with no panner node), `exportTone` / `exportSfxBuffer` / `playToneBuffer` / `playSfxBuffer` all take the pan. A streamed `<audio>` fallback gets the distance volume only, no pan. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): linear falloff only; listener always the screen center (no follow-an-object listener, no Doppler); stereo pan only through the WebAudio graph (tones and decoded SFX/UI assets); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) My first test expected a missing source/listener to read as on top of each other, but the module reads missing coords as the origin (consistent with `objectCenter`/`listenerOf`); fixed the module doc comment and the test to the origin contract. (2) My first execute-test draft used maxDistance 300 for the shorter-distance case, which is wrong (the wall sits about 382 away, so it would be silent); fixed to 400. (3) Visual QA caught a real design bug: the field was named `sourceObject`, and `HydraGridModel.buildBlockDefaults` auto-fills a blank `sourceObject` with the second object name on placement, so a fresh block stored `coin` while the panel note promised blank means X/Y, and the object select offers no blank option to get back. Renamed the field to `atObject`, which the auto-fill list does not touch (a fresh block now stores blank and the panel shows an empty select, verified by probe); renamed in `hydra-execute.js`, the catalog defaults/field, `tests/execute.test.js` (9 uses) and `tests/export-boot.test.js` (5 uses). The X/Y path is now genuinely reachable from the editor. Note the shared object control still offers no way back to blank once an object is picked; that limitation is shared with Pick Nearest and is left as is. (4) Test-side, not engine: the export-boot Phase BP bad-distance check expects 2 diagnostics, because the expression evaluator emits a companion "Unknown expression name" warning alongside the block's own warn-and-hold warning (same behavior cycles 6/7 documented for bad smoothing/parallax). (5) The full suite's first logged run failed the browser smoke test on a stale pin I missed: `browser-smoke.js` still asserted FIELD_CONTROLS is "the v4 six + v3 four" (10 controls), while the catalog now carries the v3 five (11, with `audio`); fixed the pin to 11 controls including `audio`. The very first full-suite run also ended abruptly mid-stream with no summary (the exec session completed with only a partial log, like cycle 5's mid-run restart); a per-file scan showed every node file green individually, and the logged re-run confirmed the only real failure was the smoke pin above.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-spatialaudio.js` first. A one-off script (`spatialaudio-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 7's `parallax-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each parallax load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (+25 runInContext, +54 array), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (+1 runInContext), `tests/flagship.test.js` (+1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (+1 array), idempotent; verified 25/25 runInContext followed and `node --check` passes on all four files.
- **Visual QA (spatial-audio UI):** `spatialaudio-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 4 screenshots (`spatialaudio-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir): the Audio palette with Play Sound at Position in place (last Audio entry, before the TILEMAP heading), the placed block with its properties panel (Sound Built-in tone, Frequency 440, Duration 0.2, Waveform Square, At object blank, X/Y 0, Hear within 600, Volume 0.3), the same panel with At object coin and Volume 0.5, and a live preview proving the spatial mix end to end in real Chromium. The preview proof is a gameStart chain into Play Sound at Position at the coin (volume 0.5, hear-within 600): a probe wrapped around the real AudioContext recorded gain 0.38276 and pan 0.22667, exactly the expected mix for the coin center (536, 336) against the screen-center listener (400, 300), with "No runtime diagnostics". All 4 shots were opened and inspected; no visual defects. The one real defect found was the auto-fill bug above, fixed and re-shot: the fresh panel now shows At object blank. Two QA-script lessons, both fixed in the script, not the engine: the home page needs the New Project button + name + template card + Create flow before the grid appears (the template card is hidden until then), and the palette hides behind the properties panel after a placement, so the script clicks Back to blocks before picking the next block. Non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles; the "Desktop" device pill in the preview is clipped at the right edge, pre-existing.
- Tests: new `tests/spatialaudio.test.js` (42 checks: surface + factorySource, max-distance/pan normalization incl. null/'' reading as the defaults, listener math incl. zoom and missing camera, object center, spatialMix incl. falloff/pan/out-of-earshot/missing-coords-as-origin, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BP (8 sub-tests: export inlining, missing-module guard, bell gain/pan through the real grid wiring, far silence, centered full volume with no panner, bad-distance warn-and-hold, missing-object quiet, no-`createStereoPanner` degradation); `tests/execute.test.js` (+11), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+2), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 422 to 423; FIELD_CONTROLS pin "the v4 six plus the v3 five"), `tests/docs.test.js`, and browser-smoke checks (+6: module loaded, palette block + Audio-category DOM-order grouping, placement + panel fields, Sound select offers the built-in tone with no audio assets uploaded).
- Tests (full suite): `node tests/run.js` green, exit 0, 141 PASS lines, 6840 `ok` lines (log `cycle8-suite2.log` in the workflow workdir), smoke all checks passed incl. all 6 new spatial checks. The suite backgrounds after 120s in this environment; poll the background session rather than re-running it. The `screenshots/asset-manager-e2e/` untracked dir is still another process's evidence, left in place, not committed.

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 7 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 16 — parallax backgrounds COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **parallax backgrounds** (Unity ParallaxBackground / Godot ParallaxBackground + ParallaxLayer, beginner edition) — depth without a 3D scene. Per-object `parallaxX`/`parallaxY` scroll factors (0–2, default 1): 0 pins the object to the camera like a sky, 1 moves with the world (the old behavior), 0.5 moves at half speed like distant hills, above 1 rushes past like a foreground. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-parallax.js` (`HydraParallax`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-camerafeel.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeParallaxFactor` (0–2 clamp, non-numeric/missing → 1), `normalizeParallax` (in place), `parallaxOf` (fresh record), `parallaxCamera` (fresh camera with zoom untouched), `isParallaxAction` / `isParallaxCondition`. Drawing-only, like flip/scale: physics, collision, and world positions never move. Two new **Camera** blocks in the catalog (422 total, was 420) — Set Parallax (Object + Parallax X + Parallax Y expressions, defaults 0.5/0.5, bad value warns and holds) and the Parallax Factor condition (Object + Axis + operator + value, `level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`), plus an Inspector **Parallax** section per 2D object (X/Y number inputs 0–2 step 0.05 + hint, skipped for 3D kinds and light2d kinds). The shared draw module applies the factor to every per-kind draw (sprite/text/panel/tilemap, incl. button hover math); Object Clicked / Mouse Over and the preview's button hover hit-test through the same parallax camera, so clicks keep landing on the drawn position. Identical semantics in the editor preview and exported games. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): drawing-only (keep parallax on decoration layers); 2D objects only; no repeating/tiled background fill (use a wide sprite); no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) `Number(null)` is 0, same class as cycle 6's clamp bug — `normalizeParallaxFactor(null)` returned 0 instead of 1, and `parallaxCamera(null, ...)` returned `zoom: 0`. Fixed in `hydra-parallax.js`: `saneNum` and `normalizeParallaxFactor` now treat `null`/`undefined`/`''` as missing and return the fallback. (2) The export's `normalizeObject` in `player-runtime.js` is a separate normalizer from `HydraUniversal.normalizeObject`, so exported objects never got parallax fields — fixed by adding the guarded `HydraParallax.normalizeParallax(obj)` repair there too, pinned by a static check in `parallax.test.js`. (3) Test-side, not engine: `tests/conditions.test.js` `makeObjects()` has two heroes (id 1 and id 3), so parallax factor checks use the unique `wall`; the Phase BO bad-factor check expects 2 diagnostics, because the expression evaluator emits a companion "Unknown expression name" warning alongside the block's own warn-and-hold warning (same behavior cycle 6 documented for bad smoothing).
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-parallax.js` first — a one-off script (`parallax-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 6's `camerafeel-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each camerafeel load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (53 array + 25 runInContext lines), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1 line), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (1 array), idempotent; verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` passes on all four files.
- **Visual QA (parallax UI):** `parallax-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots (`parallax-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — Camera palette with both blocks, placed Set Parallax + properties panel (Object player, Parallax X/Y 0.5), placed Parallax Factor + properties panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)", Axis X, Compare equals, Value 0.5, Invert), Inspector Parallax section on a fresh sprite (X/Y at 1), and a live preview proving the draw — and all 5 were opened and inspected. The preview proof needed a camera move: the collector template has no camera follow, and its world is exactly the viewport, so the script places gameStart → Move Camera (X 400), widens the world to 1600, and adds two layers at the same world X (hills 0.5 green, post 1.0 red). A canvas pixel probe confirmed the exact colors at the exact screen spots: hills center (230,480) = (34,136,34), post center (30,330) = (220,38,38). No visual defects: the `Parallax?` cell label fits its block, both panels read clean, the preview shows no overlap or clipping and "No runtime diagnostics". Non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles; the "Desktop" device pill in the preview is clipped at the right edge, pre-existing.
- Tests: new `tests/parallax.test.js` (41 checks: surface + factorySource, factor/object normalization, parallaxCamera math, routing sets, static wiring pins incl. index.html order, guard, inlining, dispatch lists, both ctx wirings, preview hover, draw integration, conditions hit-test, execute routing, flow level, universal + export normalization, catalog, editor section, docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BO (6 sub-tests: export inlining, missing-module guard, authored factors load, Parallax Factor fires incl. reversed, Set Parallax rewrites live factors, bad factor warns and holds, missing object quiet, pinned hero reads); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+13 incl. mouseOver hit-test proving the parallax camera), `tests/execute.test.js` (+8), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+4 incl. parallax button hover at the drawn position), `tests/flow.test.js` (parallaxFactor level pin), `tests/draw.test.js` (+9 pinning screen math over the recording ctx), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 420 → 422), `tests/docs.test.js` (passes on the new block-reference rows and user-guide paragraph), and browser-smoke checks (+8: module loaded, 2 palette blocks under Camera, Set Parallax + Parallax Factor placement + panels, Inspector Parallax section on a fresh sprite).

## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 6 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 15 — camera feel COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **camera feel** (Unity Cinemachine damping + confiner / Godot Camera2D smoothing + drag margins + limits, beginner edition) — the camera follow now glides instead of snapping, holds still while the followed object stays inside a deadzone, and stays inside a world rectangle. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-camerafeel.js` (`HydraCameraFeel`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-transition.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): `normalizeSmoothing` (0–0.95, 0 = snap, 1 would freeze so it clamps), `normalizeDeadzone` / `normalizeCameraBounds` (backwards/partial/non-finite reads as null) / `normalizeCameraFeel` (in place), `defaultCameraFeel` / `freshCameraFeel` (deep copy including bounds), `desiredFollowCamera`, `smoothToward` (per-frame factor `k = 1 - s^(dtSeconds*60)`, follows the time scale, dt ≤ 0 holds still), `clampCameraToBounds` (view wider than bounds centers on bounds; null bounds falls back to scene edges), `updateFollowCamera`, `cameraFollowing`, `isCameraFeelAction` / `isCameraFeelCondition`. The per-scene defaults live on `scene.cameraFeel` (smoothing, deadzoneW/H, bounds) with new **Scene Settings** fields in the World inspector (smoothing number 0–0.95, deadzone width/height, four bounds edges with a Clear bounds button; a half-filled rect does not take effect until complete and valid) plus the default in `hydra-universal.js`. Five new **Camera** blocks in the catalog (420 total, was 415) — Set Camera Smoothing / Set Camera Deadzone / Set Camera Bounds / Clear Camera Bounds actions and the Camera Following condition (`level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, reads `ctx.camera.followTarget` trimmed non-blank). The feel is run-level state: a fresh copy is minted from the scene config on every scene load via the new `cameraFeelOf` scene-reset hook (blocks can never rewrite project data in the preview), it never travels in save slots, and Reset Camera restores the scene's feel. Both runtimes call the same `updateFollowCamera` with the scaled dt; the export snaps when the module tag is dropped, the preview too. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): centered-rectangle deadzone only (no look-ahead, no per-axis speeds); one smoothing number for both axes; one bounds rect per scene; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) `clampCameraToBounds`'s scene-size fallback used `Number(sceneSize && sceneSize.width)` — `Number(null)` is 0, so a null scene size pinned the camera to `-viewW/2` instead of leaving it alone. Fixed with an explicit object check before reading width/height. (2) The export's hardcoded `isCondition`/`isAction` dispatch lists in `player-runtime.js` missed the new blocks, so the export reported `Unknown block type "cameraFollowing"` and the four actions never ran — the editor classifies from the catalog, so only the export needed the routing (same pattern as the transition blocks). A `camerafeel.test.js` static pin now guards the routing so the next cycle cannot repeat the miss. (3) Test-side, not engine: the Phase BN expectations had to shift one frame because frame 1 runs with dt 0 (the frame clock starts with no previous timestamp), so the follow only starts gliding on frame 2 — the same first-frame rule Phase BL documents for physics. A standalone probe (`/tmp/probe-bn2.js`, minimal vm session) printed dt/camera/follow per frame and confirmed it: frame 1 dt 0 no move, frame 2 glides to (160,110), frame 3 to (240,165). The bad-smoothing check also had to accept the expression evaluator's companion "unknown name" warning alongside the smoothing warning.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-camerafeel.js` first — a one-off script (`camerafeel-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 5's `transition-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each transition load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (52 array + 25 runInContext lines), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1 line), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (1 array), idempotent via negative lookahead; verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` passes on all four files. `tests/execute.test.js`, `tests/previewruntime.test.js`, and `tests/camerafeel.test.js` need no fallout (they never load player-runtime.js).
- **Visual QA (camera-feel UI):** `camerafeel-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 6 screenshots (`camerafeel-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — Camera palette category with all 5 blocks, placed Set Camera Smoothing block + properties panel (Smoothing 0.5), placed Set Camera Bounds block + properties panel (Min X/Y 0, Max X 800, Max Y 600), placed Camera Following block + properties panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)", Invert), the Scene Settings camera-feel section, and a live preview with the scene smoothing set to 0.9 — and all 6 were opened and inspected. One real QA-script mistake, fixed in the script, not the engine: the camera-feel fields live in the **World** inspector panel, not Scenes — the first shot showed the scene list instead of the fields, and the smoke test had clicked the wrong tab too (its value assertions passed while the fields were hidden). Both now open the World tab, and the smoke check also asserts the wrap is visible. The re-shot element capture shows the whole section clean: Camera smoothing 0 with its hint, Camera deadzone 0/0, Camera bounds Min/Max X/Y placeholders, Clear bounds button. The live preview boots and renders the Coin Rush scene with no errors. Non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles; the `Cam Smooth` / `Cam Bounds` / `Cam Unbound` / `Cam Follow?` cell labels overflow their blocks the same way existing 10–13-character labels do (`Music playing`, `Pause music`, `Stop music`, `Reset keys`) — the centered-label rendering, not a camera-feel defect; the "Desktop" device pill in the preview is clipped at the right edge, pre-existing.
- Tests: new `tests/camerafeel.test.js` (76 checks: surface + factorySource, smoothing/deadzone/bounds normalization incl. in-place identity and the null-scene-size clamp fix, desired/smooth/clamp math incl. view-wider-than-bounds centering, updateFollowCamera glide/deadzone/bounds/dt-0, cameraFollowing reads, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs + the dispatch-classifier pin); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BN (8 sub-tests, 32 checks: export inlining, missing-module guard, glide/deadzone/bounds/clear/reset/scene-switch flows through the real grid wiring, bad-expression warns + holds then recovers, reversed Camera Following after the follow stops); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+7), `tests/execute.test.js` (+11), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+11), `tests/flow.test.js` (cameraFollowing level pin), `tests/scenereset.test.js` (+5: cameraFeelOf hook, fresh copy per reset, no aliasing, missing/throwing hook reads null), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 415 → 420), `tests/docs.test.js` (passes on the new labels), and browser-smoke checks (+10: module loaded, 5 palette blocks + Camera-category DOM-order grouping, Set Camera Smoothing + Set Camera Bounds + Camera Following placement + panels, Scene Settings fields with defaults in the World tab).
- Tests (full suite): `node tests/run.js` green, exit 0 — 6663 `ok` lines (log `full-run2.log` in /tmp; run 1 with the same count used `full-run1.log` before the smoke-tab fix), all node files PASS, playtest 44/44, smoke all checks passed incl. all 10 new camera-feel checks. Note: the suite backgrounds after 120s in this environment; poll the background session rather than re-running it. The `screenshots/asset-manager-e2e/` untracked dir is still another process's evidence — left in place, not committed. ## 2026-11-06 — Cycle 5 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 14 — scene transitions COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **scene transitions** (Unity `SceneManager.LoadScene` with a transition / Godot scene transitions / Unreal level fades, beginner edition) — switching scenes can fade to black or wipe across instead of cutting at once. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-transition.js` (`HydraTransition`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-timescale.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): effects `fade` / `wipe` / `none` plus a per-call `cut` override; `normalizeEffect` / `normalizeDuration` (0–10s, 0 = instant cut) / `normalizeTransitionConfig` (repairs in place); `createTransitionRuntime` / `startTransition` / `updateTransition` / `cancelTransition` (cancel during the out half stays in the current scene with no finish recorded, cancel during the in half finishes at once; starting one replaces one already playing); `isTransitionPlaying` / `isTransitionFinished` / `transitionProgress` / `transitionAlpha` / `drawTransition` (screen-space overlay: fade paints a full-canvas black rect at the alpha, wipe grows a black panel from the left while out and slides it off to the right while in). The project default lives on `project.sceneTransition` (effect `fade`, duration 0.5) with a new **Transitions** tab (effect select + duration number input + hint) plus the default in `hydra-universal.js`. Four new **Scene** blocks in the catalog (415 total, was 411) — Transition to Scene (Scene + Effect [Use default/Fade/Wipe/Cut] + Duration expression; blank duration uses the configured default, a failed duration expression warns and holds) / Cancel Transition actions and the Transition Playing (`level` trigger default) / Transition Finished (`edge` trigger default) conditions, both NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED` and reading `ctx.transition` directly. The plain Switch Scene block still cuts at once, so old projects behave exactly as before. The transition is run-level state: it survives the scene switch it performs, freezes while paused, follows the time scale (both loops feed it the scaled frame step), resets only on a fresh run, and never travels in save slots. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): one transition at a time; two effects only; overlay always black, no custom colors, no per-effect easing; no `hs` script-API surface yet.
- **Three real bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) the half-duration boundary needed a 1ns epsilon in `updateTransition` — 15 frames at 1/60 sum to 0.24999999999999997, which delayed every 0.5s fade by one frame without it. (2) The export-boot Phase BM run failed 9 assertions on a restart that re-fired the KeyF transition the frame a fade finished: instrumenting `startTransition` in the vm sandbox showed the call arriving with empty key sets, and the cause was the dispatch's orthogonal-adjacency rule (a firing condition executes any action orthogonally adjacent to it) — my test grid had packed the `transitionToScene` action directly below the `transitionFinished` condition, so the finish fired it. That is the engine's documented legacy behavior, not a bug in it: the fix is in the test grid, which now spaces events two rows apart with a blank guard row (the template audit's one-event-per-row rule), plus a float-tolerant wipe-width check (150.0000000000001) and a `pump(1)` before the `sawFinished` checks on cut/cancel, whose Finished edge fires on the next frame because the switch happens during the grid run after the condition rows evaluated. (3) Visual QA caught the four new blocks rendering under the **Time** heading in the palette: `hydrateCatalogBlocks` in `hydra/script.js` appended every new option at the end of the list while the Scene label already existed earlier in catalog order, so a block added to an existing category landed under whatever heading came last. Fixed at the engine level, not per-instance — each option now inserts after the last option of its own category (options carry `dataset.category`, the insert walks forward from the category anchor), so any future batch adding to an existing category lands under the right heading too. The browser-smoke category check was strengthened at the same time: it now verifies the four blocks sit between the Scene label and the next label in DOM order, instead of merely asserting a Scene label exists somewhere.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-transition.js` first — a one-off script (`transition-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 4's `timescale-fallout.py`) inserted the load after each timescale load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (51 array + 25 runInContext lines), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1 line), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (1 array), idempotent via negative lookahead; verified 0 unfollowed loads and `node --check` passes on all four files. `tests/execute.test.js`, `tests/previewruntime.test.js`, and `tests/timescale.test.js` need no fallout (they never load player-runtime.js).
- **Visual QA (transition UI):** `transition-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 6 screenshots (`transition-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — Scene palette category with all 4 transition blocks, placed Transition to Scene block + properties panel (Scene to show, Effect "Use default", Duration "blank = default"), placed Transition Finished block + properties panel (Trigger "On become true (once)", Scene, Invert), Transitions tab (hint, Default effect "Fade to black", Duration 0.5), and the live preview mid-fade twice — and all 6 were opened and inspected. The palette defect above was the one real defect found, fixed, and re-shot: the Scene category now shows all 10 blocks in order (Restart / Go To Next / Go To Previous / Push / Pop / Set Scene Background / Transition to Scene / Cancel Transition / Transition Playing / Transition Finished). The live fade is proven, not just asserted: a pixel probe (`transition-probe.js`) sampled the preview canvas center through a 0.5s fade (rgb(102,99,95) → 17 near-black → 255 white in the new scene), and the QA shots with the default stretched to 2s show the Coin Rush scene dimmed under the overlay mid-fade-out (Score, player, hazard, coin, and caption all readable through the veil) and the white Scene 2 under a mid-gray veil mid-fade-in. Two QA-script lessons, both fixed in the script, not the engine: the preview starts in the currently selected scene (the chain lives in Coin Rush, so the script switches the scene switcher back to it before opening the preview), and the 0.5s default finishes before the capture pipeline runs (the script stretches the default to 2s for the shots). Non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles; the Transitions tab button truncates to "Transiti..." like the existing "Achieve..." and "Localiza..." buttons; the `TransScene` / `TransDone?` cell labels overflow their blocks the same way existing 10–13-character labels do (`Music playing`, `Stop music`, `Reset keys`) — the centered-label rendering, not a transition defect.
- Tests: new `tests/transition.test.js` (83 checks: surface + factorySource, config/effect/duration normalization incl. in-place identity, start with fade/wipe/none/cut + overrides + blank/unknown + replace + numeric id, update out→switch→in→finish + huge dt + pause freeze + failed switch + bad dt, cancel out/in/idle, playing/finished reads incl. blank/named/id-vs-string, progress/alpha, draw on a stub ctx, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BM (9 sub-tests, 31 checks: export inlining, missing-module guard, fade/wipe/cut flows through the real grid wiring, unknown scene warns + holds then recovers, cancel out/in, pause freeze/resume, tab-default wipe, plain switch leaving the transition record alone); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+12), `tests/execute.test.js` (+11), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+17), `tests/flow.test.js` (transitionPlaying level + transitionFinished edge pins), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 411 → 415), and browser-smoke checks (+9: module loaded, 4 palette blocks + Scene-category DOM-order grouping, Transition to Scene + Transition Finished placement + panels, Transitions tab hint + effect select + 0.5s duration). Three of my own unit-test bugs were found and fixed while writing the suites (wrong numeric scene in a playing check, a finish check needing a second update, a hostile-playing check that must pass a scene name to exercise the matcher) — all test bugs, not engine bugs.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 4 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 13 — time scale / slow motion COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **time scale / slow motion** (Unity `Time.timeScale` / Unreal global time dilation / Godot `Engine.time_scale`, beginner edition) — one number says how fast the whole game clock runs. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-timescale.js` (`HydraTimeScale`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-highscore.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): scale range 0–4, default 1; `normalizeTimeScale` (finite values clamp; null/undefined/empty string read as 1); `createTimeScaleRuntime` / `timeScaleOf` / `setTimeScale` (non-numeric warns and holds) / `resetTimeScale` / `scaledDt` (never exceeds the engine's 3-unit dt cap); `noteScaledFrame` banks `realMs * (1 - scale)` into `state.timeScaleDebtMs` while unpaused, and `HydraPause.gameNow` now returns `now - pausedElapsedMs - timeScaleDebtMs`, so named timers slow with the world (at scale 0.5 a 3-second timer takes 6 real seconds; at scale 2 it takes 1.5). The scale is run-level state: it survives scene switches inside a run (like the language), resets only on a fresh run, and never travels in save slots; the debt resets with the per-scene game clock. Both loops scale dt once, right after `HydraFrame.computeDt`, so physics, animations, tweens, particles, cutscenes, dialogue typing, and named timers all slow together while scripts, input, buttons, and the profiler FPS stay real-time. Four new **Time** blocks in the catalog (411 total, was 407) — Set Time Scale / Reset Time Scale / Read Time Scale actions and the Time Scale condition (operator + value + Invert, `level` trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, reads `ctx.timeScale.scale` directly). The execute dispatch for `setTimeScale` uses `exprInfo` and passes `NaN` on a failed expression, so a typo warns and holds instead of freezing the world through the engine-wide blank-means-0 rule. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): no `hs` script-API surface yet (use the blocks); no smooth ramp (the scale changes at once — ramp it yourself with repeated Set Time Scale); dialogue typing slows with the world, including while paused; a blank Scale field evaluates to 0 through the expression evaluator, so clearing the field freezes the world.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed in-cycle:** (1) my first version clamped negative debt to 0 in both `gameNow` and `noteScaledFrame` — at scale above 1 the debt must go negative, because the clock runs ahead of the wall clock (Unity behavior); verified by vm probe (100ms at 0.5 banks 50 debt, then 100ms at 2 takes it to −50 and `gameNow` advances). (2) While finishing the test additions, the new preview-runtime gameLoop checks failed with `debt=undefined`: `noteScaledFrame` read the scale off `state.timeScale`, which the export's run state carries but the preview's run state does not (the preview runtime lives in the module closure, not on the host-owned run object) — so the preview silently banked zero debt while the export worked, and the export-boot Phase BL timer checks had passed throughout, hiding it. Fixed by giving `noteScaledFrame` an optional third runtime argument (omitted, it falls back to `state.timeScale`) and passing the runtime explicitly at both call sites (`state.timeScale` in the export loop, `timeScaleRuntime()` in the preview gameLoop); two new module checks pin the explicit-runtime form (a state with no `timeScale` field banks through it; an explicit null reads as normal speed), and the two static wiring pins in `tests/timescale.test.js` were updated to the new call text. The preview gameLoop checks now pass: at half speed a 16.67ms frame writes dt 0.5 and banks 8.335ms of debt, and a paused frame banks nothing.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-timescale.js` first — a one-off script (`timescale-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir, adapted from cycle 3's `highscore-fallout2.py`) inserted the load after each highscore load: `tests/export-boot.test.js` (25 runInContext + 50 array), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1 runInContext), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1 array), `tests/playtest.test.js` (1 array), idempotent via negative lookahead. One latent bug found in cycle 3's v2 array regex (it was missing the space between the comma and the next quoted name, so its array pattern never matched — masked because v1 had done that half); fixed in my script. `tests/execute.test.js` loads the module by hand with its other sandbox loads (no player-runtime there). Verified individually: export-boot, fps-export, flagship, and playtest (44/44) all pass.
- **Visual QA (time-scale UI):** `timescale-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 4 screenshots (`timescale-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — Time palette category with all 4 blocks, placed Set Time Scale block + properties panel (Scale field, value 1), placed Time Scale condition + properties panel (Trigger "While true (every frame)", Compare "Less than (scale <)", Value, Invert), placed Read Time Scale block + properties panel (Into variable, placeholder "e.g. gameSpeed") — and all 4 were opened and inspected. No defects found: every label reads, nothing clipped or overlapping. One non-defect noted: Font Awesome glyphs on the grid blocks were blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles.
- Tests: new `tests/timescale.test.js` (71 checks: surface + factorySource, normalization incl. null/'' reading as 1, fresh runtime + `timeScaleOf`, set/reset, `scaledDt` incl. the 3-unit cap, debt banking incl. negative debt at scale 2, paused no-op, junk/negative previous debt, the explicit-runtime form, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BL (26 checks: export inlining, missing-module guard, the full flow through the real grid wiring — velocity at half/double speed, bad-scale warn-and-hold, a 0.3s timer staying quiet at half speed then firing after reset, a scale-0 freeze session with KeyR unfreeze, `readTimer` under double speed, the scale surviving a scene switch while the debt resets); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+8), `tests/execute.test.js` (+11), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+19, incl. driving `gameLoop` directly to pin dt scaling and debt banking), `tests/flow.test.js` (timeScale level pin), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 407 → 411), `tests/pause.test.js` (+5, debt subtracts / negative debt advances the clock / junk debt reads as 0), `tests/scenereset.test.js` (+1, debt clears with the fresh clock), and browser-smoke checks (+7: module loaded, 4 palette blocks + Time category, Set Time Scale + Time Scale placement + panels). One of my own conditions checks was wrong as written (0.5 >= 0 is true — the non-numeric-value pin now uses a scale-0 state, where a junk value reads as 0 and `==` fires). One smoke-test failure caught and fixed in-cycle: my Time section assumed the script tab was still active, but the High Scores section before it ends on the Scene tab, so the first palette click timed out (element not visible) — the section now clicks back to the script tab and scrolls the Time category into view first.
- Tests (full suite): `node tests/run.js` green, exit 0 — 6346 `ok` lines (log `full-suite3.log` in the workflow workdir), all node files PASS, playtest 44/44, smoke all checks passed incl. all 7 new time-scale checks. ## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 3 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 12 — local high-score table COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **the local high-score table** (Unity / Game Center / Steam local leaderboards, beginner edition) — the classic ranked, named, persistent top-N leaderboard, the companion to achievements (achievements are named trophies, the table is a ranked list that keeps score). New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-highscore.js` (`HydraHighScore`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-cutscene.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): project config `project.highScores` (`maxEntries` 1–50, default 10; `defaultName`, default `'Player'`, capped at 32 chars); runtime `{ entries: [{ name, score, date }] }` sorted best first (highest score; ties to earlier date, so first to reach a score keeps the higher rank); `submitScore` returns the 1-based rank or 0 when the score does not make a full table (a tie with the lowest entry does NOT displace it), persists on change; `isHighScore` (room or beats the lowest entry), `readHighScore` (1-based, a copy or null), `highScoreCount`, `clearHighScores` (persists on change); storage keyed by project id (`hydraHighScores:<id>`), per-game isolation. The table is PERMANENT: it survives runs, scenes, and restarts; only Clear High Scores takes scores back; `resetGame` never clears it; scene switches leave it alone; it is not part of save slots. Nothing ever throws; hostile input degrades to safe defaults. Honest limits (module header, user guide, CHANGELOG): no built-in leaderboard HUD (read ranks into variables and write into text objects); scores only, no replays or per-level splits; Submit Score does not report the earned rank back into a variable; table keyed by project id (renaming keeps it, copying to a new id starts fresh); old projects without `highScores` behave exactly as before.
- **Editor + both runtimes:** new **High Scores** tab in the Inspector (`getProjectHighScores`, `getMutableHighScoreConfig`, config card with Max entries number input 1–50 + Default name text input, stored board best first with rank/name/score/date, empty-state hint, `Clear stored scores` button that clears storage AND the live preview table); 5 new **High Score** blocks in the catalog (407 total, was 402) — Submit Score / Clear High Scores / Read High Score actions and Is High Score / High Score Count conditions (both `level` trigger, both NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, reading `ctx.highScoreTable`/`ctx.highScoreConfig` directly). Design fix made during testing: `isHighScore` requires the table to exist (missing table reads false, matching the documented contract and sibling conditions); the lowest entry is the minimum score across the table, not the last row, so an unsorted table still reads right. The player runtime holds `state.highScoreConfig` (normalized once) + `state.highScoreTable` (loaded from storage by project id) + `highScoreKey`; the preview runtime lazily loads the same table from storage via `host.getProjectId()`/`host.getProjectHighScores()`, persists on change, and exposes `clearHighScoreTable()` (empties the live table + persists, for the editor tab's Clear button) — deliberately NO scene-reset entry and `resetGame` never clears it. Docs: `docs/block-reference.md` High Score (5) section, `docs/user-guide.md` `## High Scores` section with the honest limits.
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-highscore.js` first — a one-off script (`highscore-fallout2.py` in the workflow workdir) inserted the load after each cutscene load: 25 runInContext single-line + 49 array-form inserts in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, 1 runInContext + array inserts in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, array-form inserts in `tests/flagship.test.js` and `tests/playtest.test.js`. Two real fallout-script bugs found and fixed in-cycle: (1) the first version's runInContext regex required the literal sandbox name `sandbox`, so export-boot's 25 loads (`ed.sandbox`, `pv.sandbox`, ...) were silently skipped — v2 captures any sandbox var; (2) the verify assert compared raw mention counts (cutscene 80 vs high-score 54 in export-boot), which can never match because comments and guard strings mention cutscene without being loads — v2 verifies load pairs instead (every runInContext cutscene load followed by a high-score load; no array list with cutscene directly followed by trace). Verified: export-boot 25/25 runInContext pairs, fps-export 1/1, flagship/playtest array asserts clean; `node --check` passes on all four files. `tests/cutscene.test.js` (loads the cutscene module alone) and `tests/execute.test.js` / `tests/conditions.test.js` (load subsystem modules directly) were deliberately skipped — no player-runtime load in their sandboxes.
- **Visual QA (high-score UI):** `highscore-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots (`highscore-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — High Score palette category with all 5 blocks, placed Submit Score block + properties panel, placed Is High Score block + properties panel, High Scores tab empty state with hint + config inputs, High Scores tab with a stored board — and all 5 were opened and inspected. One real defect found and fixed in the same cycle: the stored-board rows rendered pale-gray text on the shared white `.input-action-row` card — nearly unreadable — because the card sets a white background but no text color, so bare spans inherited the panel's light text; the "Max entries" label also wrapped to two lines in its narrow 52px column. Fixed with a scoped `.highscore-row` class in `hydra/style.css` (dark text `#1e293b`, muted-dark date, label `white-space: nowrap` + auto width, name flexes, score bold) applied to the config row and each board row in `hydra/script.js`; re-shot and re-inspected — every rank, name, score, and date now reads, and the config label sits on one line. One non-defect noted: Font Awesome glyphs were blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), same as prior cycles.
- Tests: new `tests/highscore.test.js` (101 checks: surface + factorySource, config normalization incl. in-place identity + caps/defaults, fresh/seeded runtime incl. best-first sort + tie to earlier date + corrupt-seed rejection + maxEntries cap + list copies, submit incl. rank returns + persist-on-change + full-table hold + tie does not displace + blank-name default + 32-char cap + 0/negative scores, qualify/read/count, clear, storage incl. key sanitize/cap/default + serialize/parse round-trip + corrupt bytes + per-game isolation + throwing storage, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BK (21 checks: export inlining, missing-module guard, the full flow through the real grid wiring — submit best-first via expressions and blank names, hold on a full table, Is High Score / High Score Count / Read High Score into variables, reversed conditions, KeyL submit hold, KeyR read past the end writes nothing, KeyC clear, storage write + fresh-boot load + persisted read, scene switch keeping the table, zero diagnostics); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+12), `tests/execute.test.js` (+11), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+16), `tests/flow.test.js` (isHighScore/highScoreCount level pins), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 402 → 407), and browser-smoke checks (+11: module loaded, 5 palette blocks + High Score category, Submit Score + Is High Score placement + panels, High Scores tab hint + config inputs + empty-board note + Clear button).

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 2 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 11 — cutscene / timeline system COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **the cutscene / timeline system** (Unity Timeline / Unreal Sequencer, beginner edition) — the bigger, riskier build cycle 0 explicitly deferred to batch 11. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-cutscene.js` (`HydraCutscene`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-achievement.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): the project table `project.cutscenes` (name + description + steps; `normalizeCutscenes` repairs in place; blank names drop, duplicate names collapse to the first, ids `cts_<n>`); the per-run runtime `{ active, stepIndex, stepElapsed, stepStarted, stepFrom, lastFinished }` with `playCutscene` (replaces a running cutscene, clears `lastFinished` so Finished re-fires on replay), `stopCutscene` (counts as finished), and `updateCutscene` (per-step clocks; a timed step's clock starts the frame after it starts, instant steps all fire in one frame, dt is never spent twice). Five step kinds: wait, text (writes into a text object at step start, the text stays after the step), move (linear glide from the object's current spot, 0 seconds teleports), sound (plays a named asset once), scene (switches scene and the cutscene keeps playing in the new scene). The runtime freezes while paused, survives scene switches, resets only on a fresh run, and is NOT in save slots. Unknown names, missing objects, sounds, and scenes warn once and hold or skip — a typo never stalls the timeline; a cutscene deleted mid-run ends without marking finished; damaged steps are dropped by normalization.
- **Editor + both runtimes:** new **Cutscenes** tab in the Inspector (`getProjectCutscenes`, `getMutableCutscenes`, `propagateCutsceneRename` repoints the four cutscene-name blocks' `cutscene` field across the global grid, scene grids, and cellData; per-cutscene cards with name input + delete, description input, per-step rows with a kind select and kind-dependent fields, add-step auto-creating a 1-second wait; kind changes wipe stale fields via `resetCutsceneStepForKind`; the scene step's select preserves a stored name that no longer matches a scene as a "(missing scene)" option); 4 new **Cutscene** blocks in the catalog (402 total, was 398) — Play Cutscene / Stop Cutscene actions and Cutscene Playing (level trigger) / Cutscene Finished (edge trigger, not in the level-default list) conditions, both NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED` and reading `ctx.cutscene` directly. The player runtime holds `state.cutscenes` (normalized) + `state.cutscene` (fresh on boot), updated in `runScripts` before the grids run; the preview runtime holds a module-level runtime, reset in `resetGame` only (drop only, so a fresh run never starts with a stale Finished) — the cutscene deliberately SURVIVES scene switches inside a run, so there is no scene-reset entry for it, and the tests pin that absence. Docs: `docs/block-reference.md` Cutscene (4) section, `docs/user-guide.md` `## Cutscenes` section with the honest limits (no scrubbing/parallel tracks/easing, one cutscene at a time, no progress counter, no save-slot travel, warn-and-skip on missing targets, rename caveat).
- **Load-order fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-cutscene.js` first — a one-off script (`cutscene-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir) inserted the load after each achievement load: 50 array-form + 29 runInContext inserts across `tests/export-boot.test.js`, `tests/execute.test.js`, `tests/fps-export.test.js`, `tests/flagship.test.js`, `tests/inventory.test.js`, `tests/playtest.test.js`, `tests/quests.test.js`. Verified counts match exactly (export-boot run 25/25, array 48/48, 0 unfollowed runInContext loads). `tests/achievements.test.js` (its own subject file, never loads player-runtime) and `tests/cutscene.test.js` (already loads hydra-cutscene.js first) were deliberately skipped.
- **One real failure caught while running the suite, fixed in-cycle:** the first full-suite run failed `tests/dialogue.test.js` on the source pin `updateDialogue(state); processScope(state,` — my cutscene update had landed between the dialogue update and the grid dispatch in `runScripts`. Fixed by moving `updateCutscene(state)` before `updateDialogue(state)` (the cutscene still runs before the grids, and the dialogue pin is restored); the dialogue-vs-cutscene order only matters when both write the same text object in one frame, which is an edge case either way.
- **Visual QA (cutscene UI):** `cutscene-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots (`cutscene-visual-qa/` in the workflow workdir) — Cutscene palette category with all 4 blocks, placed Play Cutscene block + properties panel, Cutscenes tab empty state with hint, one cutscene card with steps of four kinds, two filled cutscene cards — and all 5 were opened and inspected. One real defect found and fixed in the same cycle: the multi-field step rows (text: 4 fields after the kind select; move: 5) crushed every field to a sliver in the shared no-wrap flex row, and the move step's row overflowed the card and clipped its delete button half outside the border. Fixed with a scoped `.cutscene-step-row` class (`flex-wrap: wrap`, kind select min 120px, text inputs min 90px, number inputs min 64px) in `hydra/style.css` + the row class in `hydra/script.js`; re-shot and re-inspected — every kind label, field value, and delete button now sits inside the card and reads. Two non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs on the add/delete buttons were blank because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), and the description placeholder truncates in the narrow card (the shared card pattern from the Items/Quests tabs).

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 0 (improve): recovery of cycle 76 quest system + smoke-server pipe-stall fix COMPLETED

- Recovery first (step 2): cycle 76's quest system was left uncommitted because git could not write `*.lock` files from ~21:40 Nov 5 (environment fault); git writes work again this cycle, so the recovered diff was verified coherent and committed here. The diff also carries cycle-77 harness tuning from the concurrent process (playtest jump window 80px → 140px, smoke timeout 420s → 900s). Untracked `screenshots/asset-manager-e2e/` (7 screenshots from the concurrent asset-manager process) was NOT committed — another process's evidence, left in place. Old `stash@{0}` (Nov 4 P4 item-21 sprite-animation WIP) untouched.
- Smoke-stall diagnosis (the real find of this cycle): the full suite failed twice at the identical place — the P3 item 18 block-validation section, `page.goto` timing out 30s × 3 — after every other section passed, including all 10 quest checks. A monitored run proved the cause: `tests/browser-smoke.js` spawns `python3 -m http.server` with Node's default piped stdio and never reads stdout/stderr; the server logs every request to stderr, and after ~1108 requests the 64 KB pipe fills, so the next handler thread blocks writing its access log inside `send_response` before the body is sent and every later thread piles up behind it (server alive, CPU idle, threads 2 → 18, connections 0 → 12, curl timing out). A standalone repro (`pipe-stall-repro.js` in the workflow workdir) confirmed it: `DONE ok=1108 fail=5 firstFail=1108`. Fix: spawn the server with `stdio: 'ignore'` in `tests/browser-smoke.js`, `tests/asset-manager-e2e.js`, and `tests/visual-check.js` (same latent pattern in the latter two; none of the three scripts read the server's output). After the fix the full suite passes the validation section and completes.
- Visual QA (quest UI): `quest-visual-qa.js` in the workflow workdir captured 5 screenshots — Quest palette category, placed Add Quest Progress block + properties panel, Quests tab empty state, one quest card, two filled quest cards — and all 5 were opened and inspected: palette rows legible, properties panel (Quest + Amount with fx badge) clean, tab hint + empty state clean, cards stack cleanly with no overlap or clipping. Two non-defects noted: Font Awesome glyphs on the add/delete buttons were blank in this run because the CDN tunnel failed (`ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`), and the description input is narrow so its placeholder truncates — the same shared card pattern as the Items tab, not a quest defect.
- Tests: full suite green, exit 0 — 5693 `ok` lines in the suite log, 125 node files PASS, playtest 44/44, smoke all checks passed (log `cycle0-suite2.log` in the workflow workdir). This finally re-verifies cycle 76's browser gate, which its own entry left unverified because Chromium could not launch then.
- Step 3 (new item): every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented **Advanced batch 10 — the achievement system** (Unity/Game Center/Steam achievements, beginner edition) — the permanent companion to quests: quests reset on a fresh run, achievements stay unlocked across runs, scenes, and restarts. A cutscene/timeline system (Unity Timeline / Unreal Sequencer) was considered and deferred to batch 11 as the bigger, riskier build; waypoint patrol was not reconsidered (rejected twice already as overlapping moveToward + timers). New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-achievement.js` (`HydraAchievement`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-quest.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): the project table `project.achievements` (name + description; `normalizeAchievements` repairs in place; blank names drop, duplicate names collapse to first, descriptions coerce to strings, ids `ach_<n>`); the unlocked-set runtime `{ unlocked: {} }` with `unlockAchievement` / `lockAchievement` / `resetAchievements` (each returning whether the set changed, and persisting on change through the hooks' `persist`), `isAchievementUnlocked` / `achievementCount` / `unlockedAchievementNames` / `achievementList`; per-game storage (`hydraAchievements:<projectId>`, sanitized id, `default` fallback) with `serializeUnlocked` / `parseUnlocked` / `loadUnlocked` / `saveUnlocked` / `safeStorage` (opaque origins degrade to in-memory-only play). Editor: new **Achievements** tab in the Inspector (`getProjectAchievements`, `getMutableAchievements`, `getProjectId`, `propagateAchievementRename` repoints the three achievement-name blocks' `achievement` field across global grid, scene grids, cellData); per-achievement cards with name input + delete and a description input, add-achievement auto-naming `Achievement N` with id `ach_<ts>_<n>`. 6 new **Achievement** blocks in the catalog (398 total, was 392): Unlock / Lock / Reset / Read Achievement Count actions + Achievement Unlocked / Achievement Count conditions (both 'level' trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, evaluate while paused). The unlocked set loads from storage at boot in both runtimes and writes through on every change, so preview and exports share the same per-project store; `resetGame` deliberately never clears it (permanence is the point) and there is no scene-reset entry for it. One real bug caught by the tests while writing the module: the storage seed/parse paths coerced non-string entries to names (`42` → `'42'`), so they now accept only strings (the authored-table normalization keeps the quest-style coercion). Load-order fallout: every test sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load the new module first — a one-off script (`achievement-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir) inserted the load after each quest load (29 runInContext + 49 array-form inserts across 7 files), mirroring the quest fallout.

The player runtime holds `state.quests` (normalized) + `state.questLog` (fresh on boot); the preview runtime holds a module-level runtime (`questLogRuntime()` lazy, one per factory instance), reset in `resetGame` only — the quest log deliberately SURVIVES scene switches inside a run (player state, like the backpack), so there is no `resetQuestLog`-style scene-reset hook for it, and the tests pin that absence. Save slots are untouched (variables only): the manual persistence path is Read Quest Progress into a variable before Save Game and Set Quest Progress after Load Game, documented in the user guide.
