## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 75 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 8 — inventory system COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented it: **the inventory system** (Unity inventory / Godot resource-based items, beginner edition) — the most obviously missing piece for a complete game after dialogue, since RPG/adventure games need keys, potions, and collectibles and Hydra Quest tracks those as bare variables today. A waypoint-patrol capability was considered and rejected as too overlapping with the existing moveToward + timers (cycle 74 had already considered and rejected it on the same grounds). New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-inventory.js` (`HydraInventory`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-dialogue.js` and before `hydra-trace.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): the project table `project.items` (name + maxStack + description; `normalizeItems` repairs in place so the editor tab holds entry references — blank names dropped, duplicate names collapse to the FIRST, maxStack keeps only a finite integer >= 1 capped at 9999, anything else reads as the 99 default, descriptions coerce to strings); the runtime `{ counts: {} }` (name -> count); `addItem` (blank quiet, unknown warns + holds, bad counts warn + holds, clamps at maxStack), `removeItem` (removes up to the count, entry drops at 0), `setItemCount` (clamps 0..maxStack, 0 drops the entry), `readItemCount` (count, or null when blank/unknown), `clearInventory` (empties in place), `itemCount`/`hasItem` (unknown reads 0/false, never warns), `inventoryList` (sorted { name, count } pairs).
- **Editor + both runtimes:** new **Items** tab in the Inspector (`getProjectItems` in script.js, `getMutableItems`, `propagateItemRename` repointing all six item-name blocks' `item` field across the global grid, scene grids, and cellData; per-item cards with name input + delete, Max stack number input (1-9999) + description input, add-item auto-naming `Item N` with id `itm_<ts>_<n>`); 7 new **Inventory** blocks in the catalog (384 total, was 377) — Add Item / Remove Item / Set Item Count / Read Item Count / Clear Inventory actions and Has Item / Item Count conditions (both 'level' trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, evaluate while paused); conditions read `ctx.inventory.counts` directly in `hydra-conditions.js` (like languageIs reads `ctx.language`; an unheld item reads as 0 and the comparison still runs, the Timer Elapsed shape); actions dispatch through `hydra-execute.js` (guarded by `isInventoryAction`, counts through `expr()` so a variable can feed them, `ctx.inventoryHooks()` for warnings); `hydra-flow.js` level-default list; `hydra-universal.js` defaults `project.items` to `[]`; `docs/block-reference.md` table and `docs/user-guide.md` `## Inventory` section. The player runtime holds `state.items` (normalized) + `state.inventory` (fresh on boot); the preview runtime holds a module-level runtime (`inventoryRuntime()` lazy, one per factory instance), reset in `resetGame` only — the backpack deliberately SURVIVES scene switches inside a run (player state, like the language), so there is no `resetDialogue`-style scene-reset hook for it, and the tests pin that absence. Save slots are untouched (variables only): the manual persistence path is Read Item Count into a variable before Save Game and Set Item Count after Load Game, documented in the user guide.
- **One real module fix rides along (found by the unit tests, in the module, not a pre-existing engine bug):** `readItemCount` on a null runtime returned 0 (via `itemCount`'s null tolerance) instead of holding — it now returns null when the runtime itself is missing, so the Read Item Count action holds instead of writing a fabricated 0.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first conditions-test "no backpack at all" pin expected `itemCount >= 0` to read false with no backpack — an unheld item reads as 0 and 0 >= 0 is true, so the pin uses `> 0`; (2) my first browser-smoke category check used the wrong selector (`.block-category, h3, .category-label`) — the palette's category labels carry `.block-category-label`, as every other category check in the file uses; (3) my first browser-smoke placement steps clicked the palette option but never clicked a grid cell — selecting a tool does not place a block, the cell click does (the dialogue section has it; I had missed it); (4) my first browser-smoke second placement never clicked `#back-to-blocks-btn` after reading the first block's properties — placing a block opens the properties panel and hides the palette (`showPropertiesPanel` sets `#block-type-panel` to `display: none`), so the next palette option is unclickable until you go back to the block list (again, the dialogue section has that click; I had missed it).
- **Harness fixes (environmental flakes, not the feature — all three hit this cycle, each at a different point, while the feature checks themselves were green):** (a) the smoke test's `page.goto`/`page.reload` with `waitUntil: 'networkidle'` timed out twice more (cycle 74 had already flaked twice) — the editor loads Font Awesome from a CDN the sandbox cannot reliably reach, and a stalled CDN request starves networkidle past the 30s default. All 38 `networkidle` waits in `tests/browser-smoke.js` are now `domcontentloaded` (all HydraScript scripts are classic, no async/defer, so they execute during parsing before domcontentloaded fires; every step after a navigation already waits for the element it needs). That exposed one real timing dependency: the offline section's fallback stylesheet is injected by script after Tailwind fails, so it now waits (up to 10s, soft-fail) for the body background to read the fallback value before asserting computed styles. (b) `tests/run.js` per-file timeout raised 60s → 120s (same reasoning as the cycle-66 smoke-timeout raise): on this loaded machine (load ~3.4, swap in use) a sub-second file, `gltf.test.js`, was reported ETIMEDOUT even though every check had passed. (c) One run died with a renderer "Target crashed" at the flagship section while `window.scenes` was still undefined — same loaded-machine signature, the flagship section passed on the runs either side of it; no harness change for that one. Note for the next cycle: when the suite is run with stdout redirected to a file, `process.exit()` can truncate pending output and the file comes back empty — run it in the foreground (or through `tee`) so the result is visible; and the full suite takes ~7 minutes wall-clock, poll the background session rather than re-running it.
- **Load-sequence fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-inventory.js` first — a one-off script (`inventory-fallout.py` in the workflow workdir) inserted the load after each dialogue load: 25 runInContext inserts + 45 array-form inserts across `tests/export-boot.test.js` (69), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1); the Phase BA missing-dialogue-module guard check was left untouched. `tests/sceneinit.test.js` needs no change (it never loads player-runtime.js).

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 74 (improve): P4 Advanced batch 7 — dialogue system COMPLETED

- Every ROADMAP item was checked off, so per the cycle rules this cycle researched the next professional-engine capability and implemented it: **the dialogue system** (Unity Dialogue System / Godot Dialogic, beginner edition) — the most obviously missing piece for a complete game, since Hydra Quest has no talking NPCs and RPG/adventure/visual-novel games need one. A waypoint-patrol capability was considered and rejected as too overlapping with the existing moveToward + timers. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-dialogue.js` (`HydraDialogue`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-localization.js` and before `hydra-trace.js`, inlined into exports, hard load guard in `player-runtime.js`): the project table `project.dialogues` (name + lines of speaker/text/choices with a 0-based `goto` line; `normalizeDialogues` repairs in place so the editor tab holds entry references — blank names dropped, duplicate names collapse to the FIRST, blank-label choices dropped, `goto` keeps only finite integers >= 0 and null stays null, since `Number(null)` is 0 and a second normalization had turned "end dialogue" into "go to line 0"); the runtime `{ active, dialogue, lineIndex, revealed, charsPerSec, waitingChoice, speakerObject, textObject }`; typewriter reveal at `charsPerSec` (default 40, 0 = instant) via `updateDialogue`, which runs in both runtimes' `runScripts` phase before the grids, so conditions see the frame's text, and runs while paused (dialogue is UI, world frozen); `startDialogue` (blank quiet, unknown warns + holds, restart restarts, empty-lines ends at once, applies speaker + '' text at start), `advanceDialogue` (complete typing, then next line, then end; quiet on a choice line), `chooseDialogue` (1-based, out-of-range warns + holds, null/invalid goto ends), `stopDialogue`; on end the bound labels keep their last revealed line (the module stops writing, never blanks them, so a label also bound to a localization key is never clobbered). A scene load ends a running dialogue via the new optional `resetDialogue()` hook in `HydraSceneReset` (step 9 in its header, guarded try/catch); dialogue does NOT survive scene switches, unlike language.
- **Editor + both runtimes:** new **Dialogues** tab in the Inspector (`getProjectDialogues` in script.js, `getMutableDialogues`, `propagateDialogueRename` repointing `startDialogue` blocks' `dialogue` field across the global grid, scene grids, and cellData; per-dialogue cards with name input + delete, per-line speaker/text inputs + delete-line, per-choice label + goto select ("End dialogue" or "Line N+1") + delete-choice, "+ Choice" / "+ Line" buttons, add-dialogue auto-naming `Dialogue N` with id `dlg_<ts>_<n>`); 8 new **Dialogue** blocks in the catalog (377 total, was 369) — Start Dialogue / Advance Dialogue / Choose Dialogue Option / Stop Dialogue / Set Choice Text actions and Dialogue Active / Dialogue Waiting For Choice / Dialogue Choice Available conditions (all 'level' trigger default, NOT in `FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`); dispatch through `hydra-conditions.js` (reads `ctx.dialogue` directly, like languageIs reads `ctx.language`) and `hydra-execute.js` (guarded by `isDialogueAction`, `ctx.dialogueHooks()`); `hydra-flow.js` level-default list; `hydra-universal.js` defaults `project.dialogues` to `[]`; `docs/block-reference.md` table and `docs/user-guide.md` `## Dialogue` section. The player runtime holds `state.dialogues` (normalized) + `state.dialogue` (live runtime), updates the typewriter in its `runScripts` hook, and ends the dialogue on scene load; the preview runtime holds a module-level runtime (`dialogueRuntime()` lazy, one per factory instance), updates it in `gameLoop`'s `runScripts` phase, and resets it in `resetGame` + the scene-reset hook.
- **One real display fix rides along (found by the preview tests, in the module, not a pre-existing engine bug):** `updateDialogue` early-returned while waiting for a choice without re-applying the display, so a bound label hidden during the reveal showed the stale line until the next line started. It now re-applies the display while waiting (still returning false, so the "no-op while waiting" contract holds) and the label heals on the next frame after unhiding.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) normalize's first backward iteration kept the LAST duplicate name — fixed to forward iteration keeping the first; (2) goto null coercion: `Number(null)` is 0, so null/undefined/'' goto is tested explicitly before `Number()`; (3) miscounted 'Guards! Seize them!' as 18 chars, actually 19; (4) my first conditions-test advance sequence called advance once from a fresh start, which only completes line 0's typing — advance is two-step (complete, then move on), so the pin advances twice before the choice-line update; (5) my first export-boot Phase BA run failed the final Advance because two consecutive KeyN presses with no gap frame between them never register as a new just-pressed edge — the pin pumps one gap frame first; (6) my first preview-runtime dialogue section required the module directly, but `hydra-dialogue.js` exports the factory function, not the instance — the pin calls it; (7) my first browser-smoke card check looked for '+ Line' and a Speaker label in innerText, but the buttons read 'Line'/'Choice' (the plus is an icon) and Speaker is an input placeholder, which innerText never shows — the pin reads placeholders + button text instead.
- **Load-sequence fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard in `player-runtime.js` means every vm sandbox that loads it must load `hydra-dialogue.js` first — a one-off script (`dialogue-fallout.js` in the workflow workdir) inserted the load after each localization load: 26 runInContext inserts + 45 array-form inserts across `tests/export-boot.test.js` (69), `tests/fps-export.test.js` (1), `tests/flagship.test.js` (1); the Phase AU missing-localization-module guard check was left untouched. `tests/sceneinit.test.js` needs no change (it never loads player-runtime.js).
- **Smoke-test flake (infrastructure, not the feature):** the full suite's `page.goto` to `index.html` with `waitUntil: 'networkidle'` timed out twice at different pages (dispatch page, then the resilience page) across three runs — the page loads Font Awesome CSS from cdnjs, so a slow CDN stall starves networkidle past the 30s default. The CDN answers in under a second when probed directly, and the suite passed clean on the re-run, so no harness change was made; noted here for the next cycle if it recurs.
- Tests: new `tests/dialogue.test.js` (96 checks: surface + factorySource, normalization incl. in-place identity + duplicate collapse to first + goto null coercion, lookups, fresh runtime, start/update/advance/choose/stop, routing sets, hostile input, static wiring incl. catalog validation + docs); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BA (18 checks: export inlines the module, missing-module guard with the clear error, the full conversation through the real grid wiring — start + partial typewriter + active firing + choice waiting + both options existing + the third absent + choose jump + stop/end + scene switch ending the dialogue + unknown name warning and holding, zero diagnostics); `tests/conditions.test.js` (+13: active/waiting/available incl. reversed + no-runtime + not-frozen-while-paused), `tests/execute.test.js` (+16: start/advance/choose/stop/setChoiceText incl. unknown/blank/out-of-range/not-waiting + no-module quiet), `tests/previewruntime.test.js` (+21: module-held runtime incl. per-factory independence, conditionCtx/actionCtx fields, updatePreviewDialogue + the loop advancing the typewriter + advancing while paused, hidden-object skip + unhide heal, sceneResetHooks resetDialogue, resetGame fresh runtime, no-module degradation), `tests/scenereset.test.js` (+4: resetDialogue runs/missing/throwing/non-function), `tests/catalog.test.js` (count pin 369 → 377), `tests/flow.test.js` (dialogue conditions level-default), `tests/browser-smoke.js` (+10: module loaded, 8 palette blocks + Dialogue category, Start Dialogue + Dialogue Active placement + panels, Dialogues tab hint + add flow + card controls). Full suite ALL green (5305 checks).
- Docs: ROADMAP.md gains P4 Advanced batch 7, checked off; CHANGELOG entry added with the honest limits (plain text only, line-number jumps, one dialogue at a time, one speed per Start block).
- Next cycle: every ROADMAP item is checked off again, so the next cycle researches the next professional-engine capability, adds it to ROADMAP.md, and implements it. Note for the next cycle: the full suite takes ~6 minutes wall-clock (the Playwright smoke test dominates); `node tests/run.js` backgrounds after 120s in this environment, so poll the background session rather than re-running it. Also note: `tests/run.js` skips the smoke test entirely when any node test fails (the `if (failures === 0)` gate), so a green node run is not a green suite — always re-run after fixing a node failure so the smoke test actually executes. Also note: a `page.goto` networkidle timeout in the smoke test has now flaked twice at different pages (external Font Awesome CDN stall); if it recurs, consider a goto retry or a longer timeout rather than treating it as a product failure.

- Started the preview runtime host itself (P2 item 14) with its most self-contained piece: **the preview input host**. New DOM module `hydra/hydra-previewruntime.js` (`HydraPreviewRuntime`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-previewtoolbar.js` and before `script.js`) now owns the keyboard / mouse / touch / gamepad input state and wiring — `createInputState()` (mouse position, held/just-pressed/just-released key + mouse sets, touch zones/count, gamepad buttons/edges/axes/connected, mouse deltas, last client point), the key-name helpers (delegating to the shared HydraInput module with the plain trim fallback), `updateMouse`/`pressMouseButton`/`releaseMouseButton`/`updateTouch`, `attachCanvasInput`/`attachKeyboardInput` (property-assigned canvas handlers + document key listeners), `clearEdges` (per-frame), `clearForLaunch` (per-launch), `resetRunInput` (per-run), and `pollGamepad` (per-frame raw gamepad poll + edge derivation, passing the getGamepads function to HydraActions exactly as the old gameLoop code did). Providers reach it only through `configure()` (getHydraInput/getHydraActions/getGamepads, all optional with the globalThis/navigator defaults); the touch-zone lookup is an explicit per-call `zonesAt` argument. Deleted from script.js: `updatePreviewMouse`, `updatePreviewTouch`, `pollEditorGamepads`, the inline key/mouse/touch/gamepad state declarations, and the inline listeners. script.js is 8030 lines (from 8089).
- **In-place mutation is the contract that makes the aliases work:** the module always clears + refills the Sets and copies gamepad axes/deltas field-by-field instead of replacing them (the old code reassigned fresh Sets/objects in the gameLoop poll and resetGame), so a script.js alias never goes stale; `previewGamepadPrev` needed no alias at all (only the moved code used it) and `previewLastClient` lives entirely in the module. The `previewDevice` state and the trace-feed render cache live in the module (they were the script.js `previewDevice` let and `previewState.traceRenderedCount`); `configure()` takes the scene/viewport/clamp/tab-active/profiler/tracer/HydraProfile/HydraTrace providers from script.js, and every provider is optional with a throwing provider degrading to its safe default. The document is the explicit first argument to every DOM function (missing falls back to the global document, missing document is a no-op) and the canvas is always passed in, so the module never reads editor globals. Exports never inline it (no editor toolbar in a shipped game).
- **script.js keeps thin delegations** so every existing call site is unchanged: `resizeGameCanvas` (launchPreview / loadPreviewScene / the module's own resize path), `renderTraceFeed` (processScriptGrid's per-frame call), and the `onFrameEnd` button sync inside `drawGameHooks`; the configure + `initToolbar(document, canvas)` call sits where the old device-frame block was, right after the canvas lookup; `resetGame` calls `resetTraceRender()` where it used to write `previewState.traceRenderedCount = 0`. script.js is 8089 lines (from 8183).
- **One small fix rides along (mine, in the module, not a pre-existing engine bug):** `resetTraceRender` sets the cache to -1, not the old 0 — with 0 the first render of a fresh run could early-return when the previous run had also rendered zero events, leaving a stale feed (the waiting message surviving a restart that starts with tracing off, or vice versa). A fresh run now always rebuilds the feed; the toggle/clear handlers already reset to -1 the same way.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first innerHTML checks matched the word in the module's own header comment — the pins now match an actual `.innerHTML =` write; (2) my first trace-button-active expectation checked the class right after the click, but the class syncs on the next frame through `syncToolbarButtons` (exactly like drawGame's onFrameEnd) — the pin calls the sync first; (3) my first `src="script.js"` order check matched the substring inside an HTML comment ("the wiring lives in script.js") — the pin matches the `src="..."` attribute.
- Tests: new `tests/previewtoolbar.test.js` (57 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation + independent factory instances, device table + copy semantics, setPreviewDevice incl. unknown-id fallback + frame/label/select sync + no-document safety, resize math incl. the framed 251x544 pin + desktop fit-the-pane + no-container scale 1 + no-scene 800x600 + throwing viewport/clamp providers + no-canvas no-op, initToolbar wiring incl. select change + profiler toggle round-trip + trace toggle rendering the feed + clear + window resize refit only while the tab is active + missing-element safety, syncToolbarButtons incl. both buttons + panel visibility + no-module safety, renderTraceFeed incl. off/waiting messages + ordered lines + the unchanged-count cache + new-event rebuild + toggle cache reset + resetTraceRender rebuild + missing-element safety + throwing recent() degrading to the waiting line, static routing that the inline definitions are gone from script.js and the module owns them); `tests/preview-devices.test.js` trimmed to its HTML/CSS wiring checks (the JS pins moved into the new file). The browser smoke test covers the live behavior through the real editor (device select, profiler/trace buttons, trace panel) unchanged.
- Docs: ROADMAP item 14 carries the slice-5 note (still open — the preview runtime host is the next slice, now that the engine extraction is done); CHANGELOG entry added.
- Next cycle: continue P2 item 14 (next slice: the preview runtime host — gameLoop, launchPreview/loadPreviewScene, the script-grid dispatch wiring and its hook builders, now thin delegations over the shared modules). ## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 69 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 9 — per-block action switches as a shared module COMPLETED

- Finished the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its final duplicated subsystem: **the per-block action switches**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-execute.js` (`HydraExecute`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` after `hydra-conditions.js` and before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns every action block both runtimes previously ran in parallel — `executeAction(data, ctx, idx)` over a host-supplied context (variables, live objects, hidden/pending-destroy/destroyed sets, execution context, camera, scene records + push/pop stack, timers, storage, subsystem modules, hook builders, `reportUnknown`), and never throwing: a throwing hook, subsystem call, or corrupt field degrades to the action being skipped.
- **Both runtimes delegate:** export `execute` (one-line delegation through an `actionCtx(state)` builder; 26 action-only helpers deleted, 2249 lines from 2801) and preview `executeAction` (guarded `window.HydraExecute` call through an `actionCtx()` builder, so a dropped tag degrades to actions silently not running; 10 action-only helpers deleted, 8184 lines from 9241). The export hard-guards on `HydraExecute` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`; the preview's `previewSceneStack` is new (reset in `resetGame`, never on a scene switch, since push→switch→pop spans a switch) and the preview's `spawnCounter` is deleted (spawn ids use the export's `spawn_<Date.now()>_<random>` form). The preview's screen-shake cooldown survives as a host hook (`shakeCooldownOk(data, idx)`), and the preview's once-per-session warn dedupes, audio/music playback, scene-switch deferral, and camera clamp/follow stay host-side.
- **Eighteen divergences closed (the point of the slice, export semantics are the reference unless noted):** hidden objects no longer participate in preview actions (showHide keeps its includeHidden lookup); pushScene/popScene/setSceneBackground now work in the preview (were missing entirely); separateObjects now works in exports (was missing entirely); stopObject with no/unknown axis stops both axes in the preview; setStatic no longer zeroes velocity in the preview; setVelocity on a text object now sets the velocity in the preview; setGravity/setFriction/setMaxSpeed/setBounciness no longer clamp or default-fill in the preview (raw expression value); setPhysicsEnabled no longer creates a physics record in the preview; spawnObject no longer resets the clone's rotation to 0 in the preview; spawnAtObject now spawns at the anchor's center for every anchor in the preview; teleport/setPosition accept either toX/toY or x/y and require both expressions to parse; timerControl also accepts the `name` field in both runtimes; timerControl pause/resume on a never-started timer now starts from now in the preview; saveVariable never throws on quota error in the preview and loadVariable leaves the variable unchanged on corrupt JSON; setTile and the toward/point source lookups now exclude hidden objects in the preview; clampToScreen now uses the hitbox-aware clamp with the catalog's `bounce` field in exports (toward the preview); moveToward direct move now uses the frame `dt` in the preview; the jump assist inside forceToward now respects collision layers and ignores same-frame-destroyed objects in exports (toward the preview).
- **One real bug found while wiring the preview host (mine, not a pre-existing engine bug):** the module called the shake-cooldown hook with the cell index only (`shakeCooldownOk(idx)`), so the preview hook I wrote as `(data, idx)` received the index as `data` — the per-block `cooldown` field from the catalog (0.15/0.25 presets) was ignored and every cell shared one `shake_undefined` key. The module now passes `(data, idx)` and the header documents it; the export never defines the hook (absent means no cooldown).
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first spawnAtObject expectation said one clone per spawn — the scene holds two `coin` defs, so one spawn call makes two clones (same id, same tick, one call); the pin is two clones at the anchor center; (2) my first pushScene expectation said the switch stays pending after one frame — the export consumes `pendingSceneId` within the same frame, so after one pump the scene has already switched and the stack holds the old scene; the round-trip pin is the stable state after the pop lands (scene 1, empty stack, popped 1); (3) my first timer resume expectation said lastFiredTime becomes the resume time — resume shifts the start past the paused span (5000 + 3000 = 8000), not to now; (4) my first non-string-type expectation said unknown types do not report — the module reports any unknown type through reportUnknown, matching the export's old default case; (5) my first `calls.includes(...)` assertions compared a string against an array of call tuples — the pins use `calls.some(c => c[0] === ...)`; (6) my first sceneinit.test.js fix referenced `scriptSrc` before its declaration in the block (temporal dead zone) — the check reads the file inline instead.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-execute.js` first — 43 array entries plus 25 verbatim load sequences in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script `execute-fallout.js` in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-68). No other test file executes `player-runtime.js` in a vm sandbox (the rest only read it as text), so no further fallout.
- **Stale static checks (fallout, not engine bugs):** the full suite's first run failed two checks that pinned strings from the deleted inline switches — `camerafx.test.js` pinned the preview's inline shake/flash triggers (`window.HydraCameraFx.triggerShake(previewState.cameraShake...`), and `sceneinit.test.js` pinned the export's inline spawn clone init and the preview's two spawn-site inits. The behavior moved, not broke: the triggers live in the module (`ctx.cameraFx.triggerShake(ctx.shake...)`) with both runtimes passing the module through their action context, and clone init lives in the module's `spawnClone` (one site serving both spawn paths) with both runtimes passing `sceneInit`/`sceneInitHooks` through theirs. Both checks now pin the new locations.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 68 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 8 — per-block condition switches as a shared module COMPLETED

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its next duplicated subsystem: **the per-block condition switches**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-conditions.js` (`HydraConditions`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns every condition block both runtimes previously evaluated in parallel — `evaluateCondition(data, ctx, scope, idx)` over a host-supplied context (variables, input sets, object lookups, timers, physics/tiles/subsystem modules, hook builders, `reportUnknown`), returning `{triggered, context}` and never throwing: a throwing hook, subsystem call, or corrupt field degrades to the condition reading false. The pause freeze set (`FROZEN_WHILE_PAUSED`, 11 world-dependent types) reads false while paused with a reversed one passing; `onTimer` is not in the set — it has its own frozen early return with no record created while paused.
- **Both runtimes delegate:** export `evaluate` (one-line delegation through a `conditionCtx(state)` builder; 40 condition-only helpers deleted, 2804 lines from 3413) and preview `evaluateCondition` (guarded `window.HydraConditions` call through a `conditionCtx()` builder, so a dropped tag degrades to false; 51 condition-only helpers deleted, 9242 lines from 10134). The export hard-guards on `HydraConditions` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`; the preview's DOM work around the dispatch and its launch-time gameStart path stay host-side. One real bug found while writing the module: `subsystemCondition` must forward hooks as the final argument, because `HydraCollections.evalCollectionCondition` takes 3 args `(variables, data, hooks)` while every other subsystem eval takes `(data, hooks)`.
- **Eleven preview divergences closed (the point of the slice, export semantics are the reference):** hidden objects no longer participate in preview conditions (the export's `named()` excluded them; the preview's alive lookups included them, so objectCount, collision, overlapTouch, onFloor, distanceCheck, physics queries, position/velocity checks, textCheck, tile checks, edge checks, mouseOver/objectClicked, and animation/state reads all behaved differently in preview vs export); collision box/box pairs now use shape-aware `pairOverlap` in both (the preview's legacy AABB contact and its `side` context are gone — nothing downstream read `side`); the context reports the export's match (last matching pair for distanceCheck/onFloor, first match for positionCheck/velocityCheck); distanceCheck measures raw position centers, not hitbox centers; chance reports the evaluated, clamped percent; keyJustPressed reports `{key}` and mouseOver/objectClicked report `{obj1, targetObject}` (the preview's extra context keys are dropped); gameStart always carries `{gameStart: true}`; raycastHit, overlapCheck, and lineOfSight ignore objects destroyed earlier in the same frame (the cycle-63 rule); an unknown `logic` value on the `and` block behaves as 'and'; the preview's AABB fallback for collision/overlapTouch without the physics module is kept in the module.
- **Deliberately NOT unified (documented in the module header):** the per-block action switches (`execute` in the export, `executeAction` in the preview) and their helpers — the next and final slice (slice 9); the preview's DOM work around the dispatch (the 'triggered' cell classes, cyan 'traced' ring, Trace feed) and the launch-time gameStart path; the hosts' `scriptApiHooks` builders and subsystem hook builders (the module calls them, it does not own them).
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first onFloor expectations had the wrong match and the wrong quiet case — the context reports the last matching pair (the second hero, id 3), and the wall genuinely rests on the floor too, so the quiet case uses the ghost; (2) my first overlapCheck probe at (100,100) r24 also caught the floor and the label, and at (95,95) r10 still caught the label — the probe is (95,95) r5; (3) my first angleBetween fail case used hero→wall, but the fixture has two heroes and the second sits at 180°, so the fail case uses crate→touch; (4) my first movingTowards case had the runner moving down-right at a wall up-right (cosine negative) — the runner now moves straight down at the skeleton below it; (5) my first timerElapsed expectation said the read path repairs a legacy numeric record — it does not (both hosts' `namedTimerElapsedSeconds` return 0 for non-object records); the repair lives in `getTimerRecord`, so the pin moved to the onTimer path, where a numeric record becomes an object record and fires; (6) my first throwing-expression expectation said getVariable reads false — the module's safe `expr` degrades a throw to 0, so `score >= 5` still fires and the pin is `score <= 5` reading false; (7) my first index.html order check matched bare filenames and tripped on a comment at line 63 mentioning `script.js` — the check matches the `src="` tags; (8) my stub `pairOverlap` expanded only one shape — it now expands both, like the real module. The brace-matcher lesson from the surgery itself: a function whose parameters carry a `= {}` default (e.g. `res`, `getTimerKey`, `conditionResult`) must not be treated as starting its body at that brace — the preview surgery was re-run from git after fixing the matcher to find the body's opening brace past the parameter list.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-conditions.js` first — 42 array entries plus 25 verbatim load sequences in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js` (the bare two-argument form, handled by the same script's `includes` match), and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script `conditions-fallout.js` in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-67). No other test file executes `player-runtime.js` or the preview's `script.js` in a vm sandbox (the rest only read them as text), so no further fallout.
- **Stale static check (fallout, not an engine bug):** the full suite's first run failed exactly one check — `frame.test.js`'s "script.js tilemap lookups use the alive list" pinned three strings from the old inline tile-condition helpers, which this slice deleted. The check now pins the new location.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 67 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 7 — script-grid dispatch as a shared module COMPLETED

- Closed the shared-engine extraction's last large duplicated subsystem: **the script-grid action/condition dispatch**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-dispatch.js` (`HydraDispatch`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the traversal both runtimes previously duplicated — `processScope(grid, scopeKey, hooks)` walking the grid entries in ascending numeric order in two passes (ordinary conditions, then the deferred `and`/`triggerOnce`, which read the contexts pass 1 just wrote), gating each true condition through the flow module's `shouldTrigger` over the host's edge-memory map, noting the trigger through `onTriggered`, recording the `trigger` event in the trace ring, propagating through `HydraFlow.propagate` (passing sub-conditions note only their context through `onPropagateCondition`, never the execution context), reporting unknown block types once through `reportUnknown`, and marking the scope started. The subsystem modules arrive through `hooks` (`flow`, `trace`), so the module never reads a global: the export passes its hard-guarded modules inline, the preview passes `window.HydraFlow`/`window.HydraTrace` through one `dispatchHooks(scope)` helper so the call site can never drift. Nothing in it ever throws: a throwing evaluate reads as not-triggered, throwing bookkeeping/trace hooks are swallowed, a throwing action stops that one propagation (the rest of the scope still runs), a frozen edge-memory map skips the trigger, a missing flow module or evaluator means the scope is not processed at all.
- **Both runtimes delegate:** export `processScope` (thin wrapper building the grid + `dispatchHooks(state, scope)`, keeping the `runScripts` hook unchanged) and preview `processScriptGrid` (DOM 'triggered'/'traced' work stays host-side, then one guarded `window.HydraDispatch?.processScope?.(...)` call). The export's `runCondition` and the preview's `processDeterministicConditions` + `DEFERRED_CONDITION_TYPES` are deleted; the per-block condition/action switches (`evaluate`/`execute` in the export, `evaluateCondition`/`executeAction` in the preview) and their dozens of helpers stay host-side — the next extraction, not this one.
- **One real preview divergence closed (the point of the slice):** edge-triggered conditions now fire once per false->true transition in the preview. The preview's per-frame context reset wiped `flowTriggerStates` every frame (via the `resetContexts` phase, which runs before `runScripts`), so an edge condition that stayed true re-fired on every frame in the preview while firing once in exports. The map is now cleared only in `resetGame` (a fresh run, mirroring the export's fresh state per session); scene switches never cleared it in either runtime, so that stays as-is.
- **One contract aligned, no live change:** the two hosts' propagate paths disagreed in code about what a condition evaluated during propagation records — the export wrote only the per-frame contexts store, the preview wrote the contexts store AND the execution context. A probe against the real `HydraFlow` showed the gate path never runs today (flow edges target actions only; condition-to-condition gating is deferred), so this changes no live behavior; the shared dispatch now defines one contract for whenever gating lands (gates note context only, chained actions keep seeing the trigger's context). The pick-as-gate worry from the same probe is moot for the same reason: picks only ever ride the trigger's context, identically in both runtimes.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first dispatch-test gate expectation said the gate fires the trigger hook once — wrong: the gate condition is also a top-level condition in the same grid, so it fires as a trigger itself (triggered 0 and 5); the stub flow now propagates only from index 0 and the assertion pins the gate note separately. (2) My first Phase BD layout put the 'and'-feeder `always` at 181, directly above the `seen` action at 121 — the feeder's own propagate fired the action through up-adjacency, so `seen` read 6 instead of 1; the feeder moved to 240 (below the 'and', with the unknown block moved to 300).
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-dispatch.js` first — 41 array entries plus 25 verbatim load sequences in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-66).
- Tests: new `tests/dispatch.test.js` (42 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation, pass order incl. deferred-after-ordinary + numeric order + deferred chaining, edge gating incl. the held-across-frames pin + false-reset + fresh-map re-fire + level every-frame, hook contracts incl. trigger context + startedScopes + unknown reporting, trace recording incl. the no-trace degradation, the propagate-gate contract via a stub flow, hostile input incl. missing flow/evaluate/execute + throwing evaluate/action/hooks + frozen edge map + frozen startedScopes + non-function classifiers, static routing checks that the inline traversal is gone from both runtimes and the module loads before both); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BD (12 checks: missing-module guard with the clear error, export inlining in Phase A and in the BD session, the real loop over 5 frames — gameStart once, always 5, edge objectVisible once, deferred 'and' once, the unknown block warning exactly once naming type + cell, the trace ring recording gameStart first and the 'and' trigger after the pass-1 triggers); 4 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, seeded preview project: edge `seen` stays 1 after a second while level `ticks` climbs past 5, zero page errors). Full suite ALL green.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 66 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 6 — draw passes as a shared module COMPLETED

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its last large duplicated subsystem: **the draw passes**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-draw.js` (`HydraDraw`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the full frame both runtimes previously drew in parallel — `drawFrame(ctx, view, hooks)` composing background (bg image at the world size or the bg color), the 3D pass (Hydra3d projector over live 3D kinds + lights, host model-mesh resolver), the world pass (live objects sorted by layer, skipping destroyed/hidden/HUD/light3d/light2d/3D-kinds-in-3D-scenes), particles (zoom-scaled, world-space), the darkness overlay (2D scenes, darkness > 0, host dark canvas), the HUD pass, the screen-flash wash, and the profiler overlay, plus the per-kind draws `drawTextObject`/`drawPanelObject`/`drawTilemapObject`/`drawSpriteObject` and the small helpers (`normalizeCameraZoom`, `shakenCamera`, `roundedRectPath`, `drawScaleOf`/`applyDrawTransform`, `drawFitted`, `isScreenFixed`, `widgetScreenPos`). The subsystem modules arrive through `hooks` (ui/tiles/anim/blendSpace/skeleton/layerFx/light2d/threeD/cameraFx/particles/profile), so the module never reads a global: the export passes its hard-guarded modules inline, the preview passes `window.Hydra*` (or null) through one `drawGameHooks()` helper so the call site can never drift. Nothing in it ever throws: a missing/throwing hook degrades to its safe default, a throwing per-object draw or pass is skipped, a non-array object list draws the background only.
- **Both runtimes delegate:** export `draw(state)` (thin wrapper building `drawView(state)` + `drawHooks(state)`, keeping the shaken render camera on `state.renderCamera` and the normalized 3D camera on `state.camera3d`, called as the stepFrame draw hook exactly as before) and preview `drawGame()` (guarded `window.HydraDraw` call building `drawGameView()` + `drawGameHooks()`). The export's ~360-line inline draw block (draw/drawAny/drawTilemap/widgetScreenPos/isScreenFixedObj/roundedRectPath/buttonUiState/strokeWidgetBorder/drawPanel/drawScaleOf/applyDrawTransform/drawText/drawObject/drawFitted/renderCamera) is gone except `buttonUiState`, kept as the live-mouse host helper for the buttonState hook; the preview's ~380-line drawGame (nested drawPreview* helpers) plus the top-level `previewRoundedRectPath` and `drawImageInObject` (both draw-only) are gone.
- **One real preview divergence closed (the point of the slice):** rotated text now rotates in exports — the preview rotated text about the text-box center, the export ignored `obj.rotation` entirely, so a tilted label showed tilted in the preview and straight in the exported game. Two smaller unifications: text without a width now centers on the measured text width in both (the preview's `measureText` fallback; a context without measureText reads as 0, exactly the export's old width of 1), and the background image now draws at `worldWidth || width` (the export's world size, the preview's synced alias — identical on normalized scenes, safer on unnormalized ones).
- **Deliberately NOT unified (documented in the module header):** button hover/press state (the export computes it live from the mouse inside the text draw; the preview reads the per-frame `_uiState` that `updateUiWidgetStates` precomputes in gameLoop — same screen-box math, different timing), the Profiler/Trace toolbar sync (preview-only editor DOM, behind the `onFrameEnd` hook), the showLabel name tags (preview-only, behind the `drawLabel` hook), and dark-canvas creation (host-side via the `getDarkCanvas` hook: the export's vm-stub-safe lazy creation vs the preview's direct createElement on previewState). The object list is sorted once and reused for the HUD pass (the export sorted twice with identical order). Deliberate resilience delta, in the direction of the frame slice: a throwing per-object draw or pass is skipped instead of killing the rAF chain.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first drawFitted expectations mixed up cover and contain — cover scales to the height and crops the width (200x100 into 40x40 draws at -40,-20,80,40), contain letterboxes (draws at -20,-10,40,20); (2) my first image-sprite hook returned the image for every asset id, so the background pass drew it too — the hook now returns the image only for the sprite's asset id; (3) my first shake expectation had the sign backwards — the camera moves the world the opposite way (shake +5 moves the sprite draw to 115, not 125); (4) button/panel backgrounds draw with `fill()` after a path, not `fillRect`, so the assertions count `fill` calls; (5) the light3d skip needs the threeD module present in the hooks (null threeD means no light3d check, like the preview with a missing module); (6) Phase BC's `redFills` helper is block-scoped to Phase AW, so the phase defines its own `bcRedFills`.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-draw.js` first — 40 array entries plus 25 verbatim load sequences in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js` (bare two-argument form, one-line hand edit after the script), and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-65).
- Tests: new `tests/draw.test.js` (79 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation, zoom/shake math, rounded-rect/fitted/scale helpers, hostile input, background incl. worldWidth||width fallback, world sprites incl. skips/layer order/shake, text incl. the rotation divergence + measureText fallback + button state hook, panel/tilemap incl. throwing-tilemap resilience, the 3D pass incl. list filtering + mesh resolver, particles/darkness/HUD/flash/profiler/onFrameEnd/drawLabel, static routing checks that the inline draw bodies are gone from both runtimes and the module loads before both); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BC (13 checks: missing-module guard with the clear error, export inlining, one real frame drawing the background, hero sprite, rotated label, panel, tilemap row, HUD button after the world objects, and the flash wash, the profiler counting all five drawn objects, the shaken render camera kept on the state, save/restore balance, zero diagnostics); 7 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, seeded preview project: hero red / background black / tilemap row / panel color / HUD button blue canvas pixels, zero page errors).

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 65 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 5 — scene-load runtime state reset as a shared module COMPLETED

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its next duplicated subsystem: **the scene-load runtime state reset**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-scenereset.js` (`HydraSceneReset`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the fresh run state every scene load starts from — `resetSceneRuntime(state, scene, hooks)` sets pendingSceneId null + sceneId, a fresh 2D camera record, the 3D camera from the hook (null when the hook is missing or throws) + camera3dFollow null, a fresh particle state from the hook (null when missing/throwing), clears the per-scene collections through hooks (hidden/destroyed/pendingDestroy/timers/once/startedScopes), runs the resetContexts hook, unpauses with a fresh clock, and clears the trigger edge state (`_triggerContacts`/`_triggerPairs`/`_triggerEntered`/`_triggerExited`). Nothing in it ever throws: a throwing hook degrades to its safe default, a frozen state object returns false instead of breaking scene load.
- **Three call sites delegate:** export `loadScene` (inline hooks object) and preview `loadPreviewScene` + `launchPreview` (one `sceneResetHooks()` helper so the two preview call sites can never drift apart, like `sceneInitHooks()`/`physicsStepHooks()`). The export hard-guards on `HydraSceneReset` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`; the preview guards both calls, so a dropped tag degrades to an unreset scene load, the house guarded-call degradation.
- **Two real preview divergences closed (the point of the slice):** (1) preview scene switches now also clear the condition contexts — the export's loadScene always reset `state.contexts` + `executionContext`, the preview's loadPreviewScene never touched `previewState.conditionContexts` (no observable behavior change: contexts are per-frame scratch cleared every frame by the frame loop's resetContexts phase). (2) preview restarts now clear a pending scene switch — launchPreview delegates to the same reset, so a switchScene queued before a restart no longer fires on the restarted run's first frame and yanks the player to the other scene (the export's start path always cleared it).
- **Deliberately NOT unified (documented in the module header):** frameId (the preview resets it to 0 on a scene switch, the export keeps it counting for the trace ring/profiler), and camera shake/flash (the export never resets them on scene load, the preview resets the shake only — resetting them in the export would cut a shake short on a switch). Editor-only bits stay host-side (clearRuntimeDiagnostics, normalizeSceneDimensions, cameraShakeCooldowns, spawnCounter, resizeGameCanvas, processGameStartConditions; resizeCanvas stays in the export).
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-scenereset.js` first — 25 verbatim load sequences plus 39 module-name arrays in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-64; the fps-export sequence uses the bare two-argument form, so it needed a one-line hand edit after the script).
- **Test-authoring note (mine, not an engine bug):** my probe showed setCamera's Y move clamping to 0 in a 600-tall world (worldHeight − viewportHeight = 0), so the Phase BB / smoke scene-1 world is 1600x1200 against an 800x600 viewport and the reset pins both axes (120, 40) → (0, 0). The smoke test skips pauseGame in the preview chain on purpose: the preview fires gameStart chains at launch, so a pause there would freeze frame 1's physics step before the trigger contact could populate — the pause reset is pinned in export-boot Phase BB instead, where gameStart fires inside the frame after physics.
- Tests: new `tests/scenereset.test.js` (56 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation, the full reset incl. fresh-record aliasing + scene-without-id, hook degradation incl. missing/throwing camera3d/particle factories + throwing/non-function clears + frozen state/collections, the return contract, static routing checks that the inline reset is gone from all three call sites and the module loads before both runtimes); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BB (21 checks: missing-module guard, export inlining, a no-switch control session proving frame 1 dirties every field — camera (120, 40), paused, named timer, trigger pair '2:1' with one contact, non-empty contexts, both scopes started, 10 burst particles — then the switch session proving the deferred switch resets them all, the reset holding on frame 2, zero diagnostics); 8 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, control page dirty + switch page clean with the trigger-entered variable surviving the switch as the non-vacuous proof the trigger fired in frame 1, zero page errors). Full suite ALL green.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 64 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 4 — per-frame 2D physics step as a shared module COMPLETED

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its next duplicated subsystem: **the per-frame 2D physics step**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-physicsstep.js` (`HydraPhysicsStep`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the three passes both runtimes previously duplicated — `integrateBody` (prev snapshot + gravity/friction/clamp/move), `resolveObjectCollisions` (statics/dynamics split, trigger contacts + enter/exit edges, `resolvePair` application), and `resolveTileCollisions` (solid-tile pass), composed by `stepPhysics2d(objects, dt, hooks)` which returns the trigger summary. The subsystem modules arrive through `hooks` (`getPhysics`, `hitbox`, `isCollisionObject`, `is3dKind`/`isLight3d`, `physics2d`, `tiles`, `prevTriggerPairs`), so the module never reads a global: the export passes its hard-guarded modules inline, the preview passes `window.Hydra*` through one `physicsStepHooks()` helper so the call site can never drift. Nothing in it ever throws: a throwing reader/subsystem call degrades to defaults or a skipped pair, null entries are skipped, a non-array list is a no-op.
- **Both runtimes delegate with their old pass bodies moved verbatim into hooks:** export `updatePhysics` (live-list filter + step + trigger summary written back to state) and preview `gameLoop`'s `updatePhysics2d` hook (guarded call, trigger summary written back to previewState). The export hard-guards on `HydraPhysicsStep` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`; the preview guards the call, so a dropped tag degrades to a frozen world for the frame, the house guarded-call degradation. The preview's `resolveCollisions` / `resolveTilemapCollisions` / `getPreviousHitbox` are deleted (their only callers were the hook; `getAABBOverlap` stays — `separateObjectsPair` still uses it). `isCollisionObject` stays in both runtimes (conditions use it); `hitbox`/`getHitbox` stay (draw + condition code uses them).
- **Two real preview divergences closed (the point of the slice):** (1) text objects with collision enabled moved under physics in the preview (`!isCollisionObject(obj) && obj.kind === 'text'` skipped only non-collidable text) but never in exports — text is now always skipped in both runtimes. (2) light3d objects had no explicit skip in the preview (only the 3D-kind check); a light3d with physicsEnabled set would have moved in the preview while the export held it still — lights now never move under physics in either runtime.
- **Deliberate deltas (all in the module header):** the preview's legacy AABB fallback when HydraPhysics2D was missing is gone — with no physics2d the step integrates velocities but resolves no collisions and reports no trigger contacts (guarded-call degradation; the export is unaffected, it hard-guards on HydraPhysics2D at load). Hostile-data hardening with no live change on valid data: a non-finite dt reads as 0, a getPhysics reader returning undefined fields or throwing degrades to defaults (the export's `Object.assign` reader copied undefined over the defaults, so a gravity of undefined produced NaN velocities), a missing posX/posY reads as 0 instead of NaN. The module computes the previous hitbox through the host's hitbox hook over a shifted copy, exactly as the preview's `getPreviousHitbox` did.
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** my first direct `resolveObjectCollisions`/`resolveTileCollisions` expectations placed bodies clear of the floor/tiles and expected them to land — the resolvers resolve an existing overlap, they do not integrate; the bodies now start already intersecting (posY 170 over the floor, posY 310 over the tile row) with `_prevY` set, and the multi-frame resting check moved to `stepPhysics2d` where it belongs. My first browser-smoke seed used gravity 10, and the hero tunneled through the 20px floor on a real-time frame hitch (a dt≈3 frame moves it `velY × dt` in one step, jumping it clean past the floor) — pre-existing tunneling in both runtimes (the old inline code had the identical integration + `resolvePair` math; the module preserves it exactly), not a slice regression; the seed now uses gravity 1, so the worst single-frame move is 3px and the settle check is stable. The exact gravity-10 landing is pinned deterministically in export-boot Phase BA with fixed dt.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-physicsstep.js` first — 25 verbatim load sequences plus 38 module-name arrays in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61-63).
- Tests: new `tests/physicsstep.test.js` (74 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation, the body predicate incl. both divergences + missing-hook contract, integration incl. gravity/friction/clamp/prev snapshot/hostile dt/throwing reader/undefined fields/frozen objects, object collision incl. landing/trigger enter-stay-exit/layer gating/no-physics2d degradation/throwing resolvePair, tile collision incl. landing/no-collision-record/disabled/no-tiles-module/layer gating/tilemap-never-dynamic, the full step incl. both divergences + tile landing + trigger summary + empty-hooks degradation + frozen objects, static routing checks that the inline integration/collision math and the old resolver functions are gone from both runtimes and the module loads before both); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase BA (10 checks: missing-module guard with the clear error, export inlining, the real loop — frame-1 dt 0 nothing moves, frame-2 hero falls one step, frame-3 hero lands at posY 160 velY 0, frames 4-5 hero stays put and the collision-enabled label never falls, zero diagnostics); 5 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, seeded preview project: hero falls and lands at posY 160 velY 0, label never falls, position frozen across a 600ms re-read, zero page errors). Full suite ALL green.
- Docs: ROADMAP item 15/12 carries the slice-4 note (still open — the rest of scene loading / state init and the draw passes remain duplicated); CHANGELOG entry added (and the slice-1/2/3 entries' honest limits corrected: the physics step is no longer a separate copy).

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 63 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 3 — per-frame update order as a shared module COMPLETED

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its next duplicated subsystem: **the per-frame update order**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-frame.js` (`HydraFrame`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the canonical phase sequence both runtimes step through — `computeDt(lastTime, timestamp)` (realMs = max(0, timestamp - lastTime), dt = min(realMs / 16.67, 3), falsy/non-finite lastTime is the first frame with dt 0, matching both hosts' old `if (!lastTime)` checks) and `stepFrame(host)` (calls the host's phase hooks in order: profile, notePaused when paused, the unpaused world sim — decayCameraFx, updateCamera, updatePhysics2d, updatePhysics3d, solveJoints, updateAnimations, updateBlendSpaces, updateSkeletons, updateTweens, updateParticles — then updateUiWidgets, resetContexts, pollInput, runScripts, surfaceWarnings, applyPendingDestroy, applyPendingScene, draw, clearEdges; `PHASES` exports the order). Every hook is optional (missing/non-function skipped), a non-object host is a no-op returning false, and nothing ever throws: a throwing phase is reported to the optional `onPhaseError(phase, error)` hook and the frame continues.
- **Both loops delegate with their old phase bodies moved verbatim into hooks:** export `loop` (timing via computeDt, stepFrame with the hard-guarded modules, `draw` + `clearEdges` as hooks, rAF scheduling unchanged) and preview `gameLoop` (timing via computeDt with the same 3-line math as an inline fallback when the tag is missing, stepFrame with guarded module calls, `drawGame` stays outside the loop so the scene still renders while the game is stopped). The export is inlined via `sharedSources` and hard-guards on `HydraFrame` like every other shared module; the preview guards the stepFrame call, so a dropped tag degrades to a drawn-but-frozen preview, the house guarded-call degradation.
- **Two real preview divergences closed (the point of the slice):** (1) joints now solve AFTER 3D physics in the preview (export order) instead of before — both are collision response, so joints get the final say over every collider in both runtimes; only a 3D object with a joint could ever observe the difference. (2) pending-destroy application now happens AFTER scripts in the preview (export order) instead of before — a destroyed object no longer gets one extra physics step in the preview on the frame after it was destroyed. The preview's script-time lookups already excluded pending-destroy objects through `getAlivePreviewObjects()`; the eight remaining raw-list reads now exclude it too (frame live-object snapshot, camera-follow target, expression lookups, the two tilemap-condition lookups, the setTile action, the include-hidden action pool, forceToward's source list), so scripts still never observe a destroyed object — exactly the export's `named()` semantics. The 2D-physics bodies themselves were NOT unified (the preview's text/collision-object skip and missing light3d skip differ from the export's — a body divergence, not an ordering one, left for a later slice).
- **Deliberate resilience delta:** a throwing phase no longer freezes the game. The old inline loops had no guard at all — one throwing subsystem killed the rAF chain and the game froze. A phase error is still a bug and still shows up in the test suite's outcome assertions; it just no longer takes the game down in front of a player.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-frame.js` first — 25 verbatim load sequences plus 37 module-name arrays in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load (one-off script in the workflow workdir, same pattern as cycles 61/62; the "26th" sequence from cycle 62's count is Phase AY's `filter(m => m !== 'hydra-sceneinit.js')` line, which correctly stays a filter).
- Tests: new `tests/frame.test.js` (46 checks: surface + factorySource re-instantiation, PHASES order incl. joints-after-3D-physics and destroy-after-scripts, computeDt incl. first-frame/hitch/backwards/hostile, unpaused + paused stepFrame order, missing/non-function hooks, throwing phase + throwing isPaused + throwing reporter, static routing checks that the inline dt math and old phase sequence are gone from both loops and the module loads before both); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase AZ (10 checks: missing-module guard with the clear error, export inlining in Phase A and in the AZ session, the real loop through stepFrame — frame-1 dt 0 with tick 1 and no fall, frame-2 dt 1 with the victim falling exactly 10px before the scripts run, destroy firing at tick 2, the destroy visible to later scripts in the same frame via objectCount 0, no further physics steps on frames 3-4, zero diagnostics); 5 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, seeded preview project: destroy visible same-frame with the victim fallen once, position frozen across a 600ms re-read, zero page errors). Full suite ALL green.

- Continued the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12) with its next duplicated subsystem: **scene-load object init**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-sceneinit.js` (`HydraSceneInit`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the fresh-runtime-state sequence every object gets when it enters the live world — `initSceneObjects(objects, hooks)` (2D + 3D velocity to 0, `_runtimeText` cleared, `_alive` true, fresh animation state with play-at-start autoplay, fresh state machine entering `animStateInitial` with the initial state winning over play-at-start, fresh blend + skeleton runtimes, fresh `_tweens`/`_tweenFinished`) and `applyBoundTexts(objects, localization, language, boundTextFor)` (the localization pass at the same points: bound text objects start with their translation in the run's current language, unbound texts and untranslated keys keep their authored text). The subsystem modules arrive through `hooks` (`{ anim, stateMachine, blendSpace, skeleton, warn }`), so the module never reads a global: the export passes its hard-guarded modules, the preview passes `window.HydraAnim` etc. (or null) through one `sceneInitHooks()` helper so the four preview call sites can never drift apart. Nothing in it ever throws: a missing subsystem degrades to a null runtime field (the preview's `?.` behavior), a throwing subsystem call leaves that one field null and the rest of the object still initializes, null entries are skipped, a non-array list is a no-op.
- **Six call sites delegate:** export `loadScene` (per-object init + bound-text pass) and `spawnAt` (clone init), preview `launchPreview` (per-object init + bound-text pass), `loadPreviewScene`, and both spawn paths (`spawnObject`, `spawnAtObject`). The preview guards every call (`window.HydraSceneInit?.`), so a dropped module tag degrades to uninitialized objects with the gameLoop's lazy init covering `_anim`/`_blends`/`_skel` — the same guarded-call degradation the other shared modules use; the export hard-guards on `HydraSceneInit` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`.
- **Two real preview divergences closed (the point of the slice, not a side effect):** (1) preview scene switches (`loadPreviewScene`) previously only reset velocity/`_alive`/`_runtimeText`, so a scene switched into mid-game started its objects half-initialized — no play-at-start autoplay, no initial-state entry, no 3D-velocity reset, no fresh tween state — while the same switch in an export started them fully. Scene switches now run the full init. (2) preview spawns previously only set velocity/`_alive`/`_runtimeText`, so a spawned clone never auto-played its start animation or entered its initial state, while the export's `spawnAt` did both. Preview spawns now run the full init. Export spawns additionally gain the 3D-velocity/`_runtimeText`/`_alive` resets through the same call; all three were already 0/undefined/true-equivalent on a fresh clone, so no live behavior changes there.
- **Test-authoring fix (mine, not an engine bug):** my first Phase AY expectation said re-entering scene 1 spawns a *second* clone — wrong: `loadScene` replaces the whole object list on every switch, so the first clone is discarded with the old scene and the re-fired gameStart spawns exactly one fresh clone. ## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 61 (improve): P2 item 15/12 slice 1 — camera effects as a shared module COMPLETED

- Started the shared-engine extraction (P2 item 15/12, the highest-risk item, done last) with its smallest duplicated subsystem: **camera effects**. New shared pure module `hydra/hydra-camerafx.js` (`HydraCameraFx`, factorySource pattern, loaded in `index.html` before `player-runtime.js` and `script.js`) now owns the screen-shake + screen-flash logic both runtimes previously duplicated verbatim — `isValidHexColor`, `createShake`/`createFlash`, `normalizeShake`/`normalizeFlash` (in-place repair), `triggerShake`/`triggerFlash` (in-place, never replace the record), `decay` (both timeLefts, floored at 0), `shakeOffset` (fresh random jitter in [-strength, +strength] per axis, {0,0} when idle), and `flashAlpha` (the linear `intensity * timeLeft / maxTime` fade, clamped 0-1). Nothing in it ever throws: corrupt records repair in place, null records degrade to safe defaults, non-finite numbers read as 0.
- **Semantics preserved exactly (verified, not assumed):** shake fires replace the running shake outright; flash fires hold the old flash on a bad color (the module returns false and the host warns — once per editor session in the preview via `warnFlashColor`, one diagnostics entry per fire in exports); intensity clamps 0-1; zero/negative duration records a flash with timeLeft 0 that draws nothing; decay runs inside the unpaused update so both effects freeze while paused; the flash draws above the HUD, below the profiler overlay. The preview keeps its shake cooldown (preview-only: exports never had one) and its guarded-call degradation (a dropped module tag means shake/flash silently stop firing, the house style for guarded modules); the export hard-guards on `HydraCameraFx` like every other shared module and inlines it via `sharedSources`.
- **Wiring:** export (`hydra/player-runtime.js` — hard guard, state init via `createShake`/`createFlash`, decay in `loop`, both dispatch cases, `renderCamera` via `shakeOffset`, flash overlay via `flashAlpha`, `setSceneBackground` via the module's `isValidHexColor`, the local `isValidHexColor` definition removed, `HydraCameraFx` added to `sharedSources`) and preview (`hydra/script.js` — state init via the module with literal fallbacks, decay in `gameLoop`, both dispatch cases, shake offset and flash alpha in `drawGame`). Host state shapes are unchanged (`state.shake`/`state.flash` in exports, `previewState.cameraShake`/`previewState.flash` in the preview), so the Phase AW flash assertions needed no edits.
- **Test-load fallout (mechanical, not engine bugs):** the new hard guard means every node sandbox that loads `player-runtime.js` must load `hydra-camerafx.js` first — 25 verbatim load sequences plus 35 module-name arrays in `tests/export-boot.test.js`, the single sequence in `tests/fps-export.test.js`, and the `loadRuntime` list in `tests/flagship.test.js` all gained the load. The export-boot Phase A inline check and the Phase AW session list gained it too.
- Tests: new `tests/camerafx.test.js` (75 checks: surface + factorySource, color validation, fresh records, normalization incl. null/junk, trigger clamps + NaN/Infinity safety + in-place identity, bad-color hold, zero-duration no-op, decay incl. hostile steps, shake-offset bounds + jitter, flash-alpha fade + clamps, static routing checks that the inline decay/alpha/jitter/hex-regex math is gone from both runtimes and the module loads before both); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase AX (9 checks: missing-module guard with the clear error, shake state + decay + chained action, flash fired by the same chain, record identity across frames and retriggers, shake pause-freeze + resume) + Phase A inline check; 4 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (module surface live in the editor, Screen Shake in the palette, placement, Strength + Seconds panel fields). Full suite ALL green.
- Docs: ROADMAP item 15/12 carries the slice-1 note (still open — the per-frame update, scene loading, and draw passes remain duplicated); CHANGELOG entry added.
- Next cycle: continue P2 item 15/12 (next slice: the per-frame update ordering or the scene-load/state-init path — the two largest remaining duplications), or take a preview-only UI sub-slice if the engine work stays parked. The engine extraction stays the highest-risk item and proceeds one subsystem at a time.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 60 (improve): v4 gap closed — Screen Flash block COMPLETED

- Implemented the recorded v4 gap from cycle 54: a **Screen Flash** action block, in the Camera category next to Screen Shake (v4's Effects category does not exist in v3). Catalog entry in `hydra/block-catalog.js` — Color (color picker), Intensity (expr, 0-1, default 0.6), Seconds (expr, default 0.2) — plus two Game Flow Presets, **White screen flash** (white, 0.6, 0.2s, for pickups/scene changes) and **Red damage flash** (red, 0.7, 0.25s, for taking damage).
- **Semantics (identical in both runtimes, mirroring screenShake):** firing sets `flash = { color, intensity clamped 0-1, timeLeft, maxTime }` in ms; the wash fades linearly from full intensity to transparent (`alpha = intensity * timeLeft / maxTime`) and draws above the HUD, below the profiler overlay, so debug info stays readable through a flash. The decay runs inside the unpaused update, so a flash freezes while paused like the shake. A bad color warns and holds the old flash (once per editor session in the preview via `warnFlashColor`, one diagnostics entry per fire in exports); a zero/negative duration is a no-op flash; `HydraExpr.evaluate` always returns a finite number, so no NaN can reach the state. Unlike v4's frame-counted duration (10 frames, alpha relative to a 10-frame window), v3 uses seconds like the shake block, so the fade is exact for any duration.
- **Wiring:** preview (`hydra/script.js` — `previewState.flash`, decay in `gameLoop`, dispatch case next to `screenShake`, overlay in `drawGame`) and export (`hydra/player-runtime.js` — state init, decay, `screenFlash` added to the `isAction` list, dispatch case, overlay in `draw()`) both changed; no new shared module was needed (the logic is inline in both runtimes, like the shake).
- **Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs):** (1) my first catalog entry used the bare identifier `control: color` — the color control is the string `'color'` (expr/obj are the bare constants), so the catalog failed to load; (2) my first export-boot draw check expected the flash rect at 300x150 (the stub canvas size) — the runtime resizes the canvas to the 800x600 scene viewport at start, so the wash is `[0, 0, 800, 600]` (a throwaway debug script in the workflow workdir proved it); (3) the catalog test pins the block count, 366 → 369 (1 block + 2 presets).
- Tests: `tests/catalog.test.js` now asserts `KNOWN_GAPS` is empty (the last v4 gap is closed); new `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase AW (13 checks: flash state + chained action + silent path, fullscreen red wash recorded through a recording canvas context, fade-out over the duration, nothing drawn once finished, pause freeze + resume, intensity clamps both ways, bad color warns + holds while the chained action still runs, zero duration draws nothing); 6 new `tests/browser-smoke.js` checks (Camera category, Screen Flash in the palette, both presets, placement, Color field, Intensity + Seconds fields). Full suite ALL green.
- Docs: block reference gained the Screen Flash entry (Camera 8 → 9) and the two preset entries (Game Flow Presets 8 → 10); user guide Camera section gained the shake/flash bullet and the count (Eight → Nine); ROADMAP item 14/11 (catalog) carries the closure note. CHANGELOG entry added.
- **Assessment for the next cycle (recorded, not a re-scope):** P2 item 14's remaining slice is the preview runtime, but the preview runtime is a second engine implementation — `gameLoop`, `drawGame`, and the whole `processScriptGrid` action/condition dispatch in `script.js` duplicate `player-runtime.js`. Moving that verbatim into a DOM module would relocate the duplication rather than remove it, so the honest next step for that slice is the shared-engine extraction (item 15/12), or a sub-slice of preview-only UI (toolbar, device frames) if the engine work stays parked. Cycle 60 took the v4 gap instead, per the cycle-59 note.

## 2026-11-05 — Cycle 59 (improve): P2 item 14 slice 4 — scene stage as a provider-injected DOM module COMPLETED

- Implemented the fourth slice of P2 item 14 (editor becomes a DOM-only module): the scene stage now lives in `hydra/hydra-stage.js` — `normalizeSceneDimensions` + the four world/viewport getters, `snapValue` / `clampZoom` / `kindName3d`, `applySceneZoom` / `getScenePointFromEvent`, `updateCameraFrame` / `applySceneSize` / `updateSceneBg`, and the in-editor 3D viewport (`isScene3dScene`, `get3dView` / `set3dView` / `update3dViewToggle` / `render3dView`) with its Scene Settings field sync (`syncScene3dCameraFields` / `onScene3dCameraInput`, `syncSceneDarknessFields` / `onSceneDarknessInput`) — and `script.js` keeps thin one-line delegations so all call sites are unchanged. The 3D-view flag that was the script.js `sceneEditor3dView` let now lives in the module (independent per instance); the zoom level stays a script.js let because the drag code reads it directly, and script.js passes it into the zoom/point functions.
- **Provider injection:** `configure()` takes, alongside `onDirty`, `normalizeScene` (script.js passes HydraUniversal.normalizeScene; unconfigured it resolves from `globalThis.HydraUniversal`, and without that the local math still runs), `getBgDataUrl` (assetLibrary lookup), `getImage` and `resolveModelMeshFor` (the 3D viewport background and model meshes), and `getHydra3d` / `getHydraLight2d` (defaulting to the globals). A throwing provider degrades to its safe default instead of breaking the stage. The document is the explicit first argument to every DOM function (falling back to the global document; a missing document is a no-op) and the scene is always passed in, so the module never reads editor globals.
- **Bodies moved verbatim, four documented deltas (all in the module header):** a throwing scene normalizer is swallowed and the local math still runs (the old code would have thrown out of getCurrentScene); `snapValue` coerces a non-finite value to 0 instead of producing NaN positions; `getScenePointFromEvent` returns `{0,0}` with no stage element (the old code threw on null's getBoundingClientRect) and treats a zero/non-finite zoom as 1; `render3dView` keeps the old try/catch around the projector. `kindName3d` is currently unused in the editor (kept: the 3D kind display names belong with the stage).
- Test-authoring fixes (mine, not engine bugs): (1) my first load-order check used `indexOf('script.js')`, which matches an earlier comment mention — it now matches the `src="..."` tags; (2) the CHANGELOG edit initially clipped the start of the slice-3 entry — restored, and the slice-1/2/3 entries' honest limits corrected (the scene stage no longer lives in the editor file).

Nothing ever throws; hostile input degrades to safe defaults.

The module's `adjacentIndexes` uses reading order, matching HydraFlow, and the header comment says so.
