## Architecture comparison (v3 vs v4)

### v4 — the target architecture (modular, declarative)

Load order: `hydra-core.js` → `hydra-blocks.js` → `hydra-engine.js` → `hydra-templates.js` → `hydra-editor.js`.
Each file is an IIFE exporting one namespace object (`HydraCore`, `HydraBlocks`, `HydraEngine`, `HydraTemplates`, `HydraEditor`), zero tooling, one-directional dependencies.

- **hydra-core.js**: data model + normalization firewall (clone/clamp/uid/escapeHtml), 28-component registry, factories (`createProject/createScene/createObject`), normalizers (`normalizeProject/Scene/Object`) applied at every load/save boundary, grid→event-row conversion, condition/action type classifiers.
- **hydra-blocks.js**: the scripting language as *data*. 40 block defs in 11 categories, each declaring category, runtimeType, kind (condition/action/meta), label, color, defaults, and a `fields` schema (`text/textarea/select/color/expr/object` control types). Generic palette, search, property panel, event-sheet rendering. No per-block UI code.
- **hydra-engine.js**: headless-capable runtime. `start(project, {canvas})` with zero editor-DOM dependency: rAF loop, input, AABB+MTV collision, camera follow/shake, particles, screen flash, `new Function` expression evaluator, condition/action executors for grid rows, canvas renderer, WebAudio synthesized tones, and `buildPlayableHtml` (pure project→HTML function). Shared by preview and export.
- **hydra-templates.js**: 6 starter projects as pure builder functions on core factories, emitting real `gridData` logic (movement, coin collection, camera follow).
- **hydra-editor.js**: all DOM/UI. Project persistence (localStorage `hydraProjects`, 1.2s debounced autosave with quota fallback), scene editor (drag/zoom/snap, hierarchy+search, inspector with component picker), 60×60 logic grid editor (palette, block properties, grid + event-sheet views, global + per-scene scopes), preview tab (restart/stop), `exportGame` (downloads playable HTML), shortcuts, picker dialog.

Project model (v4): Project `{id, name, scenes[], currentSceneId, globalGridData, logicGrids, assets, activeScriptScope, eventsVersion, dateCreated, lastModified}`; Scene `{id, name, objects[], gridData, bgColor, bgAssetId, worldWidth/Height, viewportWidth/Height, layers}`; Object `{id, name, kind, posX/posY, width/height, rotation, color, text/*, physics, physicsEnabled, collision, trigger, hitbox*, variables, tags, effects, components[], children[], assetId}`; grid cells `{type, kind, blockType, _idx, ...params}` keyed by cell index. `normalizeObject` *derives* components from legacy flat fields, so old data keeps working.

### v4 — what is better than v3's monolith

1. Real separation of concerns: engine runs with `(project, canvas)` only; preview and export share one runtime.
2. Declarative block catalog: one data entry per block drives palette/property-panel/event-sheet.
3. Normalizer firewall: resilient to corrupt/hand-edited project JSON (fills defaults, coerces types, mints missing ids).
4. Data-first plain-JSON model: save/load, templates, import/export are the same serialization path.
5. Additive components derived from flat fields: enrichment without breaking old data.
6. Scope model + `gridToEventRows`: event-sheet view alongside the grid editor.
7. Templates as pure builder functions: headlessly testable.

### v4 — critical defects found (do NOT port these; fix them instead)

1. **Exported games cannot start.** `serializeCore()` does `JSON.stringify({clone: clone.toString(), ...})` — in the exported page `HydraCore.clone`/`clamp` are *strings*, so `clone(project)` in `start()` throws `TypeError: clone is not a function`.
2. **Scene-scoped logic never executes (preview AND export).** Core mints string uid scene ids (`scene_ab12cd`), but engine's `getScopeGrid`/`loadScene` compare `s.id === Number(sceneId)` → `NaN` → find always fails. Only `global` scope runs; `loadScene` always falls back to scene 0; `switchScene` lands on scene 0. Templates put logic in `scene.gridData`, so shipped template logic does nothing.
3. Engine duplicates its own simpler local `normalizeScene`/`normalizeObject` instead of reusing HydraCore's — a fork waiting to diverge.
4. `bindInput` leaks: `stop()` never calls `unbindInput`; every preview restart stacks duplicate handlers.
5. `triggerOnce` key collision: blocks without `_idx` share one key per scope; only the first fires.
6. `evalExpr` uses object names as `new Function` params; names with spaces/invalid chars throw (caught → 0), silently wrong.
7. **No diagnostics console at all**: rAF exceptions kill the game loop with zero UI feedback; no block validation; no warnings for dangling references.

### v4 — feature gaps vs a complete engine

- Import/export of project JSON exists only in `home.html` (the hub), not in the editor; editor has no per-project JSON download.
- Templates reachable only from `home.html`; editor "new project" only builds blank.
- Asset support basic: image upload for backgrounds/sprites; no audio file assets, no asset-manager panel; assets silently dropped on storage quota.
- Diagnostics console: absent (see bug 7).

### v3 — deep inspection findings (2026-11-04)

v3 (`v3-baseline/`) is the feature-complete build. File roles and exposed globals:

| File | Role | Exposes on `window` |
|---|---|---|
| `block-catalog.js` | Declarative catalogue: ~40 base blocks + generated presets (key/variable/movement/physics/visual/game-flow). Each: category, blockType (CSS), runtimeType, kind (condition/action/meta), label, color, defaults, fields schema, note | `HydraBlockCatalog.blocks` |
| `hydra-universal.js` | Shared foundation: component defs (Core/Web-UI/2D/3D-FPS), project normalization, grid→instruction/event-row conversion, diagnostics store + renderer | `HydraUniversal` (COMPONENTS, normalizeProject/Scene/Object, ensure/add/removeComponent, gridToInstructions, gridToEventRows, rebuildProjectEventSheets, formatInstruction, collectProjectDiagnostics, pushDiagnostic, clearRuntimeDiagnostics, renderDiagnostics) |
| `player-runtime.js` | Self-contained runtime used **only by exported games**. IIFE `installHydraPlayer(global)`; `buildPlayableHtml` serializes the whole IIFE via `.toString()` and inlines it into the export. Fully self-contained export: no CDN, no network; `<` escaped to `\u003c` | `HydraPlayer` (start, buildPlayableHtml) |
| `project.js` | Editor-side lifecycle: create/load/save (localStorage `hydraProjects`), project name, export trigger, return-to-home. Silent save + export on demand | `projectManager` (saveProject, getCurrentProject) |
| `script.js` (5384 lines) | Entire editor + **a second, independent in-editor preview runtime**: tabs, 60×60 logic grid, block property panels, DOM scene editor, object/component inspectors, expression evaluator, physics/collision, canvas preview loop, autosave (~900ms after `markProjectDirty`) | `scenes`, `cellData`, `assetLibrary`, `globalGridData`, `renderSceneObjects`, `processScriptGrid`, `renderEventSheet`, etc. |
| `starter-templates.js` | 17 starter templates (blank, webapp, platformer, top-down, collector, shooter, physics, full multi-scene, 3D/FPS variants). Loaded only by `home.html`, **not** by the editor | `HydraStarterTemplates` (templates, createProject) |
| `home.js` | Project gallery: list/sort/search/rename/delete, import/export `.hydra` JSON, new-project wizard with template picker, backup-all. Guards `HydraUniversal` uses with `?.` since home doesn't load it | none (DOMContentLoaded closure) |

Load order: `index.html`: block-catalog → hydra-universal → player-runtime → project → script (`?v=20260517-rebuild1`); `home.html`: starter-templates → home (`?v=20260531-motionlab1`); home.js navigates with `?v=20260811-fixed1` (version-string drift, cosmetic).

**Data model:** plain JSON in `localStorage['hydraProjects']`. Project `{id, name, dateCreated, lastModified, eventsVersion:1, globalGridData, logicGrids, eventSheets, scenes[], currentSceneId, nextSceneId, activeScriptScope, sceneCount, assets, playableRuntime?, fpsConfig?}`. Scene `{id, name, objects[], entities (same ref as objects!), bgColor, bgAssetId, width/height, worldWidth/Height, viewportWidth/Height, gridData, logicGrids:{main: alias of gridData}, layers[]}`. Object: legacy flat fields (`posX/posY/width/height/color/assetId/static/physicsEnabled/physics{}/layer/text*/hitbox*/tags/variables/effects/children/collision{}`) **plus** newer `components[]` (Transform, SpriteRenderer, Collider2D, Rigidbody2D…) kept in sync by `syncComponentToLegacyFields` (script.js ~5240). **The simulation reads the legacy flat fields, not components** — components are editor-side metadata today.

**Scripting semantics:** 60×60 grid (3600 DOM cells), cells `{type, ...params}` keyed by `row*60+col`. Scopes: one grid per scene (`scene.gridData`) + global (`globalGridData`). Condition fires → `getEventActionIndexes()`: same `eventId` group first, else orthogonally-adjacent actions, else rightward same-row actions until a non-action/non-comment cell. `hydra-universal.js` `gridToEventRows` implements the same grouping for the event-sheet view and diagnostics.

**Critical structural fact:** two independently implemented runtimes for the same block language — editor preview (`script.js` gameLoop/executeAction) and export (`player-runtime.js` loop/execute). They have already diverged (see bugs).

**Export mechanism:** `project.js` exportPlayableGame → silent save → deep clone → `HydraPlayer.buildPlayableHtml(project)` → Blob download. Exports embed the whole player IIFE via `.toString()` + full project JSON. Boot wraps in try/catch; sets `document.documentElement.dataset.hydraPlayer="ready"|"error"` with a red overlay on failure (useful for automated smoke tests). `playableRuntime==='retroFps'` emits a hardcoded raycaster FPS that ignores scene contents. `saveVariable`/`loadVariable` share `localStorage` `hydraSave:` keys across all exports on one origin.

## Test results

- 2026-11-04: `node --check` on all 14 JS files in both builds: PASS.
- 2026-11-04: `node tests/run.js`: ALL PASS — 8 syntax checks + 2 test files:
  - `tests/expr.test.js` (32 assertions): shared evaluator handles numbers, vars, builtins, `object.prop`, missing refs, unknowns, and `Math.*` (Math.floor/Math.PI/Math.random/Math.max).
  - `tests/export-boot.test.js` (17 assertions): real `buildPlayableHtml` output is self-contained (expr module inlined, no external deps); the verbatim generated scripts boot in a stubbed DOM and report `dataset.hydraPlayer="ready"`; a pumped 3-frame game loop fired `gameStart → setVariable(Math.floor(25.7))` giving `score === 25` with no expression warnings.

## Known bugs

### v3 bugs (from 2026-11-04 deep inspection)

**High severity**
1. ~~`Math.*` expressions broken in BOTH runtimes~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** Extracted the single canonical evaluator into `hydra/hydra-expr.js` (pure, no DOM). `resolvePropAccess` now passes `Math.*` through to the real `Math` object instead of rewriting it to `0(...)`. Editor preview (`script.js` `evalExprInfo`) and exported runtime (`player-runtime.js` `exprInfo`) both delegate to it; `buildPlayableHtml` inlines `factorySource` into exported games so exports stay self-contained. The old duplicated `exprInfo`/`unknownIdentifiers` in player-runtime.js and `getUnknownExpressionIdentifiers` in script.js were removed. Verified by `tests/expr.test.js` + `tests/export-boot.test.js` (Math.floor(25.7) → 25 in a real exported game loop).
2. ~~Event-sheet / logic-views UI missing from `index.html`~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** The view code in script.js was complete; only the DOM was missing. Added the Grid/Event Sheet toggle (`logic-view-grid-btn`, `logic-view-events-btn`), the `event-sheet-panel` with toolbar + `add-event-row-btn` + `event-sheet-list`, and toggle styles (event-sheet CSS already existed). Verified in real Chromium via `tests/browser-smoke.js`: event sheet renders the collector template's logic rows, Add Event adds a row, switching back to Grid shows the new blocks.
   Also fixed a resilience bug the smoke test caught: home.html's inline `tailwind.config = {...}` threw a page error when the Tailwind CDN was unreachable — now guarded with `if (window.tailwind)` so the hub works offline.
3. ~~Middle-mouse conditions~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** Editor `evaluateMouseCondition` never fired for `button:'middle'`; export `evalMouse` mapped middle→left (0). Extracted the canonical name→code mapping into `hydra/hydra-input.js` (`mouseButtonCode`: left→0, middle→1, right→2), used by editor (`evaluateMouseCondition`, `evaluateObjectClickedCondition`) and export (`evalMouse`, `evalPointObject`); `buildPlayableHtml` inlines it into exports. The mouse-condition property panel now offers Middle Click. Verified by `tests/input.test.js` + an end-to-end middle-mousedown dispatch in `tests/export-boot.test.js`.
4. ~~Two divergent runtimes (editor preview vs export)~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.**
   - `overlapTouch`/`collision`/`onFloor` unified: the export runtime now mirrors the editor preview exactly — `collision` honors `countTouchingEdges` (default true → 3px touching tolerance), `overlapTouch` honors `data.tolerance` (default 4), `onFloor` honors `countTouchingEdges` (false → 0, else 4px) and requires a bottom-side contact via a shared `aabbContact` port of the editor's `getAABBContact`.
   - Key matching unified: the canonical `normalizeKeyName`/`keyNameAliases`/`keySetHas` moved into `hydra/hydra-input.js`; both runtimes use it (exports previously matched only raw `e.key`/`e.code`).
   - Unknown block types no longer fail silently: both runtimes report them once (editor → diagnostics panel via `warnUnknownBlockType`; export → `state.diagnostics` + throttled `console.warn` + ⚠ badge on the exported HUD). Switch `default` cases kept as defense-in-depth; the grid-level filters are where the reporting happens.
   - `lastExpressionWarning` is now surfaced in exports through the same diagnostic channel instead of being set-and-ignored.
   Verified by extended `tests/export-boot.test.js` (collision/onFloor/overlapTouch fire identically; unknown block + bad expression produce diagnostics, console warnings, and a HUD badge) and `tests/input.test.js` (key helpers).
5. ~~`switchScene` mid-frame reentrancy~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** Both runtimes swapped scenes immediately inside the action list, so remaining old-scene actions executed against the new scene's objects. Scene changes (`switchScene`, `restartScene`, `nextScene`, `previousScene`) are now deferred to end of frame: the editor sets `previewState.pendingSceneId` (applied in `gameLoop` after `processScriptGrid`, before `drawGame`); the export sets `state.pendingSceneId` (applied in `loop()` after `processScope`/`pendingDestroy`, before `draw`). Last request wins; same-scene requests are no-ops. Verified by a new Phase D in `tests/export-boot.test.js`: a `switchScene` followed by a `setVariable(hero.posX)` in the same frame reads the old scene's object (50, not 0).

**Medium severity**
6. ~~Dead code in script.js~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** Removed 474 lines: all 20 superseded `process*Conditions` functions (each verified single-reference via grep before deletion), the never-read `gameStartFired` flag, the never-read `collisionStates`/`collisionTriggerTimes` Maps (+ their `.clear()` calls), and a `getElementById('cell-highlight')` reference to a never-created element. Kept the 6 helpers in that region that live code uses (`getMousePointForObject`, `pointInsideObject`, `aabbOverlapsTolerance`, `isActionRuntimeType`, `isCommentRuntimeType`, `getEventActionIndexes`) and the live `processGameStartConditions`. Full suite (incl. real-browser smoke) passes after the deletion.
7. Dead state: `collisionStates`/`collisionTriggerTimes` Maps never read/written; `gameStartFired` set-never-read; `getElementById('cell-highlight')` references a never-created element.
8. ~~Destroyed objects remain hittable for rest of frame~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** `pendingDestroy` now counts as destroyed immediately in both runtimes' central lookups: the editor's `getAlivePreviewObjects()` and the export's `named()` both exclude pending-destroy ids, so a destroyed object can't collide, be clicked, or be targeted later in the same frame. Verified by Phase E in `tests/export-boot.test.js` (destroy then same-frame collision check does not fire).
9. ~~`spawnObject` fallback dead~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** `evalExpr(...) ?? src.posX` could never fire (evalExpr never returns nullish), so blank coordinates spawned at 0,0. Both runtimes now treat a blank spawnX/spawnY as "spawn at the source's position" (`isBlankSpawnCoord`); explicit values (including 0) still evaluate normally. Verified by Phase F in `tests/export-boot.test.js`.
10. `home.html` doesn't load `hydra-universal.js`: imported projects get no normalization on the home screen (editor normalizes on open).
11. ~~Data duplication at save~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** `entities` aliased `objects`, `logicGrids.main/global` aliased gridData, and derived `eventSheets` embedded full grid copies — scenes+scripts stored ~4×. New `HydraUniversal.serializeProject()` strips aliases + derived sheets at every save point (editor autosave, hub gallery, `.hydra` exports); `normalizeProject` restores them on load. Measured 81% smaller saves. `hydra-universal.js` now loads in `home.html`, so hub imports normalize on load. Verified by `tests/serialize.test.js`.
12. ~~Template object IDs collide across scenes~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** Templates numbered each scene's objects from 1. `createProject` now renumbers all objects to project-unique ids (blocks reference objects by name, so this is safe). Verified by a per-project uniqueness check in `tests/export-boot.test.js`.
13. ~~`retroFpsScene` export ignored scene contents~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** The FPS export was a fully hardcoded raycaster. `buildRetroFpsHtml` now builds a level from the project's scene via `fpsLevelFromScene()`: the `FirstPersonController` object (or `PreviewPlayer`) sets the player spawn, `Drone` objects (or `TriggerZone` components) become enemies, and `ReactorCore`/`AmmoBox`/`Medkit` become pickups; the win threshold adapts to the number of cores placed. Empty scenes fall back to the built-in layout unchanged. The template's hint text now documents the name mapping. Verified by new `tests/fps-export.test.js` (9 assertions).
14. No `default` case in executeAction/execute/evaluateCondition/evaluate: unknown block types fail silently.
15. `keySetHas`/`hasKey` asymmetry: editor uses key aliases, export uses raw `e.key`/`e.code` + Space special-case.

**Low severity / risky patterns**
16. ~~`new Function(...)` eval of expressions = arbitrary code execution from shared `.hydra` files. Document the trust boundary.~~ — **DOCUMENTED 2026-11-04.** `SECURITY.md` now states the trust boundary: expression text in project files is compiled with `new Function` at runtime (editor preview + exported games), lists what project data reaches the evaluator, the mitigations in place (unknown-identifier gate, `object.prop` rewriting, string stripping), and user/developer guidance. Linked from `README.md`.
17. `compare` uses loose `==`/`!=` (likely intentional).
18. ~~`beforeunload` synchronous full serialize~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** `saveProject` now early-returns when the dirty flag is clear (`markProjectDirty` is the single setter, called on every mutation path), so closing a clean project does no work at all; a dirty close does one cheap save.
19. ~~Autosave deep-clone jank~~ — **FIXED 2026-11-04.** `saveProject` no longer deep-clones assets, scenes, and grid data separately (3–4 full project clones per save). It assigns the live stores by reference and lets `serializeProject()` perform the single deep clone needed for the localStorage snapshot. Verified by new `tests/save.test.js` (10 assertions: skip-if-clean, single write, stripped form, reference-not-clone, snapshot isolation, flag cleared).
20. 3D/FPS components (MeshRenderer, Camera3D, FirstPersonController, RaycastWeapon, Fetch, Route, DataStore, FormInput, Button) are definitions with editor UI but no runtime behavior; `Rigidbody3D` is a placeholder.
21. Exported game input: `bindInput` never detaches, `resize` listener per `start()` call (benign, single-use).
22. `image()`/`getImage()` caches never invalidate on asset dataUrl replacement.

### v4 bugs (unported; must not be copied)

As listed in the architecture comparison above (export serialization strings, scene-id Number() mismatch, duplicated normalizers, input leak, triggerOnce key collision, fragile evalExpr scoping, no diagnostics).

## Decisions made

- 2026-11-04: v3 is the baseline; v4 is the architectural target. Do not replace working v3 code with reduced-functionality v4 code.
- 2026-11-04: v3 keeps **numeric scene ids** (nextSceneId counter). The v4 string-uid convention is NOT adopted for v3 data; any future migration to string uids must fix every `Number(sceneId)` comparison in the player runtime first (that's the exact bug that broke v4's scene logic).
- 2026-11-04: expression evaluation has ONE canonical source: `hydra/hydra-expr.js` (pure, no DOM). The editor and the exported player both delegate to it; exports inline `HydraExpr.factorySource` so they stay self-contained. Never duplicate resolver logic again — extend the module.
- 2026-11-04: input mappings have ONE canonical source: `hydra/hydra-input.js` (pure, no DOM), same pattern as hydra-expr.js. Exports inline `HydraInput.factorySource`. Extend the module rather than duplicating mappings.
- 2026-11-04: Switch-scene semantics become id-based, not name-based, during migration.
- 2026-11-04: Schema versions via `eventsVersion` (bump on model changes).
- 2026-11-04: Repo layout: `repo/v3-baseline/` (pristine copy), `repo/v4-reference/` (pristine copy), working tree `repo/hydra/` created next.

## 2026-11-04 — Repaired broken committed tree (asset foundation reconciliation)

- Commit 208ff1c deleted `hydra/hydra-assets.js` (created by 459809d) but left `tests/assets.test.js` expecting helpers that never landed in `hydra-universal.js` (`collectAssetUsage`, `removeAssetReferences`), plus a mismatched API (`collectAssetUsages` used a `{label,detail}` entry shape vs the test's `{where,objectName,sceneId,sceneName}` shape; default asset name `'asset'` vs the test's `'Untitled asset'`). The committed tree failed the suite on assets.test.js.
- Reconciled by adopting the test's API as canonical in `hydra-universal.js`: `collectAssetUsage` (singular) with the entry shape the test asserts (sprite/background/component usages, dangling refs still reported), `collectAssetUsages` kept as a thin alias, new `removeAssetReferences(project, assetId)` (clears sprite + background + component refs, returns count cleared), default name `'Untitled asset'`. Correction to the earlier entry: the asset foundation lives in `hydra-universal.js`, not a standalone module.
- Full node suite green after the repair; the browser smoke test runs at the end of the full suite per the normal cycle.

## 2026-11-04 — Continuous development goes always-on (replaces 8h cron)

- Ky'lin asked for HydraScript to be worked on constantly, not on a schedule. New mechanism: saved workflow `hydrascript-continuous-dev`, launched as an async run — an endless loop where each cycle picks the next roadmap item (P4 pillars first, then P2/P3; researches new capabilities when the list is exhausted), implements it, runs the FULL suite (`node tests/run.js`), updates PROGRESS/ROADMAP/CHANGELOG, and commits. One bounded repair attempt per failed cycle; unfixable failures are reverted and recorded as blockers, then the loop moves on. A `needs_user` result pauses the loop and surfaces the question to Ky'lin.
- The old cron `hydrascript-continuous-improvement` is now a watchdog (every 2h, retitled "HydraScript dev-loop watchdog"): it only checks the workflow run is alive and relaunches it if the run ever dies. It does no engine work itself.
- Collision guard: each cycle checks `git status --porcelain` first; if the tree is dirty (someone mid-work), the cycle only runs the test suite and defers.
- Stop mechanism: Ky'lin says stop → `workflow.stop` the run and disable/remove the watchdog cron.

## 2026-11-04 — Recovered interrupted asset work; hardened the loop's collision guard

- The first continuous-dev run was stopped while workers were mid-cycle. Recovery: one worker had added 3 lines to `hydra/hydra-universal.js` calling an undefined `normalizeAsset` — reverted as incomplete/broken. The same worker had also written `hydra/hydra-assets.js` (162 lines): a complete, pattern-following shared asset module (classify/normalize, `window.HydraAssets`, `factorySource` for export inlining, same shape as hydra-expr.js/hydra-input.js). Kept as the unwired foundation for P4 item 20 (asset manager); wiring (index.html load order, player-runtime inlining, editor upload UI) is the next step.
- Lesson applied to the workflow: the old "defer when dirty" guard could stall the loop forever on leftovers from killed workers. New rule: each cycle adopts coherent leftovers (tests must pass) or reverts broken ones, records the decision in PROGRESS.md, and always continues to a new item. The tree is never left dirty.
- Design principle added (Ky'lin's direction): as powerful as the real engines, but simple enough that anyone can build a complex game — every feature ships with visual blocks, sensible defaults, and guided editor UI, never code-only.

- Tests: new `tests/sound.test.js` (17 assertions on the pure module); `tests/export-boot.test.js` Phase G (9 assertions: asset bundled, played from its dataUrl at the block's volume in a real exported loop, legacy tone untouched, dangling assetId falls back silently). Full suite green including the real-browser smoke test.
- Concurrency note: this cycle ran alongside other live workers on the same tree (a docs worker that finished P1 item 12 / SECURITY.md, and an asset-manager-panel worker adding an inspector Assets tab). This cycle committed ONLY its own files/hunks and left the others' uncommitted work untouched. ## 2026-11-04 — Event sheet removed; template logic fixed; branding upgraded (Ky'lin's direction)

- **Event sheet removed entirely.** Ky'lin: it's too close to GDevelop/everyone else and takes away from HydraScript's uniqueness. Removed: the Grid/Event Sheet toggle + `event-sheet-panel` DOM (index.html), `setLogicView`/`renderEventSheet`/`renderEventCell`/`addEventRow`/`addBlockNearEvent` + `activeLogicView` state (138 lines, script.js), all event-sheet CSS (~170 lines, style.css), and the smoke-test's event-sheet steps (rewritten to test the Add Condition picker placing a block on the grid instead). Kept `openLogicBlockPicker` (the Add Condition/Add Action toolbar buttons use it) and `gridToEventRows`/`rebuildProjectEventSheets` in hydra-universal.js (diagnostics' internal representation, not UI). The logic grid is now the one and only scripting view; uniqueness added as design principle #4 in ROADMAP.
- **Collector + shooter templates rewritten.** Root cause of the "doesn't look intentional" logic: only conditions are evaluated each frame — lone actions NEVER execute. So the collector's `setText` (score display), `wrapScreen`, and `randomVariable` blocks were silently dead, and coin respawn worked only by orthogonal-adjacency accident. Same disease in the shooter (dead `setText`, dead enemy movement, adjacency-accident respawn). Both templates rewritten so every action sits in an explicit condition-led row; coin/enemy respawns use inline `Math.random()` expressions (the pattern the shooter already used). Added ROADMAP P3 item: audit remaining templates for lone actions.
- **Text hitbox fix.** The `text()` template factory defaulted `hitboxWidth/hitboxHeight` to 180x34 regardless of the width/height override, so selection boxes rendered too big (Score labels) or too small (hint text) vs the element. Now derives hitbox from the resolved width/height unless explicitly overridden (matches the sprite factory's pattern).
- **Branding.** Header wordmark is now bold all-caps "HYDRA SCRIPT" with letter-spacing, on a gradient (violet→blue) icon badge; same treatment on the loading screen and home hub. Cache-busters bumped to `20261104-brand1`.
- **Loop incident.** The continuous-dev workflow malfunctioned: ~10 cycle agents ran concurrently on the same tree (deferral-loop commits, 65M input tokens burned). Stopped the run, closed all stray agents, stashed the interrupted item-21 animation WIP (in `git stash`; resume after). The workflow script needs a mutual-exclusion guard before relaunch — file-lock in the cycle prompt.
- Ky'lin approved for the roadmap: plain-language block tooltips (catalog `note` fields exist — surface them), guided 5-minute tutorial, CDN-resilient home page styles, preview FPS counter + device frames.

## 2026-11-04 12:08 EST — loop script bug fixed (missing `await`), loop relaunched, watchdog tightened
- Root cause found for both the token burn AND the silent stop: the workflow script called `agent(...)` without `await` in its for-loop. The loop fired all 5000 cycle calls at once, the top-level returned immediately ("completed 0 successful improvement cycles"), and the runtime ran the spawned agents concurrently (677 in the last run, ~88M input tokens, nearly all deferring on the lock). When the platform daemon restarted mid-run, the already-returned run finalized as "completed" instead of continuing.
- Fix: added `await` to both `agent()` calls in the saved `hydrascript-continuous-dev` script. Cycles now run strictly one at a time; the lock file remains as a backstop.
- Relaunched the loop (run d527b0ab, queued 12:07 EST); first cycle acquired `.hydra-cycle.lock` at 12:07:13, working.
- Watchdog cron `hydrascript-continuous-improvement` tightened from every 2h to every 20m, so a dead loop is relaunched within ~20 minutes without anyone having to notice.
- Lesson: in workflow scripts, an un-awaited `agent()` call does NOT run sequentially — it fires concurrently. Always `await` when cycles must be serial.

## 2026-11-04 12:40 EST — watchdog flaw found and fixed; deferral status fixed
- The 12:27 watchdog check exposed a real flaw: `workflow.list_runs` is scoped to the watchdog's own session, so it could not see the loop running in the chat session. It concluded "no run exists" and launched a duplicate loop run. The duplicate's first cycles hit the live `.hydra-cycle.lock`, deferred, and the watchdog worker terminated the duplicate. No harm done, but the flaw would have repeated every 20 minutes.
- Fix 1: watchdog body rewritten to a lock-file-first protocol — check `.hydra-cycle.lock` age + live cycle agents via subagent.list (both cross-session signals); only relaunch when no live agent exists and the lock is missing/stale (15+ min). Never relaunch on list_runs alone.
- Fix 2: cycle/repair prompts now return status `ok` (tests_passed false) instead of the custom `deferred` status when the lock is held — the runtime only accepts ok/error/blocked, so a deferral now skips cleanly instead of failing the agent call.
- Verified: single loop running (run d527b0ab), cycle-2 (F2 UI widgets) active, no duplicate runs or stray agents.

## 2026-11-04 14:20 EST — hotfix (main agent): button labels vertically centered
Ky'lin flagged that button labels in Hydra Quest screenshots were jammed against the buttons' top edge. Root cause: both canvas renderers drew button labels with `textBaseline='top'` at the button's top-left, so the label started exactly at the button's top edge instead of being vertically centered (the DOM scene-editor view already centered via flexbox). Fix: in `drawPreviewText` (hydra/script.js) and `drawText` (hydra/player-runtime.js), buttons now draw with `textBaseline='middle'` at `sp.y + height/2`; plain text objects keep top-alignment. Verified with `node --check` on both files and a fresh Playwright screenshot of the flagship menu (labels centered, zero page errors). Uncommitted in the tree for the active cycle to pick up; the next full-suite run should cover it.

## 2026-11-04 14:28 EST — priority directive (Ky'lin): multi-platform export next
Ky'lin directed that HydraScript must export easily to many platforms (APK, HTML/WebGL, EXE, iOS, beyond). Encoded as ROADMAP P4 items E1–E4 (web targets incl. PWA, desktop wrapper via Electron, mobile wrapper via Capacitor, export hub UX). Priority: work E1–E4 next after the in-flight item 32 (physics upgrade), ahead of 34/35. Honest limits baked into the items: standalone HTML already covers "web" (no separate WebGL target needed for a 2D canvas engine); iOS IPA generation requires macOS + Xcode, the engine generates the full project but Apple builds the binary.

## 2026-11-04 14:35 EST — design doctrine + roadmap expansion (Ky'lin)
Ky'lin's design doctrine: simple on-ramps, never cages. Presets/templates/components are the easy way, never the only way — every preset must be decomposable into ordinary blocks the user could place by hand, and there must always be a manual path from primitives; rival Unreal/Unity/GDevelop on power and customizability, win on simplicity. Encoded in the workflow's CORE DESIGN PRINCIPLE and the ROADMAP P4 header. New roadmap: 37 block-catalog expansion workstream (GDevelop parity, batched), 3D pillar 3D1–3D5 (Three.js foundation, GLB assets, composable FPS/TPS character controllers as block recipes — no black boxes, 3D physics basics, lighting). Priority chain: 32 (in flight) -> E1-E4 export -> 37 blocks -> 3D1-3D5 -> 34 -> 35 -> P2 modularization last.

## 2026-11-04 15:20 EST — flagship tuning hotfix (main agent, Ky'lin playtest)
Ky'lin playtested Hydra Quest and reported 4 issues, all confirmed real. Root causes: (1) menu scene contained a physics-enabled hero with no floor, falling forever in the background — removed it; (2) Space "moving right" was preserved horizontal momentum at excessive speed, not a binding bug — jumping keeps velX by design; (3) run speed 600px/s (force 1.2/frame vs friction 0.88) — lowered to 0.5 (205px/s); (4) jump apex only ~67px because engine friction damps velY, but platforms need up to 110px rise — raised jump impulse -15 to -30 and maxSpeed 18 to 32 (apex now 137px, measured in the real engine loop). Verified: node --check, full flagship.test.js green (incl. scripted playthrough, zero diagnostics), empirical physics sim (apex/drift/landing/speed), fresh standalone export boots hydraPlayer=ready with zero page errors, screenshots inspected. Committed as bfa3a57 (template + test only; cycle's E4 tree untouched).

## 2026-11-04 15:35 EST — visual QA fixes + block concept board (main agent, Ky'lin)
Ky'lin flagged the empty-logic hint bar rendering one-word-per-line and the dotted grid background. Root cause of the hint bug: the generic `button { width: 100% }` rule was never overridden on `.grid-empty-hint-btn`, so the CTA took ~526px and crushed the copy to 0 width (found via computed-style debugging). Fixed with width:max-content on the bar + width:auto on the CTA; verified with a real screenshot. Logic grid background changed from dots to 56px grid lines aligned to cells. Committed as 1dfa107.
Also built the requested puzzle-piece block concept board (concepts/block-concepts-v1.html): three directions — A Notch & Tab (subtle), B Bold Jigsaw (true punched holes), C Hex Rail (technical) — each with a connected pair and a 4-socket/4-tab capacity demo. Awaiting Ky'lin's pick before implementing.
